The wheel is drawn as a Counter-Strike case opening (`src/ui/components/CaseReel.tsx`, used by the Draft and the head-to-head screen). It only presents a draw the engine has already made: `spin` picks the season first (uniformly over the seasons in the pool) and then a club inside it, and the reel shows them in that order. Nothing on it feeds the game. The filler cards and the stop jitter use `Math.random`; `dealReel` (`src/game/logic/reel.ts`, pure) takes the random source as an argument, so the tests pass a seeded one.

- **Stage 1, the season (1.0 s).** A drum rolls for 850 ms through the seasons the wheel could land on this spin (the seasons in `wheelPool` with the twist applied, counting up to the drawn one) and stops on the drawn season, written `2017-18`. The label beside it reads "Season", or the twist's name ("Golden spin"). The strip is veiled while the drum rolls, and only real seasons from the pool ever pass through the drum; once it stops, the drawn season is the only one shown.
- **Stage 2, the club strip (5.8 s, from 1.0 s to 6.8 s).** 70 cards, every club-season of the drawn season in the draft's leagues (`reelLeagues`: the rules' leagues, as `wheelPool` filters them, or every loaded league when the rules name none; head-to-head reads the same rules): the whole league that year, or with several leagues all of them, each card tagged ENG, ESP and so on. They are dealt in shuffled rounds, outwards from the landing card, so a one-league strip shows every club of that season and no card appears twice in a row. The drawn club-season is card 60. Cards the wheel could stop on are lit: `wheelPool` filtered to that season (`draftReelPool`), or in head-to-head the pool `h2hSpin` draws from (`h2hReelPool`: the club-seasons useful to both managers still drafting, else the first picker's wheel). The rest are greyed out, so a golden spin runs through the whole league with only the champion lit. The strip slides on one curve, `reelPosition` in `src/ui/reelCurve.ts`, sampled into the animation's keyframes (linear between them, one every 16 ms or so): a deceleration on `cubic-bezier(0.05, 0.7, 0.65, 1)` (`STRIP_CURVE`) for 5.6 s that runs past the stop point by `OVERSHOOT` (12% of a card) and comes to rest there, then a half-cosine ease back onto the stop point over the last 200 ms, so position and speed are continuous and there is no jitter. It stops anywhere up to 0.45 of a card either side of the card's centre (`REEL_JITTER`, `Math.random`, the near miss), so the marker can sit near an edge; the club is the engine's, only the offset is cosmetic.
- **Measured** (unit test over 68 and 84 px cards, windows 258 to 720 px and the full jitter range; production build in Chromium). Ten to fifteen cards pass in the first quarter second and half the distance in the first second; the last 1.5 s creeps over 1.8 to 3 cards and the overshoot's return. The ticks (section on sound in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`) go from 6 to 15 ms apart at the start (capped at 15 ms when played) to about 400 ms at the end, four in the last 1.5 s, the last in the ease back. After the strip comes to rest the reveal waits 400 ms (`FULL_TIMING.hold`): the reveal and the pick come about 7.2 s after the tap (`SETTLE_MS`), measured 7.6 to 8.4 s on a loaded machine.
- **Stage 3, the landing (0.5 s).** After the 400 ms hold at rest the card pops with a ring and glow in its tier colour, the reel window's edge flashes the same colour, and a gold card gets a sweep of light. The line under the reel shows the club, the season and the tier chip ("Gold · Elite"). The Draft opens the pick at the landing, not on a timer.
- **Speed.** Re-spins always run `QUICK_TIMING` (a 450 ms drum, the strip from 0.5 s, a 2 s slide with a 150 ms ease back, a 150 ms hold: about 2.7 s to the reveal), detected as a run that replaces another drawn club-season with no idle reel between; so does every run when the viewer turns on Reel speed: Quick in Profile (`touchline-quick-spin`, `src/ui/viewerPrefs.ts`). Auto-complete runs no reel at all. A skip still lands at once, with no hold.
- **Skip.** A tap or click on the reel, the "Tap to skip" button, or Space or Enter while the reel runs jumps both stages to the landing. Space and Enter are left alone when they are meant for a field or another enabled button.
- **Reduced motion** (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`). No slide and no roll: the season shows at once, and the landed card with its glow 0.5 s later.
- **Resizing.** The stop is measured from the layout, so a `ResizeObserver` on the reel window and the landing card measures it again whenever either changes size (a phone turned, a window dragged, the cards growing from 68 px to 84 px at the 420 px container breakpoint): mid-slide the slide retargets with `setKeyframes`, and after the landing the strip moves so the marker stays on the drawn club. With reduced motion the strip stays where it is until the landing, so a resize never shows the drawn card early. Before this, resizing the window from 1280 to 1000 px wide after a landing left the marker ten cards away, on a club that was not drafted.
- **Screen readers.** One polite live region says the season once when the drum stops ("Season 2017-18, Golden spin") and the club and tier once at the landing ("Manchester City 2017-18: Gold, Elite"), never the passing cards. The strip itself is `aria-hidden`.
- **One run per spin.** Each spin mounts its own run, keyed by the spin number and the club-season, so nothing of the previous spin (its landed card, its season, its squad) can show at the start of the next, and a re-spin onto the same club-season still runs (at the quick timing). The run is measured and started from zero in a layout effect before the first paint, and moves with the Web Animations API (`element.animate`). The pick opens at the reveal, the hold after the slide's `finished` promise resolves, exactly once per run; unmounting or a new spin cancels the animations and the reduced-motion timer. The Draft and head-to-head screens derive "spinning" from whether the reel has settled on the current `draft.current` object, so a new spin reads as spinning in the very render the store hands it over, and Re-spin is disabled before a second tap can land. Before this a double tap on Re-spin burned both re-spins, the first spin after the Draft mounted was the only one that slid in full (the reset and the slide were folded into one React render), and the hub showed a random year from 1990 to 2026 while the reel ran.

#### Rarity tiers

`src/game/logic/rarity.ts`. A club-season's strength is the mean overall rating of its eleven best players (season ratings; `clubSeasonStrength(cs, rating?)` takes another accessor, for a future prime-ratings mode). `tierTable(u, leagues)` ranks every club-season of the draft's leagues across all seasons by strength, ties broken by the club-season key, and cuts the ranking into fixed shares. It is memoised per universe, league set and rating accessor, and it never sees the draft, so a club keeps its colour from the first spin to the last. A one-league draft is ranked within its league; a multi-league draft across all of its leagues.

| Tier | Share | Colour | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gold | top 5% | `#e4ae39` | Elite |
| Red | next 10% | `#eb4b4b` | Great |
| Pink | next 20% | `#d32ce6` | Good |
| Purple | next 30% | `#8847ff` | Decent |
| Blue | last 35% | `#4b69ff` | Underdog |

The colours are `--tier-gold` to `--tier-blue` on `:root`, in the reel section of `styles.css`. Text on a tier colour uses `--tier-<tier>-ink`: black, or white on purple, each at 4.5:1 or better (gold 10.4, red 5.6, pink 5.3, purple 4.8, blue 4.8), the same in both themes because the chip carries its own background. Every reel card has a rarity bar along its bottom edge and a faint glow in its colour. Once the reel has landed, the Draft and head-to-head squad headers show the tier chip after the record line, and each row of the draft log carries a dot in its tier colour. With the 2026-10-25 England data, 17 of the 18 finished title winners are gold or red (Leicester 2015-16 is purple), and Manchester City 2017-18 is gold.

### Spin odds

`src/engine/draft.ts` (`spinOdds`, `spinWeight`), `src/engine/h2h.ts` (`h2hSpinOdds`, `h2hWeight`), `src/game/logic/tierOdds.ts` (`tierOdds`, `h2hTierOdds`), `src/ui/components/SpinOdds.tsx`. Before every spin in every mode (Quick Draft, the Daily, Season and Dynasty drafts, head-to-head), and before a re-spin while a spin is showing and re-spins are left, a panel under the reel (`data-testid="spin-odds"`) gives the chance of landing in each rarity tier: a bar of tier-coloured segments and the percentages in the tier colours, each to one decimal and apportioned by largest remainder (`shownOdds` in `src/game/logic/oddsDisplay.ts`) so the printed figures add up to exactly 100.0%, a chance that can come up never printed as 0 (it takes 0.1% from the largest); the panel's note reads "exact odds" only when the printed figures are the exact chances, "odds (rounded)" otherwise (until 2026-10-26 it said "exact odds" over whole-number figures that summed to 99.3% to 101%). The manager spin's and the January case's panels print the same way; the end case's table is whole per cent and exact. It is computed from the same steps `spin` takes, in the same order, on the real wheel pool: the Icons roll (Gamble only), the twist (a Crazy golden surprise, or each twist whose wheel is not empty, equally likely, on a twist spin), a season uniformly among the twisted pool's seasons, then each club-season of that season by `spinWeight` (the weight `spin` itself now calls, unchanged). Under Position First before a slot is chosen the panel reads "Next spin, any open slot". The tiers are the reel's (`tierTable` over the draft's leagues). `gamble.test.ts` checks that the chances sum to 100% in every state of many drafts (plain, twisted, Crazy, Gamble, Underdog, Position First) and that 2,500 seeded spins from one state land in each tier within four standard errors of the panel, on a plain spin and a twist spin, and the same for head-to-head.

### Quick Draft modes

`src/engine/modes.ts` (cards, toggling, words, `crazyFormation`), `src/engine/draft.ts` (the rules), `src/ui/components/SetupBits.tsx` (`ModeCards`). Setup's **Modes** panel (Quick Draft only, never the Daily or a challenge, which carry their own rules) is a row of icon cards that switch on and off, several at once: Crazy 🌀, Hard 💀, Underdog 🐶, Legends 👑, Speedrun ⏱️, Gamble 🎲 and Upgrader ⬆️. Five are flags in `DraftRules.modes` (`QuickMode[]`, optional, left out when none is on, read through `modesOf`/`hasMode`); Upgrader is its own rule (`DraftRules.upgrader`, with a `RULE_CHECKS` entry); Hard is the existing difficulty, so tapping the Hard card sets the Difficulty cards to Hard and tapping it off (or choosing Easy or Normal) clears it (`toggleModeCard`, `withModes`, `setupRules` with `choices.modes`). Underdog and Legends exclude each other (switching one on switches the other off). The draft header shows the cards as chips (`data-testid="draft-modes"`), the summary line under the tier banner and the share text carry `modesLine` ("🌀 Crazy · 💀 Hard · ⏱️ Speedrun", Hard then dropped from the tag line), the setup line says "crazy mode" and so on, and the fixed-rules pills of a challenge name each flag. `modes` is in `RULE_CHECKS` (known flags, each once), so a challenge link carries it in its rules diff and replays it.

- **Crazy.** `nextSpinTwisted` is true on every spin (`withModes` also turns `twists` on, so the twist card shows), so every spin draws a twist from the game's stream as a twist spin always has. The formation is drawn at Start by `startFormation` (`logic/setup.ts`), which calls `crazyFormation(seed)` from a stream seeded apart from the game's (`${seed}:crazy-formation`) and then travels in the settings and a link's `f`, so a replay never draws it again; Setup shows "Crazy: seed X draws a 3-5-2" in place of the formation grid. After every pick that leaves the draft open, one draw from `DraftState.crazyRng` (seeded by `newDraft` from `${seed}:crazy-surprises`, never the game's stream) lands a surprise with chance `CRAZY_SURPRISE_CHANCE = 0.3` (tested within 0.1 over 60 drafts) and a second draw picks it: a **free re-spin** (`respinsLeft + 1`), a **golden spin** (`forcedTwist = 'golden'`: the next spin is golden without a twist draw; offered only when champions are on the wheel at the roll, so it is never announced under Underdog or in a league without tables, where it would do nothing) or a **forced position** (`forcedSlot`, only while the XI is filling with two or more open slots some club can fill: the next pick must go there, the wheel lands only on clubs that can fill it, the bench is shut and `chooseSlot` refuses another slot; under Position First it is also the chosen slot, so a re-spin row names it). `targetSlot` returns the forced slot first. The Draft screen shows "Surprise!" and the forced slot on the pitch.
- **Underdog.** `rulesAdmit` keeps only `bottomHalf` club-seasons: below half the places of that season's final table; a league-season without a table (France, the Netherlands, Portugal) ranks its clubs by the mean of their eleven best ratings (ties by club id) and the lower half count, cached per universe in a `WeakMap`. Golden twists then never qualify, so the twist draw offers the others.
- **Legends.** `rulesAdmit` keeps only club-seasons with a player rated `LEGENDS_RATING = 85` or more that season (`hasLegend`).
- **Gamble** 🎲 (`modes: ['gamble']`, `src/engine/icons.ts`). Every spin until it has come draws once from the draft's own Icons stream (`DraftState.gambleRng`, seeded `seed:gamble-icons` by `newDraft`); with chance `ICONS_CHANCE = 0.01`, and only when an icon fits this spin (an open slot, the chosen slot, the nation rule; anyone undrafted for the bench), the spin lands on the **Icons collection** instead of a club-season: `d.current` is `{ clubId: 'icons', season: 0 }`, the game's stream is not drawn at all, and no twist applies. At most once per draft (`iconsUsed`: landed on, re-spun or picked from). The collection (`iconsCollection`) is the `ICONS_SIZE = 60` best players of all time on the draft's wheel leagues, each at his prime season (`primeSeasons`: highest rating, ties to the earlier season, then the smaller id), one season per player; with European Nights that is all seven leagues. Classic seasons (estimated ratings) are never in it, Classic on or off. The reel lands on a gold-star Icons card in its own colour above gold (`--tier-icons`, aqua), with a shimmer and a star burst; the odds panel lists Icons at 1% above gold; the squad list shows the icons with their clubs and seasons; the draft log marks the row with a star. Gamble also brings the Player Upgrader.
- **Upgrader** ⬆️ (`DraftRules.upgrader`, also on with Gamble; `src/engine/upgrader.ts`, `src/ui/components/Upgrader.tsx`). Once per draft, from the draft screen's Player Upgrader panel: choose a drafted player and a boost of +2, +5 or +10. The chance is shown as a whole per cent on a dial in the rarity colours before confirming (`upgradeChance`: 80%, 55% or 30% for a player rated 60 or less, falling linearly to a quarter of that at 100, clamped to 1-95%), and it is the exact chance: the roll is 0-99 from the draft's own `upgradeRng` (seeded `seed:upgrader`, forked once by the player-season id and the boost, so it is the same whenever in the draft it is made, and replaying the seed with "Run it back" after seeing one choice's needle tells nothing about another choice) and wins when `roll < chance`. A win raises his rating by the boost (never above 99), a loss drops it by half the boost rounded up (1, 3 or 5); he stays in the side either way. The result is `DraftState.upgrade` (`{ id, boost, chance, roll, won, delta }`, kept with a quick game's draft); `playerLookup` moves that player's overall and every attribute by `delta` (`boostedPlayer`, a cached copy; the universe is never changed), so the draft pitch, team strength and the whole quick season see it. A challenge link carries it as `g: [row, boost]` (the row of `p` whose pick was upgraded), and the replay makes the same gamble after the draft, which lands the same. In-game only: nothing is bought, there is no currency, and the odds are always shown first. The sheet (`UpgraderStage` in `Upgrader.tsx`, shared with the January Upgrader) is an upgrader site's: the players, three presets (+2 Safe, +5 Bold, +10 All in, each with its exact chance for the chosen player), the win and loss lines, and a big dial (`UpgradeDial`) whose win arc covers rolls 0 to chance - 1 clockwise from twelve o'clock in the boost's rarity colour (purple, pink, red). Confirming asks the store for the gamble first; the needle then spins five turns with a long ease-out (`src/ui/upgradeDial.ts`: 5.2 s, 2.2 s with Quick spin, a 0.5 s jump with reduced motion) onto a fixed spot inside the logic's roll (`landingAngle`, a fifth to four fifths across the roll's 3.6 degrees, so the needle is inside the arc exactly when the roll won; tested for every roll and chance), ticking as it passes the ring's twenty marks, then the verdict: a glow, a shake and a rising sting on a win, a dim and a falling "wah-wah" on a loss. Tap the dial, Tap to skip, Space or Enter lands it at once.
- **Power-ups** 🧰 (`DraftRules.powerUps`, a toggle under the Modes cards, with a `RULE_CHECKS` entry; `src/engine/powerups.ts`, `src/ui/components/PowerUps.tsx`, `PowerUpButton.tsx`, `src/game/logic/powerups.ts`). A tray under the reel of six power-ups, each usable once per draft, each with an icon, a state line and an Info tip (`InfoTip`): **Upgrader** ⬆️ (the Player Upgrader above, moved into the tray; power-ups turn it on); **Re-roll season** 🔁 (while a spin is showing: keep the club and draw another of its seasons on this spin's wheel, twist and rules applied, each equally likely and listed with its chance before confirming; one draw from the draft's own stream `powerRng`, seeded `seed:powerups` by `newDraft` and forked by the spin, so the game's stream does not move; the spin count does not change; never on an Icons spin); **Peek** 👁️ (between spins: the season the next spin lands on, worked out by spinning a copy of the draft with a copy of the game's stream, so nothing is drawn; while it shows, the Position First slot cannot change and Swap waits, so the spin lands where the peek said); **Swap** 🔀 (between spins, no peek showing: move a starter to another slot he can cover, exchanging with the player there when he can cover the first slot; no RNG, no re-spin spent; a Position First slot it fills is cleared); **Captain's armband** ©️ (+2 to one drafted player's overall and attributes for the season, applied with the Upgrader's change by `playerLookup` through `draftBoost`); **Lock** 🔒 (before the Upgrader: an Upgrader loss on the locked player leaves his rating as it was, `DraftUpgrade.locked`, the roll unchanged). On screen each power-up is a Lucide icon (`POWER_UP_ICON` in `src/ui/components/icons.tsx`: an arrow up, a repeat, an eye, two arrows, the armband's C, a padlock; the case's Icon a star, the locker a backpack), on the tray, its sheets, the locker, the draft log, the Profile and the case's reel and odds; the emoji in `POWER_UP_INFO` are for the share text. Uses are kept in `DraftState.powerUps` (kind and the history row `at`), listed in the draft log (`powerUpLog`, each line with its `kind`) and as one icon line in the share text (`powerUpsShareLine`, "🧰 Power-ups ⬆️+5 🔁 👁️"). A challenge link carries them as `w` (`['r', row]`, `['p', row]`, `['s', row, from, to]`, `['a', pickRow]`, `['l', pickRow]`, each kind once, checked by `isChallengePowerUps`; a peek no spin followed, because the bench closed while it showed, changed nothing and gets no row); `replayDraft` makes the swap before its row's spin, the peek before it and the re-roll after it, then the replay locks, upgrades and gives the armband, so the same picks land on the same squad.
- **Speedrun.** The Draft screen runs a `SPEEDRUN_SECONDS = 20` clock per pick (a chip in the header, red from 5 s; paused while the reel runs; reset by every pick). It is the UI's alone: on expiry the store's `speedrunPick` calls `assistantTurn`, which chooses a slot under Position First, spins when no spin is showing (from the game's stream, as any spin) and makes `assistantChoice`'s pick (the choice `autoDraft` makes, now shared), so the draft history is an ordinary one and a link replays it.

No mode changes a draw of a draft without it, and with no flag `DraftRules` has no `modes` key at all, so the modes needed no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (it stayed `'4'`; the assistant's choice moved into `assistantChoice` unchanged; every seeded test passes as before). `modes.test.ts` checks every landed club-season of 30 Underdog and 30 Legends drafts, a twist on every Crazy spin, the surprise rate and kinds, 200 assistant drafts over five mode sets, both draft modes and a bench without a dry wheel, and verified replays of links with modes (a Crazy Position First draft included).

### Moneyball wheel: the salary cap

`DraftRules.budget` (£M, optional; off when absent, so every older save and every cap-less seed draws exactly as before) puts the draft under a salary cap: the board hands over a kitty and every player carries a price, so every spin is a budget call between stars and scrubs and spreading it.

- **Price** (`capPrice`, `src/engine/draft.ts`): `max(0.3, round1(0.25 × 1.21^(rating − 60) × POS_FACTOR[first position]))`, `POS_FACTOR` GK 0.75, CB 0.9, LB/RB 0.85, DM/CM 1, AM/LW/RW 1.1, ST 1.15. It is the data pipeline's own model value (`modelValue` in `scripts/import/build.mjs`) at peak age, deliberately age-neutral: on the real `value` an 80+ player of 33 or more costs about a quarter of a younger one, and a quick season has no ageing, so the oldest good man in every squad would be the only plan. A CM costs £1.7M at 70, £4.4M at 75, £11.3M at 80, £29.3M at 85, £52.0M at 88, £76.1M at 90 and £111.4M at 92; the dearest man in England is about £106M. `rating` is the season overall (under replica-setup's Prime ratings it becomes the drafting rating; `capPrice` takes it as an argument for that).
- **Presets** (`src/game/logic/cap.ts`): for an XI, Shoestring £120M, Moneyball £180M (the default once the cap is on), Big Spender £250M, or Custom £50M to £600M in £10M steps. A bigger squad scales the XI cap by `1 + 0.08 × (squadSize − 11)`, rounded to £1M (`capForSquad`): Moneyball is £252M for 16 picks, £281M for 18, £223M for the Gauntlet's 14. Uncapped, 120 auto-drafted England 4-3-3 XIs cost £190M / £222M / £274M / £346M / £380M at p10 / p25 / p50 / p75 / p90, so Moneyball sits under almost every assistant's XI.
- **Affordability** joins every usability check through one predicate, `affordable(d, p)`: a player is usable only when `capPrice ≤ budget − spent − 0.5 × (picks still to make after this one)` (`capRoom`). `BUDGET_FLOOR` (£0.5M) is kept back for every later pick, and England has 120 to 270 players at £0.5M or less in every position (GK 227, CB 269, LB 168, RB 151, DM 120, CM 266, AM 156, LW 146, RW 144, ST 192 player-seasons), so the wheel never runs dry. It sits in `canUse` and `eligibleSlots`, so `rulesPool`, the no-dead-spin rule, the spin weighting's usefulness count, `canSpin`, `autoDraft` and the Draft screen all inherit it; `pick` refuses with "Rodri (£52.0M) would take you £14.0M over the cap" (adding ", keeping £0.5M for each of the 5 picks still to make" when it is the floor that rules him out) and adds the price to `DraftState.spent`. Every check runs only when `rules.budget` is set, so the pins hold and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump is needed. **With a Quick Draft bench** (r7 QA) the floor is owed to the open XI slots only (`picksOwed`: the bench is optional and may close once the XI is full), and a bench pick made while XI slots are open keeps the floor for every one of them (`benchAffordable`, one floor more than an XI pick; `pick` refuses with "… would leave too little for the 3 XI places still open"). Before, bench picks counted against the XI's eleven, so benching early on Shoestring let the last XI places go unpaid and the wheel ran dry with a keeper still to find (about one draft in six on Shoestring with a bench of 3 or 5, drafted at random with the bench taken 40% of the time it was offered; none after); and `finishDraft` closes a dry wheel only with a full XI. Drafts that fill the XI before the bench (every assistant draft) price exactly as before.
- **The assistant spreads the money.** Under a cap, `autoDraft` (Auto-complete, Sim to the end's bonus spins) weighs only the players priced at no more than twice an even share of what is left (`CAP_AUTO_STRETCH`), or the cheapest usable man when nobody is; without a cap it is unchanged. Buying the best it could afford spent the cap on the first two picks. Measured over 60 seeds of a £180M quick XI: greedy 42 points on average, one share 59, 1.5 shares 59, two shares 61, three shares 56. With two shares, over 100 quick seeds (`cap-measure-0` to `-99`, England 4-3-3, calibrated, Burnley 2025-26): no cap 68.1 points (20 titles); £120M 51.9 (spent £119.1M on average, 1 title); £150M 56.5 (1); £180M 59.9 (spent £172.6M, 5); £250M 63.5 (spent £220.6M, 8). Every capped draft completed.
- **Twists stack.** A golden spin whose champions are all too dear drops out like any empty twist (the twist candidates narrow the same affordable pool). Blind hides ratings, never prices.
- **Where it applies.** Quick Draft, Season and the first year of a Dynasty (Setup: the Moneyball card in the Quick Draft's Modes row, or "Salary cap" under Advanced / More options), and the arena's Cup and Gauntlet (scaled to 16 or 14 picks; `ArenaChoices.capXI`). The Daily never has one: its rules are pinned. A managed season's cap binds the draft only; `beginSeason` keeps the spend as `Game.capSpent` for the summary, and the Dynasty summer draft drops the cap (`budget: undefined` in `advanceToNextSeason`'s `newDraft`), so the market takes over. A Gauntlet bonus spin grows the run's cap with its squad (`grownBudget`: the same XI cap, scaled to one more pick), so the extra man is always affordable.
- **Not with one club or one nation** (`capFits`, `src/engine/draft.ts`). Their pools are too thin for the floor: Brazilians in England have nobody at £0.5M in some positions, so on £120M a draft that buys the dearest affordable man every spin runs dry short of an XI (8 of 78 one-nation drafts by hand across the 26 offered nations, 27 of 78 on £50M; one club on £50M, 19 of 129), and a dry wheel only lets you finish once the XI is full. Setup switches the cap off when a club or nation is chosen and clears them when a cap is chosen (`withCap` in `src/game/logic/cap.ts` drops a cap the rules cannot carry), and `isRulesDiff` refuses a link carrying both.
- **Screens.** Setup: a Moneyball card in the Quick Draft's Modes row (`moneyball-toggle`, on: the £180M preset) and `CapControl` (`src/ui/components/CapControl.tsx`, the presets and a custom cap) under Advanced or More options; the arena setup: `CapControl` under More options; the help line gives the cap for the squad size; the setup line adds `cap £180M`. Draft: a header chip `£178M of £180M` (the next pick's room in its tooltip), every squad row's price, and a `£` chip on a man who cannot be afforded, dimmed like any unusable row, its tooltip giving the reason. Summary: the share text opens its extra lines with `💰 £178M of a £180M cap · 0.52 pts per £M` (an arena run: `💰 £221M of a £252M cap`, no rate), and the card footer reads `Seed K7Q2-MX9A · £180M cap`. Profile page: `LeaderboardEntry.budget` and `spent`, a `£ £178M of £180M` chip, and a "Best on a budget" filter (capped seasons only, by points, then the smaller spend). The Quick Draft's line above Spin names it too (`Moneyball £180M cap`, `quickSetupLineFull` in `src/game/logic/setupLine.ts`, since 2026-10-27), and the summary banner carries a `£` chip with what was spent of the cap (`£172M of £180M`, `casualChips` in `src/game/logic/casualChips.ts`).
- **Links.** `RULE_CHECKS.budget` accepts a number from 50 to 1000. `budget` is not in `V1_RULE_KEYS`, so a capped link travels as version 2 and an older build reads it as "newer version"; it replays and verifies like any other, `pick` checking the cap on every row.
- **Achievement** `moneyball`, Bargain Hunters: win the league on a cap of £150M or less for an XI (a bigger squad's budget is read back to its XI on the £10M grid, `xiCapOfBudget`), in the cap's own season (not a Dynasty's later years, not a sacked season).

### Teammate Chain

38-0's Teammate Chain. `DraftRules.chain` (optional boolean; off when absent, so every older save and every chain-less seed draws exactly as before): every club the wheel lands on must hold a former teammate of your last pick, and you must draft one of them, so the XI is one unbroken dressing room. It is a pure 38-0 puzzle and makes no chemistry claim: the quick draft's one-club-season-once rule forbids the same-season pairs that chemistry rewards.

- **Teammates** (`src/engine/teammates.ts`): `teammateIndex(u, leagues)` maps every real player id to the club-seasons (`clubId@season`) he appeared in, memoised in a `WeakMap` by universe and leagues. Two men are former teammates when they share one of those club-seasons, in any season (the era rule narrows the wheel, not who counts as a teammate). The index covers the club-seasons of `rules.leagues` only, never every loaded pack: player ids are shared across packs (a man who moved from England to Spain is one id in both), so an index over whatever the browser had loaded would give an England-only seed a different wheel on a device that had opened Spain. `formerTeammates` lists them, `sharedClubSeasons` names the club-seasons two men shared, latest first.
- **The rule** (`src/engine/draft.ts`). From the second pick, `DraftState.chainFrom` is the last pick and `DraftState.chainMates` the real ids of his former teammates (stored on the draft, sorted, so `canUse` needs no universe; about 43 ids for the median England player, 272 at most). `chainAllows(d, p)` joins `canUse` (through `usableBy`, which builds the teammate set once per pass over the wheel) and `eligibleSlots`, so the rules pool, the no-dead-spin rule, the spin weighting's usefulness count, `autoDraft` and the Draft screen all follow the chain; bench picks follow it too. `pick` refuses anyone else with "Modrić is not a former teammate of Kroos" and writes the link on the history row: `chainLink`, the latest club-season the two shared.
- **The break.** When no club-season in the pool holds a usable teammate of the last pick, the chain breaks: `spin` sets `chainMates` to null, that spin is free (the plain pool), the pick on it carries `chainBroken: true`, and the chain restarts from that pick. `canSpin` is unaffected, so the chain never runs the wheel dry. `chainBreakDue(u, d)` tells the Draft screen before the spin.
- **Measured** (England, 4-3-3, 100 auto-drafted quick XIs `chain-0` to `chain-99`): the chain-bound wheel holds 80 / 152 / 222 of the 380 club-seasons at p10 / p50 / p90, never fewer than 13; no draft broke. The chain breaks where the pool is thin: one nation (Brazilians in England: 76 breaks over 40 drafts, 38 of them broken at least once; French 67), or a £50M cap (5 breaks over 40). One season or one club never broke. It costs quality: the assistant's chained XI averaged 79.5 overall against 82.6 unchained, and 60 quick seasons (`chain-measure-0` to `-59`, Burnley 2025-26, calibrated) scored 50.6 points with 2 titles against 69.6 and 16 unchained.
- **Gated:** every chain check runs only when `rules.chain` is set, so the pins hold and no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump is needed. Setup writes the rule only when it is on (`chain: true`), so a chain-less link's rules diff is unchanged.
- **Where it applies.** Quick Draft, Season and the first year of a Dynasty (Setup: the Teammate chain card in the Quick Draft's Modes row, or the toggle under More options for a season). The Daily never has one. A managed season keeps the record as `Game.teammateChain` (`{ clubs, broken }`, `chainRecord` in `src/game/logic/chain.ts`) when `beginSeason` drops the draft; the Dynasty summer draft drops the rule (`chain: undefined` in `advanceToNextSeason`). It stacks with twists, one club, one nation and the salary cap.
- **Screens.** Setup: the Teammate chain card in the Modes row (`chain-toggle`; for Season and Dynasty the same `ChainToggle` under More options); the setup line adds `teammate chain`. Draft: `Teammate chain` in the header line; a notice in the wheel column naming the man the next pick must have played beside, or "Chain broken: no teammate of Kroos left on the wheel" before a free spin; every teammate's row carries a `🔗 Real Madrid 2017-18` chip (the club-season they shared), everyone else is dimmed with "not a teammate"; the log shows each link ("🔗 Kroos → Modrić, Real Madrid 2017-18") and each break. Summary: `🔗 Teammate chain · 11 links` (every pick in one chain) or `🔗 Teammate chain · broken once` in the share text's extra lines, and the card prints the record and the chain of club codes in its `lines`, eleven codes to a line (`ARS→MCI→LIV`, a break written `/`; a squad of 18 takes two lines, the second starting with the arrow that carries it on). Since 2026-10-27 the Quick Draft's line above Spin says `Teammate chain` (`quickSetupLineFull`), the summary banner carries a link chip (`Chain: 12 links` or `Chain broken twice`, `casualChips`), and the draft log's chain note takes a row of its own under the pick.
- **Links.** `RULE_CHECKS.chain` is a boolean and not a v1 key, so a chain link travels as version 2; it replays and verifies like any other, `spin` and `pick` applying the chain on every row.
- **Achievement** `chain-champions`, Dressing Room: win the league with an unbroken teammate chain, in the chain's own season (not a Dynasty's later years, not a sacked season).

### Ghost drafts: challenge links and run-it-back

A finished quick draft can be packed into a link and replayed on another device, because the wheel, the picks and the quick season all run on the one seeded `Rng`.

- **Payload** (`src/engine/challenge.ts`, `ChallengePayload`, `v: 1`, or `v: 2` past the version 2 gate in section 15): the data stamp (`LoadedUniverse.generatedAt`) and `ENGINE_VERSION` it was built on; the challenger's name, cut to `CHALLENGE_NAME_MAX = 24` characters with control characters stripped; `s` = seed, formation, league, wheel leagues, host club, league season, difficulty and the draft rules as a diff against `QUICK_RULES` (`leagues` always travels as `wl`); `p` = one row per draft history row, `[playerId, slotId]` with the real player id (`s200458`) rather than the player-season id, and `[null, null]` for a re-spin or a discarded spin (`[null, slotId]` under Position First); `c` = the claim (points, won, drawn, lost, tier); `k` = `historyKey`, the cyrb53 hash in base 36 of the club-seasons the wheel landed on, in order; `x` (optional, `ChallengeSeason`) = the quick season's own choices when any differ from a default season: `t` the first-half tactics (the whole season without a window) as the instructions off `defaultTactics` plus `captain` and `penalties` player-season ids, `t2` the second-half tactics when they changed at the January pause, `j` the January deal as `[incoming player-season id, slot id]`, `s` the Quick Draft bench changes as `[first matchweek, slot id, incoming player-season id]` (at most three, section 12), `m` the Quick Draft manager's id (section 12, "Managers"). A default season writes no `x`, so its link is byte for byte what it was before. `encodeChallenge` writes it as UTF-8 JSON in base64url after `#c=`: about 600 to 650 characters for an eleven-pick England quick draft with default rules, 28 of them the always-written `difficulty` (a full player-season id per pick would be about twice that). `decodeChallenge` checks every field, the formation id, the tier and the rules diff key by key (only the twenty-four known rules, each with its type: `squadSize` an integer from 11 to 18, `respins` 0 to 10, `benchPicks` 0 to 5, `minFit` one of the four fits, `difficulty`, `draftMode` and `ratings` one of their values, the flags booleans, `leagues` never, since it travels as `wl`), and throws "Not a Touchline challenge link" (or "comes from a newer version" when `v` is above 2) rather than returning a partial payload. `openChallenge` then refuses a link naming a league this build does not ship, or a host club that did not play that league season.
- **Replay** (`replayDraft` in the engine, `replayChallenge` in `src/game/logic/challenge.ts`): `createGame` from the link's settings, then for every row one `spin`; a null row decrements `respinsLeft` and logs the row the way `respin` does without spinning again, a pick row rebuilds `${playerId}@${season}@${clubId}` from the club-season the wheel landed on and calls `pick` (which takes no RNG). The replay must end complete (or, on a one-nation wheel that has run dry, with eleven and no spin left), with as many history rows as `p`, and with `historyKey` equal to `k`; then the season from `x` (the tactics applied; with `t2` or `j` the window opened, the second-half tactics set, the deal found among the listings or the replay refused; otherwise `runQuickSeason` in one go) and the result is compared with the claim figure by figure. The outcome is `verified: 'yes'` when it matches, `'no'` with the reason ("Spin 4: no player s000001 in the burnley 2025 squad", "the picks replay to 70 points (20-10-8), not 73", "The wheel did not land on the same club-seasons") when the picks or the claim were tampered with, and `'unverified'` with the reason when the data stamp or the engine version differ, in which case nothing is replayed beyond a best-effort draft so the ghost picks can still show. Nothing throws. A replay costs one quick season, about 300 ms in the test suite.
- **Every spin is in the history.** "Auto-complete" used to drop a pending spin (`draft.current = null`) that had already consumed RNG, so a link built from such a game would drift on replay; it now pushes the spin as `{ pickedId: null, slotId: null, skipped: true }` (with its twist) and the draft log shows it as "skipped". `respin` already logged the spin it discarded.
- **Opening a link.** `App.tsx` reads `location.hash` once the default league is loaded (and on `hashchange`), clears it with `history.replaceState` so a reload does not reopen it, and calls the store's `openChallenge`: decode, refuse a league this build does not have, `ensureLeagues` for the link's league and wheel leagues, replay, then `pendingChallenge` (transient, never saved) and the Setup screen. `sw.js` serves `index.html` for navigations, so the link works offline and under a base path. Setup's daily lock is generalised into a `fixed` object (daily or challenge) that hides the seed, the wheel rules, the league, the host, the season, the difficulty and the formation and shows the banner "Challenge from Minh: 70 pts as Leeds United 2025-26, 4-3-3 (TOP-SIX FINISH, 20-10-8). Same seed, same wheel, calibrated difficulty, twists on. Seed …" with the verification line under it; an unverified or unverifiable link still plays. The game starts with `mode: 'quick'` whatever seed the link carries, a daily seed included, so the Daily streak and `dailyResults` are untouched, and `GameSettings.challenge` (`ChallengeSettings`: name, the picks resolved to player-season ids with their club-season, the claim, `verified`, `reason`, data and engine stamps) travels with the save; older saves have no field and read as a plain game.
- **The ghost.** The Draft header reads "vs Minh (70 pts)", and once the wheel settles a chip under it shows the challenger's row for the same spin number: "Last time here: K. De Bruyne" when the wheel landed on the same club-season, "Minh's spin 5: K. De Bruyne (Manchester City 2017-18)" once the drafts have diverged (a different pick changes `takenPlayers` and the pool, so later spins land elsewhere), "Last time here: Minh re-spun" for a null row. The chip is hidden in blind mode (hard or Show ratings off, `ratingShown(rules, 'ghost')`) and absent when the picks could not be resolved on this data.
- **Summary.** "Head-to-head · vs Minh": both XIs on two pitches with points, tier and record, the verification line, and a verdict, `⚔️ Beat Minh by 4 points: 74 to 70`, `⚔️ Minh wins by 1 point: 90 to 89`, `⚔️ Level with Minh on 90, fewer defeats`, `⚔️ Dead level with Minh: 90 points each`, which also goes into `extraLines` of the share text. "Challenge a friend" (any quick or daily result, since quick mode keeps `game.draft` after the season) packs this game with the manager name from the Profile page (the team name when there is none), copies the link and shows it in a field; "Run it back" starts the same wheel at once with this result as the ghost named "your ghost", verified without a replay because it is this device's own record; "Draft again" drops the challenge with the seed. The leaderboard entry stores `challenge: { name, points }` and the Profile page shows "vs Minh 70" beside it, green when beaten.
- **Best on this wheel.** The Profile page groups the quick and Daily entries of the leaderboard by seed: with two or more runs on one seed the best carries a gold "Best on this wheel · N runs" chip and the others "2nd of N on this wheel". Season and Dynasty entries are never grouped: a dynasty writes one entry per year under one seed, which is not the same wheel twice.

- **Arena links.** A finished Gauntlet, Cup or Cup Final packs into a link too (`arenaChallengeFromGame`), so a friend climbs the same ladder or faces the same draw with your result on every step; section 17 has the payload, the replay and the honesty rule (only a run the assistant played verifies; a run managed by hand reads "managed by hand").

Links break on an engine bump (`ENGINE_VERSION`) or a data rebuild, by design: the stamps make that an "unverified" banner instead of a silent mismatch.

## 6. The player model

A **player-season** is the unit the wheel drafts: one person at one club in one season, with the rating he earned that year (`src/engine/types.ts`, `PlayerSeason`). Its id is `${playerId}@${season}@${clubId}`, so a mid-season move produces two drafts of the same human, of which you may take only one.

Fields: name, nationality, age, club, season, up to three natural positions (primary first), thirteen attributes (eight classic, five depth), four hidden traits, overall, value (GBP millions, today's money), weekly wage (GBP thousands), preferred foot, height, and accolades (`Champion` when his club won that league).

### Positions and attributes

Ten positions: GK, CB, LB, RB, DM, CM, AM, LW, RW, ST, grouped as GK, DEF, MID, ATT.

Eight attributes on 1 to 99: pace, shooting, passing, dribbling, defending, physical, mentality (decision making, composure and leadership rolled together), goalkeeping (only meaningful for keepers). They decide ratings, roles and fit.

Five depth attributes on 1 to 99, the Football Manager layer (`src/engine/depth.ts`): **stamina** (condition lost per match, the fade after the 70th minute, who comes off, the injury roll when tired), **strength** and **jumping** (set pieces and the aerial mismatch between one side's attack and the other's defence), **aggression** (cards, a little bite in the press) and **work rate** (how well pressing and counters run, how fast a player learns the system). They never enter `overall` or a role rating, so a 78 is still a 78; they change what happens to him over ninety minutes and a season. The data packs carry them (docs/DATA.md); an older pack has them derived from the eight: stamina from physical and age, strength from physical and height, aggression from defending and mentality, jumping from height and physical, work rate from mentality.

Four hidden traits in 0 to 1: consistency, injury proneness, big-match temperament, professionalism. They are never shown as numbers: the attribute panel draws big match, consistency and professionalism as a bar with a word (low, average, high) and injury proneness as a 1 to 5 **Injury risk** (`ceil(proneness × 5)`: robust, sturdy, average, fragile, glass). They shape variance, injuries, big games, discipline, morale and development.

The attribute panel (`src/ui/components/AttributePanel.tsx`, in the Dugout's player modal and Explore's career view) groups them FM-style: Technical (shooting, passing, dribbling), Physical (pace, stamina, strength, jumping), Mental (mentality, aggression, work rate, big match, consistency, professionalism), Defending, Goalkeeping, Fitness (injury risk and, for a squad player, condition). The squad list has a per-browser toggle that adds each player's stamina as a chip.

### Position weights and fit

`POSITION_WEIGHTS` gives each position a relative attribute mix (for example ST: shooting 0.38, pace 0.18, physical 0.16, dribbling 0.14, mentality 0.14). `overall` is the weighted mean at the primary position; in the shipped data it is the source rating from the dataset, with the weighted mean only as a fallback.

Out of position, a multiplier applies. Natural is 1.0. Anything not in this table is **unsuited** at 0.62, and any outfield player in goal (or keeper outfield) is 0.35.

| Natural | Related positions and factor |
| --- | --- |
| CB | DM 0.88, LB 0.86, RB 0.86 |
| LB | RB 0.90, CB 0.86, LW 0.84, DM 0.82 |
| RB | LB 0.90, CB 0.86, RW 0.84, DM 0.82 |
| DM | CM 0.93, CB 0.86, LB 0.80, RB 0.80 |
| CM | DM 0.92, AM 0.90, LW 0.80, RW 0.80 |
| AM | CM 0.90, LW 0.88, RW 0.88, ST 0.86 |
| LW | RW 0.94, AM 0.90, ST 0.86, LB 0.78, CM 0.78 |
| RW | LW 0.94, AM 0.90, ST 0.86, RB 0.78, CM 0.78 |
| ST | LW 0.88, RW 0.88, AM 0.86 |

Fit bands: **natural** 1.0, **accomplished** ≥ 0.86, **awkward** ≥ 0.75, **unsuited** below. The draft requires at least accomplished; a player with several natural positions uses the best factor among them.

### Roles and the rating formula

Thirty-four roles (`src/engine/roles.ts`), each with its own attribute weights, a split of the player's quality between the team's units, and flat tactical effects. The rating of a player in a role at a slot is:

```
roleRating = (0.55 × overall + 0.45 × roleWeightedMean) × positionFactor
```

The 55/45 blend anchors to the rating people recognise while letting the role decide who suits the job.

| Position | Role | Unit contribution (GK / DEF / MID / ATT) | Effects |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GK | Shot Stopper (default) | 1 / 0 / 0 / 0 | solidity +2 |
| GK | Sweeper Keeper | 0.85 / 0 / 0.15 / 0 | creativity +2, solidity −1 |
| CB | Stopper | 0.95 / 0.05 / 0 | solidity +2 |
| CB | Covering Defender (default) | 0.95 / 0.05 / 0 | solidity +1 |
| CB | Ball-Playing Defender | 0.80 / 0.20 / 0 | creativity +2, solidity −1 |
| LB, RB | Full-Back (default) | 0.80 / 0.15 / 0.05 | solidity +2 |
| LB, RB | Wing-Back | 0.50 / 0.20 / 0.30 | creativity +3, solidity −2, fatigue +6 |
| LB, RB | Inverted Full-Back | 0.55 / 0.40 / 0.05 | creativity +1, solidity +1 |
| DM | Anchor (default) | 0.60 / 0.40 / 0 | solidity +3, creativity −1 |
| DM | Deep-Lying Playmaker | 0.30 / 0.60 / 0.10 | creativity +3, solidity −1 |
| DM | Ball-Winning Midfielder | 0.50 / 0.50 / 0 | solidity +2, fatigue +6 |
| CM | Box-to-Box (default) | 0.30 / 0.45 / 0.25 | fatigue +5 |
| CM | Central Playmaker | 0.15 / 0.65 / 0.20 | creativity +3, solidity −1 |
| CM | Mezzala | 0.10 / 0.50 / 0.40 | creativity +2, solidity −2 |
| CM | Carrilero | 0.40 / 0.55 / 0.05 | solidity +2 |
| AM | Advanced Playmaker (default) | 0 / 0.50 / 0.50 | creativity +3 |
| AM | Shadow Striker | 0 / 0.25 / 0.75 | creativity +1 |
| AM | Trequartista | −0.05 / 0.45 / 0.60 | creativity +4, solidity −3 |
| LW, RW | Winger (LW default) | 0.05 / 0.30 / 0.65 | creativity +3 |
| LW, RW | Inside Forward (RW default) | 0 / 0.15 / 0.85 | creativity +1 |
| LW, RW | Wide Playmaker | 0.05 / 0.50 / 0.45 | creativity +3, solidity −1 |
| LW, RW | Defensive Winger | 0.30 / 0.45 / 0.25 | solidity +3, creativity −2, fatigue +5 |
| ST | Poacher | 0 / 0 / 1 | creativity +1 |
| ST | Target Man | 0 / 0.20 / 0.80 | solidity +1 |
| ST | Complete Forward (default) | 0 / 0.15 / 0.85 | creativity +2 |
| ST | False Nine | 0 / 0.40 / 0.60 | creativity +3, solidity −1 |
| ST | Pressing Forward | 0.10 / 0.15 / 0.75 | solidity +2, fatigue +6 |

Creativity and solidity are percentages of expected goals (summed over the eleven; see section 8). Fatigue is extra condition lost per 90 minutes, averaged over the eleven.

### Live modifiers

In Season and Dynasty each squad player carries live state (`SquadPlayer`): condition, morale, sharpness, familiarity (all 0 to 100), the last six match ratings, injury, suspension, yellow cards and season statistics. The effective rating in a slot is `roleRating` multiplied by:

| Modifier | Formula | Range |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Condition | 1 at ≥ 85; `1 − (85 − c) × 0.003` down to 70; `0.955 − (70 − c) × 0.0045` below | about 0.66 at 5, 1.0 at 85+ |
| Morale | `1 + ((m − 65) / 35) × 0.02` | 0.969 to 1.02 |
| Sharpness | 1 at ≥ 75; `1 − (75 − s) × 0.0012` below | 0.934 to 1.0 |
| Familiarity | `0.95 + (f / 100) × 0.07` up to 70, `0.999 + (f − 70) × 0.0004` above | 0.95 at 0, 0.9745 at 35, 0.999 at 70, 1.011 at 100 |
| Form | `1 + clamp((avg of last 5 ratings − 7) × 0.02, −0.015, +0.015)` | 0.985 to 1.015 |

Engine 6 halved morale (±4% before) and form (±3%) and flattened familiarity above 70 (`0.95 + f × 0.0007` all the way before, 1.02 at 100); below 70 it is as it was, so a new squad's handicap and the arena's balance stand (a line flattened about 70 at both ends lifted a fresh arena squad 1.1% and the Gauntlet's Realistic climb to 2.14 rungs, over its guard of 2). The match engine turns rating points into goals exponentially and stretches them above 80, so a winning side's +9 per cent (morale 100, form +3%, familiarity 100) lifted an ordinary drafted XI to an elite one: managed Seasons averaged 3.65 to 3.88 goals a game and the assistant's drafted side 122 league goals (section 8, "Calibration").

Quick Draft and Daily pass no state, so all five are 1.

Starting state: drafted players begin at condition 96, morale 70, sharpness 70, familiarity 35. Signings arrive at familiarity 20 and sharpness 65. AI squads start at familiarity 70 and sharpness 85.

**AI sides keep their morale, familiarity and form** (engine 6, E6-3; `applyMatchToAiState` in `src/engine/season.ts`, used by the league, the cups, the Continental Cup and the arena): after a match an AI man's condition, sharpness, cards, suspensions, injuries and season figures move as a user's do, but his morale, familiarity and recent ratings stay where they began. A real squad already knows its system, and the AI never rotates or talks, so nothing pulled a winning side back: on engine 5 Arsenal's strength climbed from 82.3 to 86.7 by matchweek 15 and managed leagues drifted from 2.7 to 3.7 goals a game with 100-point champions. Nothing displays an AI man's morale or form. Morale reverting a third of the way to 70 each week (the to-do list's proposal) still left AI-only matches at 3.06 goals a game; frozen, 2.79 on Calibrated.

**The reserve keeper** (engine 6, E6-5; `src/engine/reserve.ts`): 190 club-seasons in the packs list one keeper and two none, so an injury put an outfielder in goal (goalkeeping about 20) for weeks. When an AI side has no fit keeper, `aiTeamInput` adds a reserve keeper to its pool: one fixed player-season per club-season (`reserve-gk-<club>@<season>@<club>`, his own `playerId`), rated at the squad's fifth percentile, never in the universe's index, so no draft, market, pool or search sees him; `aiLookup` and `playerLookup` find him for the match screens, and an arena shoot-out reads the AI side's takers and keeper through `aiLookup` too (before the release review it read the index, so he went in goal at a flat 30 and kicked as an unknown 50).

## 7. Team strength, chemistry and balance

`computeTeamStrength` (`src/engine/ratings.ts`) turns the eleven into four unit ratings.

1. Each slot's effective rating is split into GK, defence, midfield and attack by its role's contribution. Each unit's rating is the contribution-weighted mean of what flows into it.
2. **Numbers.** Defence, midfield and attack are each scaled by `0.88 + 0.12 × clamp(share / baseline, 0.5, 1.5)` against baselines of 4.0, 3.2 and 2.8 contribution shares, so a shape that starves a unit pays for it and one that floods it gains a little.
3. **Fill.** Units are multiplied by `filled slots / 11`; a missing goalkeeper multiplies goalkeeping by 0.4.
4. **Chemistry** is added to every unit. Over the 55 pairs in the eleven: +1 for two players from the same club-season, +0.4 for the same club within two seasons, +0.2 for a shared nationality (unknown nationality excluded). `linkPoints = min(1.5, score / 55 × 5)`. Average familiarity with the system adds `((avgFam − 50) / 50) × 1.2`, so chemistry runs from −1.2 to +2.7 rating points. The Dugout lists the top three club-season links.
5. **Balance** is `1 − (max unit − min unit) / 35`, clamped to 0 to 1.
6. **Overall** is `(0.18 GK + 0.30 DEF + 0.26 MID + 0.26 ATT) × (0.96 + 0.04 × balance)`.

`autoPickLineup` fills the hardest slots first (GK, then by scarcity of natural players), in two passes (fit ≥ 0.86, then anything), scoring `roleRating × condition × sharpness`, and benches the next nine by overall. It drives AI clubs. The user's side goes through `assistantLineup` (`autoPick` in `src/game/logic/squad.ts`: the Auto-pick button, the start of each season (the strongest eleven regardless of where the draft put people) lineup repair before kick-off and the arena hub's draw), which runs the greedy and then repairs it (round 6, item 6): a slot the greedy gave to a cover while a natural man for it was left out gets that natural man when the XI's total score rises, the cover moving into a slot of his own position (whose man leaves the XI) or to the bench, best move first, until no move helps. Filling the scarce centre-back slots first, the greedy had taken a DM/CM at 0.86 (D. Rice −12, N. Kanté) over a natural centre-back and left his own midfield slot to a weaker man. A cover stays only when he outscores every natural man left out (Haaland at right wing over a 77). Over 120 seeded Season drafts in five shapes the XIs with a cover beside a left-out natural went from 28 to 3, the total never drops, and about a quarter of Season XIs changed; AI clubs keep the greedy, so engine 6's calibration and every AI side hold.

### The slot picker

Apart from Auto-pick, the Dugout has one way to change the XI: tap a pitch slot and a picker opens (`SlotSheet` in `src/ui/screens/Dugout.tsx`, built on `Modal` with `sheet`: on screens of 720px or narrower a bottom sheet docked to the bottom edge, 80vh at most, rounded 16px at the top and sliding up in 0.32 s, as every phone sheet does, unless reduced motion is set; wider, an ordinary centred modal). It replaced the old two-tap swap (tap a slot, then a player or a second slot) on every screen size; tapping a player in the squad list or the depth chart now always opens his details. Its contents come from the pure `slotSheet(game, u, slotId)` (`src/game/logic/slotSheet.ts`):

- **On top**, the man in the slot with a **To bench** button, or a note that the slot is empty.
- **Can play there**: every other squad player with `canCover(p, position, 'accomplished')`, a position factor of at least `ACCOMPLISHED_FACTOR` (0.86), which is exactly the line `autoPickLineup` draws for its first pass (the literal 0.86 there and in `describeFit` is now that one constant). Sorted by `ratingAt(p, position)` (attributes weighted for the position, times the fit factor), each row has the state bars, a fit chip (fits, acc.), his whereabouts (`Starting at CM`, `Bench`, `Unused`) and on the right the number his disc would show there, `discRating` = `round(overall × positionFactor)`, the same function the pitch now uses.
- **Out of position**, folded by default: everyone else (awkward or unsuited, the pitch's awk.), with the penalised numbers.
- Injured or suspended men (`isAvailable` false, the filter auto-pick applies and the two cases `lineupProblems` reports) are dimmed and cannot be tapped, so the sheet never offers a man the pre-match check would then refuse.

Tapping a row calls `store.playInSlot(id, slotId)`: `assignPlayer` as before (a man from the bench or unused pushes the occupant to the bench; a man starting elsewhere swaps slots with him, which is the old two-tap swap), then a toast worded by `assignmentNote` from the lineup as it stood: `Saka in at RW, Bowen to the bench`, `Grealish in at RW, Salah to LW`, `Saka in at RW` into an empty slot, `Saka to the bench, RW is empty` from To bench. Names are surnames with their particles (`De Bruyne`, `van Dijk`); a position the shape repeats reads `left CB`, `right CB` or `left`, `centre`, `right` for three (`slotLabel`). The squad list's chip for a man neither starting nor on the bench now reads **Unused** rather than Out, so it is not read as injured.

In the Draft, a player with more than one eligible open slot no longer shows a "Choose a slot on the pitch" notice: the notice carries one chip per slot from `draftSlotChoices(d, p)`, which is exactly `eligibleSlots(d, p)`, reading `left CM 77 · fits` or `RW 73 · can cover` (`round(ratingAt)` in that position, `?` under hard mode or a blind spin), and tapping one makes the pick; the eligible slots on the pitch still pulse as a second way in. On screens of 720px or narrower the squad panel scrolls into view (`scrollIntoView`, smooth, to the top) each time a spin is revealed, since the stacked columns put it under the wheel.

## 8. The match engine

`simulateMatch` (`src/engine/match.ts`) produces expected goals per side, draws goals from a Poisson distribution and then dresses the result with a minute-by-minute report. It is played in three segments with two stops (1-45, 46-70, 71-90): `beginMatch` plays the first, `advanceMatch` the next two, `finishMatch` whatever is left plus the report, and `simulateMatch` is exactly `beginMatch → advanceMatch(70) → finishMatch`, so a week the assistant sims and a week played by hand run one path (a test holds them identical event for event, through a JSON round trip of the paused state). See "The 70th-minute window" below for the segment shares.

### Expected goals

```
BASE_XG = 1.28                        (1.32 to engine 5)
K       = 0.055
stretch(x)      = x + max(0, x − 80) × 0.8
attackPower(s)  = stretch(0.62 × attack + 0.38 × midfield)
defencePower(s) = stretch(0.55 × defence + 0.28 × goalkeeping + 0.17 × midfield)
base            = BASE_XG × pairingGoalFactor(home, away)

λ_home = base × exp(K × (attackPower(home) − defencePower(away)))
         × home.tactical.attack × away.tactical.defence × 1.12 × homeTeamTalk
λ_away = base × exp(K × (attackPower(away) − defencePower(home)))
         × away.tactical.attack × home.tactical.defence × 0.90 × awayTeamTalk
```

Neutral venues (cup final, friendlies) use 1.0 for both. **League scoring levels** (engine 6, E6-7): an AI side of Spain, Italy or Germany carries its league's `goalFactor` (`LEAGUE_GOAL_FACTOR` in `season.ts`: Spain 1.21, Italy 1.22, Germany 1.22); every other league's side carries none (`aiTeamInput` leaves out a factor of 1), as the user's does; a pairing takes the mean of two, or the one side's when only it has one, so a quick season or Season in La Liga scores like La Liga, two sides of those three leagues meet at the mean of theirs, and an English, French, Dutch or Portuguese AI side meeting one of them (a Continental Cup or arena tie, the Super League) plays at the Spanish, Italian or German level, not the mean (found in the release review; changing it moves the pinned Daily, so it is left for the owner). One base had left La Liga at 2.30 goals a game against the real 2.68, Serie A 2.29 against 2.74 and the Bundesliga 2.58 against 3.07: the packs rate those leagues' sides closer together than their results were. The stretch means the gap between a 90 and an 87 counts for far more than the gap between a 72 and a 69, so an all-time XI feels like one. Training and one-off event boosts multiply λ next (section 10).

Then:

- **Big matches.** When importance > 1, each side's λ is multiplied by `1 + (avgBigMatch − 0.5) × 0.14 × (importance − 1) × 2.5`. Importance is 1.4 for a cup final, 1.3 for a title decider (matchweek 28 onwards, you in the top two, them in the top three), 1.25 for a derby or cup semi-final, 1.1 for any other cup tie, 0.9 for a friendly.
- **Variance.** `λ × exp(N(0, σ))` with `σ = 0.06 + 0.12 × (1 − avgConsistency)`. A metronomic side swings about 6 per cent; a flaky one about 18.
- **In the air.** A player's aerial power is `(strength + jumping) / 2`. Each side's λ is multiplied by `1 + 0.04 × clamp((attackAerial − oppDefenceAerial) / 8, −1, 1)`: the attacking side's power weighted by each man's attack share (plus 0.3 so everyone counts) against the defending side's weighted by defence share, so a mismatch of eight points or more moves expected goals by 4 per cent each way (`aerialOf`, `mismatchFactor`). On top, every side gets a set-piece term from its three most powerful outfielders on the pitch: `((top3 aerial − 72) / 100) × 0.04`, clamped −0.03..+0.04 (`aerialBonus`), beside the designated takers' bonus below. Measured on a mid-table XI with the same seed: all men at 90 strength and jumping against all at 55 gives ×1.0475 and ×0.9535.
- **Legs.** After the 70th minute every starter still on has his effective rating cut by `0.10 × legs(stamina)`, `legs = clamp((75 − stamina) / 50, 0, 1)` (nothing at 75 and above, 5 per cent at 50, 10 at 25); the side's fade is the mean cut over the eleven on the pitch, substitutes and the goalkeeper counting as fresh, and the 71-90 segment's λ is multiplied by `(1 − ownFade) × (1 + oppFade)` for each side (`applyLateFade`). Over 600 seeded matches a side at stamina 35 scores 23.7 per cent of its goals after 70 against 24.0 for the same men at 90. Keepers are exempt from every legs effect (the fade, the stamina factor on condition loss and the tired-legs injury term) because their stamina in the data reads low by convention (36 on average in the Premier League pack against 67 for outfielders): he does not run, so the number says nothing about his last twenty minutes.
- **Short-handed.** Minutes played with fewer than eleven (red cards, injuries with no replacement) reduce own attack by up to 30 per cent and lift the opponent's by the same.
- **The soft ceiling** (engine 6, E6-4; `softCeiling`): λ is untouched up to `XG_KNEE` = 3.5, then bends towards `XG_CEILING` = 4.6 (`3.5 + 1.1 × tanh((λ − 3.5) / 1.1)`: 4.0 stays 3.97, 5.0 becomes 4.46, 5.8 becomes 4.57), floored at 0.12; each segment's λ is capped at its share of `XG_CEILING × 1.3` (the 1.3 leaves room for a man advantage). Engine 5 clamped λ at 5.8 and each segment at 3.5, which let sides reach 14-0 and 2-17. The knee sits above what the elite XI of the 38-0 chase makes against a calibrated league, so the chase keeps its odds; a plain `4.2 × tanh(λ / 4.2)` (the to-do list's proposal) also trimmed ordinary matches and took the elite XI's 38-0 from 1.8% to none in 400 seasons. Goals are `poisson(λ)`. Shots are `max(goals, poisson(7.5 λ))`, on target `max(goals, min(shots, poisson(3 λ)))`.
- Possession: `50 + 1.1 × (home midfield − away midfield) ± 4 per short or direct passing side + 2 for home + N(0, 3)`, clamped 28 to 72.

### Calibration

Engine 6 aimed at real football: 2.6 to 2.9 goals a game, the best side on 80 to 100 goals a season, a Golden Boot of 20 to 35, home advantage about 0.3, with the upsets, the xG story and the 38-0 chase kept. Measured on the England, Spain, Italy and Germany packs of 2026-10-25, engine `'5'` at ec520f3 against engine `'6'` on `engine-e6` (the measurement harness is not committed). All-AI leagues: every league-season with a table from 2008-09, played as an untouched league of AI sides. Managed Seasons: England 2012, 2015, 2018 and 2021, the assistant drafting and managing, 56 seasons on engine 5 and 28 on engine 6 per difficulty. Quick: 2025-26 Calibrated, the assistant's draft at each draft difficulty, 600 seasons on engine 5 and 300 on engine 6. The elite XI: `perfectChase.test.ts`'s lineup, 1,000 seeds. Real figures for the same seasons: the Premier League 2.78, La Liga 2.68, Serie A 2.74, the Bundesliga 3.07 goals a game.

| Measure | Engine 5 | Engine 6 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All-AI goals a game, England (Calibrated / Realistic) | 2.71 / 2.84 | 2.66 / 2.71 |
| All-AI goals a game, Spain / Italy / Germany (Calibrated) | 2.30 / 2.29 / 2.58 | 2.69 / 2.71 / 3.05 |
| All-AI best side's goals, England (Calibrated / Realistic) | 88.0 / 103.5 | 87.5 / 98.1 |
| All-AI Golden Boot, England (Calibrated / Realistic) | 30.0 / 36.9 | 31.2 / 35.1 |
| Home advantage (goals), home / draw / away | 0.30, 44.0 / 23.1 / 32.9% | 0.28, 43.6 / 23.9 / 32.5% |
| Managed Season, goals a game (Calibrated / Realistic) | 3.65 / 3.88 | 2.84 / 2.97 |
| Managed Season, draws | 16.3% | 22.7% |
| Managed Season, matches with 7 or more goals | 13.0% | 4.5% |
| Managed Season, the best AI side's goals | 152.9 (max 201) | 92.1 (max 128) |
| Managed Season, the drafted side's goals | 121.4 (max 225) | 99.9 (max 159) |

| Quick season, goals a game / champions' points | 2.83 / 79.0 | 2.74 / 78.9 |

Win rate by the strength gap between two AI sides (England, any venue, about 29,000 matches each):

| Gap | Engine 5: stronger / draw / weaker | Engine 6: stronger / draw / weaker |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 to 2 | 41.2 / 25.6 / 33.1% | 40.7 / 26.5 / 32.8% |
| 2 to 4 | 49.1 / 25.2 / 25.7% | 49.4 / 25.3 / 25.3% |

A fresh league barely moves, and the upset rate stands. What moved is the managed league, where engine 5's feedback (section 6, live modifiers) inflated every AI side's ratings over a season. The drafted side still wins most managed Calibrated titles (27 of 28) with fewer goals. The best AI side on Realistic (98.7) and the Spanish champions (103 to 114) sit at or a little over the 100 target, as Barcelona's and Real Madrid's real seasons did. The Gauntlet's Realistic climb (guard under 2) is 1.93 rungs, against 1.73 on engine 5. Its Calibrated climb is 4.44 rungs (11.3% reach ten), against 5.13 (22.3%).

### Team talks

Chosen before each match (default Encourage). Effects are a multiplier on your own λ with a line in the report:

| Talk | Outcome |
| --- | --- |
| Calm | ×1.02 if you are underdogs by more than 3 rating points or on a 55% roll, else ×0.99 |
| Encourage | ×1.025 on a 60% roll, else ×1.0 |
| Demand | Favourites by more than 4: ×1.03 (60%) or ×0.985. Otherwise ×1.02 (35%) or ×0.975 |
| Fire up | Professional squad (avg professionalism > 0.6): ×1.035 (50%) or ×0.98. Otherwise ×1.03 (60%) or ×0.97 |

### Discipline

Per player, probability of a yellow card over the match: `(0.108 + 0.12 × defensive share + 0.02 if physical > 82) × pressing (1.25 high, 0.9 low) × 1.2 if derby (importance ≥ 1.2) × (1.15 − 0.3 × professionalism) × (0.7 + 0.6 × aggression / 100)`, rolled once per segment at the segment's share of the match (`(to − from + 1) / 90`: 0.5, 0.278, 0.222). A booked player has a `0.07 × share × aggression factor` chance of a second yellow in each later segment. Straight red: `(0.002 + 0.002 × defensive share) × derby factor × share × aggression factor`. A keeper is booked at a fifth of an outfielder's rate (the factor 0.2 on every card roll: time-wasting, a late challenge). Engine 6 (E6-6): keepers were never booked, and a match averaged 2.46 yellows and 0.21 reds against the Premier League's 3.5 to 4.2 and 0.12 to 0.15; all-AI Premier League seasons now average 3.57 and 0.15. The aggression factor runs from 0.7 for the meekest to 1.3 for the most combative; over 500 seeded matches a side with every man at 99 collects 1.65 times the cards of the same side at 10 (737 against 447), and because outfield aggression averages about 68 the engine's card rate rose 15 per cent when it arrived (2.81 a match over the pinned 2,000-match rotation, from 2.45). A booking is stamped with its minute, so a second yellow, a straight red or a substitution for that player never comes earlier than the booking. A sent-off player stays in his slot for the second-half numbers (the man disadvantage is costed once, by the short-handed factor) and still counts as having been on the pitch before his dismissal. Suspensions count down one match for every match the player's side plays without him, for your squad and for every AI club.

### Injuries

Per player not sent off: `0.021 × (0.5 + injuryProneness) × conditionFactor × fatigueFactor × share` per segment, at a minute after his booking when he was booked in that segment (engine 6, E6-1: a booking used to land after he had gone off injured, in 0.76% of matches, and his replacement waited for the card's minute; a man booked so late that nothing is left of the segment is not rolled), where condition below 55 doubles the risk, below 70 is ×1.45 and below 80 is ×1.1, and `fatigueFactor = 1 + 0.3 × clamp((70 − stamina) / 40, 0, 1) × clamp((80 − condition) / 40, 0, 1)`: tired legs on an outfielder with little stamina add up to 30 per cent (stamina 30 or less at condition 40 or less), fresh legs, a durable player or a keeper add nothing. Over 1,500 seeded matches at condition 40 a side at stamina 30 suffered 636 injuries to 490 for the same men at 90; at condition 95 the two are level (245 to 249). Injury proneness is shown as the 1 to 5 Injury risk. Severity:

| Roll | Injury | Weeks out |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 55% | Knock, dead leg, tight hamstring, bruised ribs, twisted ankle | 1 to 2 |
| 30% | Hamstring, calf or groin strain, sprained ankle, hip problem | 3 to 5 |
| 12% | Torn hamstring, knee ligament damage, broken foot, shoulder dislocation | 6 to 10 |
| 3% | Ruptured ACL, broken leg, Achilles rupture | 12 to 24 |

Injured players are replaced from the bench if a fit, unsuspended substitute exists (scored by `overall × positionFactor × (condition / 100 + 0.5)`), otherwise the side finishes short. No automatic change (an injury, a routine change, the quick season's injury cover) ever brings a keeper-only man on outfield (`eligibleReplacement` in `src/engine/match.ts`, `fieldXI` in `quickBench.ts`); an injured keeper with no keeper on the bench is replaced by the best outfielder, the emergency case. Engine `'4'` and before scored a keeper outfield at `overall × 0.35` and took him when nobody else was left, so a Cup run had Al-Habsi come on for Modric at 70' while Henderson stayed in goal (TL-01; 25 of 80 assistant arena runs and 5 of 1,140 Season matches had one, `keeperSubs.test.ts`).

### Substitutions, goals and ratings

- Up to five substitutions; up to three routine changes around the 68th minute (σ 9, between 50 and 88), drawn once at the restart and tiredness-driven: a starter wants off if `condition − 8 (when tactical fatigue > 4) − 8 (if aged 33+) + 0.3 × (stamina − 65) + N(0, 6)` is below 72, or on falling odds of 75, 55 and 30 per cent for the first three candidates, the candidates ordered by that score so the low-stamina men go first (over 300 seeded matches with half the XI at stamina 40 and half at 90, the 40s came off 631 times to the 90s' 246). A change due after the 70th minute is decided then (the replacement is chosen and reserved) but parked in `pendingSubs` and made when the last segment is played, so the pitch at the 70' stop is the one the manager sees; a red card or injury to the man coming off, or a used substitute, cancels it.
- Each goal picks a minute (bias to late in each segment; a fifth of the last segment's goals fall in stoppage time, about 4.7 per cent of all goals, as 9 per cent of a whole second half's did), then: 2.5 per cent own goal by a defender; 9 per cent penalty, taken by the best shooting plus mentality; otherwise a scorer weighted by `(attack share × 1.6 + 0.08) × (shooting / 100)^2.2`, with an assist 78 per cent of the time weighted by midfield and attack share, passing squared and role creativity.
- Keepers are credited with a save only for a chance the log records as saved; a summary match without a log tallies saves at three of every eight chances, so AI keepers' ratings are comparable.
- Player match ratings start at 6.6, move 0.22 per goal of margin (capped ±0.8), +0.9 per goal, +0.5 per assist, +0.5 for a clean sheet or up to −0.6 for goals conceded (defenders, DM and GK), GK saves +0.12 each (max +0.9), `(effective rating − 75) / 100 × 1.2` for quality, Gaussian noise scaled by `0.45 × (1.1 − 0.7 × consistency)`, −1.2 for a red, −0.15 for a yellow, damped for cameos under 30 minutes, clamped 4.5 to 10. Man of the match is the highest rating with at least 20 minutes.
- Condition lost per player: `(26 + tactical fatigue + 5 if 33+ or 2 if 30+ + (100 − physical) / 10 + 2 if fast tempo) × (1.25 − 0.5 × stamina / 100) × minutes / 90`: the stamina factor is 1.0 at 50, 0.8 at 90 and 1.1 at 30 (a keeper always takes 1.0), so a side at stamina 35 loses nearly a third more than the same men at 90 (365 against 283 points over the XI per match in the seeded test).

The match screen plays the match on the match-day dashboard (see "The match-day dashboard" below), then shows the report: scoreboard, goals (either side of the minute; in a narrow box, each goal after its minute, led by its side's code on the side's colours), the momentum bar and the xG graph story, the shot map, possession and territory, xG (per attempt), possession, shots (on target) and the expected goals before kick-off, all four the dashboard's own numbers at the whistle, team ratings, tactical notes with the attack and defence multipliers, the half-time talk and the shout as they landed, player ratings with rating bars for both sides, substitutions, both sides' shapes with their instructions and the opponent's unit ratings.

### Watch, Auto-play and Sim result

`GameSettings.matchPlay` (optional; unset reads as `'watch'`, so every save written before it plays as before; Season and Dynasty only: `matchPlayOf` returns `'watch'` for Quick Draft and the Daily) is how the Dugout's play button plays the next match, changed in one tap on the Dugout's "Match:" chips. The store's `playWeek` calls `playMatchweekAs` (`src/game/logic/assistant.ts`):

- **Watch**: the match stops at half time and at 70 minutes and the dashboard's stop panel (`StopPanel.tsx`, test ids `half-time-panel` and `late-panel`) opens as a pop-up (`DecisionSheet`: a dialog centred over the board on every width, phone included, over a light scrim, at most 86 per cent of the window high and scrolling inside itself with its action row always visible; focus moves in and back; Escape or ✕ minimises it to a "Decisions" pill and the match stays paused). It holds the talk or shout, the four instructions and the substitutions (the top bar's drawers edit the same pending changes), and five actions. The substitutions list the stop's pending changes, each with Undo, and "Make a change" (`stop-make-change`) turns the pop-up into the same substitution board as the top bar's sheet (see Substitutions below), inline: drag, tap or key a substitute onto a starter, then "Make changes" sends the staged list through the hand's `submit` into the stop's pending changes, exactly as the sheet does at a stop, and the pop-up comes back with them listed; Back or Escape leaves without them. On a desktop the board sits inside the pop-up with no scroll (the sheet widens to 940 px and the pitch takes what its 86 per cent, 720 px cap leaves); on a phone it is full height. Changes at the break still spend no stoppage. The actions: Play on (your changes), No changes, Let the assistant decide (`assistantCall`), Finish the match (your changes now, the assistant takes the later stop and the match plays on the pitch to the whistle) and Full sim (your changes now, then straight to the whistle and the report). While watching, the top bar's "Auto from here" hands the stops still to come to the assistant and "Sim result" (while a stop is still to come; in the last twenty minutes "Skip to full time" does the same) finishes the match with him at once. From 70 the assistant's call, like yours, holds the last twenty minutes live (`assistantAdvance(true)`) so they play on the pitch to the whistle.
- **Auto-play**: the whole match is watched, and each stop is answered by the assistant 0.7 s after it is reached, with no pop-up (a note says the assistant is making the calls; the wait is keyed on the stop and the switch, so a re-render never restarts it). A match reloaded mid-way under Auto-play carries on with the assistant (`assistantTakesStops`, read when the save loads); a cup tie that follows the league match goes back to the saved preference, whatever "Finish the match" or "Auto from here" asked for the league match.
- **Sim result**: the match is played to the whistle at once with the assistant at both stops (`simLiveMatches`, the user's cup or European tie that follows too) and the screen opens on the report.

`assistantCall(game, u)` is pure and draws nothing. At half time: a talk from the score and the XI's mean morale (calm when ahead, encourage when level, fire up when behind with morale at 65 or more, encourage otherwise), and a change only for a man whose estimated condition is below 50 (a man injured in play has already been replaced by the engine when a fit substitute was free, so the assistant never sees him on the pitch). At 70: a shout from the score (all-out attack when behind, shut up shop one or two goals up, keep shape when level or three up) and changes for the injured and the tired, estimated condition below 65 (his kick-off condition less the engine's own cost per ninety for his minutes: age, physique, tempo, the side's fatigue and stamina), the most tired first; a keeper is never taken off for tiredness. Each is replaced by the best man on the bench for the slot: fit and unsuspended, not yet used in the match or this call, a natural fit before anyone else, then by rating in the slot times condition; never more than the changes left (three at the break, five in all). Instructions are left alone, because a change there would be written into the saved tactics. The calls go through `advanceLiveMatch`, the path a human's take, so the engine sees nothing new: Sim result and Auto-play give the same result, squad and generator state for the same save (tested over 35 matchweeks), and Watch with "No changes" at both stops reproduces results pinned before this change (six seeds, eight matchweeks, the final generator state included). `ENGINE_VERSION` did not change. Switching to a parked game, or starting a new one, sets the assistant switch from that game's own preference, never from the last game's. A live league match followed by a cup tie now shows the league result (the 71-95 replay) before the tie, which it skipped before.

### The 70th-minute window and touchline shouts

A live match stops twice: at half time and at the 70th minute. `MatchInProgress.clock` is 45, 70 or 90 (a paused match saved before the window existed has no clock and reads as 45, so it resumes at half time). `advanceMatch(mp, lookups, changes, to)` plays from the stop to 70 or to 90:

- **Segment shares.** Each segment takes `(to − from + 1) / 90 × (0.94 in the first half, 1.06 in the second)` of the match's expected goals: 0.47 for 1-45, 0.294 for 46-70 and 0.236 for 71-90 (0.53 for the second half, as before; Poisson additivity keeps the full-match goal distribution exact).
