Cards and injuries take the plain minute share (0.5, 0.278, 0.222); the two-half engine hard-coded 0.5 per segment, which three segments would have inflated by half. Pinned on the old engine over 2,000 seeded matches (seed `window-baseline`): 3.982 goals, 2.449 cards and 0.3745 injuries a match; the three-segment engine measures 4.027, 2.446 and 0.3755, within 3 per cent, and the last twenty minutes carry 19 to 26 per cent of the cards.
- **At half time** (clock 45): the human side's talk, instructions and up to three substitutions, as before; the AI side two goals down goes attacking (pressing low → mid), two up drops from attacking to balanced.
- **At 70** (clock 70): the human side's instructions, whatever is left of the five substitutions (minute 70, reason `70th minute`) and one shout for the last twenty minutes. All-out attack multiplies own expected goals by 1.3 and the opponent's by 1.35 and adds 4 to the side's fatigue modifier; shut up shop is ×0.55 own and ×0.72 opponent; keep shape is ×1 both ways. The shout is read at 70 only, the talk at 45 only, and the multipliers apply to the 71-90 segment alone. The AI side trailing at 70 goes attacking with a fast tempo; one goal up it goes defensive with a deep line; level or two or more up it keeps what it has. Both rules live in `advanceMatch`, never in the UI, so a simmed match reacts the same way. If the manager makes a substitution at 70, his side's parked routine changes are dropped; the AI's always go ahead.
- Strengths, modifiers and man-marking are recomputed at both stops, and the substitutions the engine made at a stop are written back into the lineup and the bench (only those: a man who has already played cannot come back on, so the panel at 70 does not offer the men taken off at the break, and a change the engine refuses never reaches the lineup). The report's `halfTimeEffect` and `lateEffect` carry the two interventions' text.
- The store action is `advanceLive(changes)`; `advanceLiveMatch` in `week.ts` plays to 70 and returns with `game.live` intact, or finishes the match and carries the week on. `finishLiveMatch` remains as "play to the whistle": the changes apply at the current stop and the side plays on unchanged. Because every match now consumes the stream differently, `ENGINE_VERSION` became `'3'`: challenge links built on `'2'` verify as unverified rather than failing. The depth attributes moved it to `'4'` (every λ change moves how many draws a Poisson takes), and the pinned means over the same 2,000-match rotation are now 4.122 goals, 2.812 cards and 0.3935 injuries a match (from 4.027, 2.446 and 0.3755); a seeded elite-against-mid match (`depth-pin`) is pinned at 1-0 with 25 events and 5 cards so any later change to the draws is noticed.

### Match detail and the pitch views

Each segment, once its goals, chances, cards and substitutions are decided, forks a stream off a copy of the generator (`Rng.fromState(rng.getState()).fork('detail-<from>')`) and fills in the detail the pitch views need (`src/engine/matchDetail.ts`). Because the fork is taken from a copy, the match's own stream, and the week's fixtures after it, never move: a pinned test (seed `detail-chances`, 100 matches) holds the goals, the chances and saves and the caller's final generator state to the engine without the detail, and the detail never moved `ENGINE_VERSION`. The pin was taken at 404 goals and 774 chances and saves on engine `'3'`; the depth attributes (engine `'4'`) moved it to 432 and 769, the same with the detail calls switched off, so it was re-pinned at those values.

- **Shot positions.** Every goal, penalty, own goal, chance and save carries `position: { x, y }`: x 0 to 100 along the pitch towards the goal the side attacks, y 0 to 100 across (105 by 68 metres, so the box edge is x 84.3 and the box spans y 20.4 to 79.6). Penalties are at the spot (89.5, 50). Own goals are at x N(96, 1.5) clamped 92 to 99. Headers, corners, tap-ins, six-yard and on-the-line efforts (read from the event text) are at x N(95, 2) clamped 90 to 99, y N(50, 7) clamped 30 to 70. Otherwise one effort in five (and every "edge of the box" one) is from outside the box, x N(79, 3.5) clamped 68 to 84, y N(50, 11) clamped 25 to 75; the rest are in the box, x N(90, 3.5) clamped 84.3 to 98.5, y N(50, 11) clamped 22 to 78. Over 150 seeded matches more than 60 per cent of shots are in the box and 10 to 30 per cent outside it.
- **Momentum.** `momentum` holds eighteen values, one per five-minute bin (1-5 to 86-90, stoppage time in the last), each the home side's share of the pressure, 0.08 to 0.92. A bin starts from `λh / (λh + λa)` for its segment (the expected goals the engine used, so tactical modifiers, shouts and a man down are already in it), adds the mentality and pressing lean (attacking +0.03, defensive −0.03, high press +0.015, low −0.015, home minus away), a chase term for the side behind (`0.05 × min(2, goal difference)`, ×1.2 from the 46th minute and ×1.6 from the 71st), ±0.12 for a goal in the bin and ±0.05 for one in the bin before, ±0.04 per chance or save in the bin and an N(0, 0.06) wobble, and then carries 35 per cent of the bin before. A match paused before the detail existed fills its missing bins with the base share.
- Both live in `MatchInProgress` (so a paused match carries the segments played) and in `FullMatchResult`. The team report adds `appearances` (each man's slot, minutes on and off, a red or an injury) and the final `tactics`, for the shape views. All fields are optional; older results simply show no shot map or pitch. Only a full-detail match (the user's league, cup, European and friendly matches) carries them: a summary match (the other fixtures of a week, every match of a quick season or a head-to-head) skips the detail, since nothing draws its pitch and every one is kept in the save's fixtures. With the detail on all 380 fixtures a season's save grew from about 9.8 to 14.2 KB per played fixture and passed the browser's 5 MB storage quota before the last matchweek; skipping it moves no result, because the detail never draws from the match stream. Since then the save also compacts every older stored result to its score (section 15).

The pitch views are pure UI (`src/ui/match/` and `src/ui/components/LivePitch.tsx`, `MatchCharts.tsx`):

- **Placement.** A slot's point is its formation coordinate, +4 along the pitch for an attacking mentality and −4 for a defensive one, the back line (slots below y 36) +7 for a high line and −6 for a deep one, wide slots (more than 22 from the centre) spread ×1.14 for wide and ×0.8 for narrow; the keeper never moves. The away side is mirrored through the centre spot. Opposing slots closer than six metres are pushed apart, and a substitute takes the point of the slot he fills.
- **The ball: build-up and phases.** `buildBallPath` (`src/ui/match/playbook.ts`, re-exported by `possession.ts`) lays out keyframes on one continuous timeline from a seed of its own (`ball-<seed>-<from>`, never the game's generator), one stretch per spell of the match path (`epochs.ts`, which also hands it both sides' style from their instructions: tempo, passing, width, pressing). A kick-off at 0 and 46 (both sides in their own halves for a moment), every other spell (71, the minute after a change) carries on from where the last one ended (`start`), and a stretch picked up mid-match without a start (a quick-season highlight) begins with the keeper of the side on top rolling it out. Every keyframe carries the phase of play (`phase`: build-up in a side's own third, progression through the middle, the final third, a counter for a quick break after a ball won in its own half, a set piece) and what the hop is (`play`: short, forward, back, switch, long, through, cross, cut-back, lay-off, dribble, tackle, interception, press, clearance, claim, header, deflection, rebound, foul, distribution, out, set piece).
  - **Open play.** The man on the ball takes a touch and looks up (0.5 to 1.1 s at 1x at the back, 0.35 to 0.85 in midfield, 0.25 to 0.6 in the final third, 0.15 to 0.35 on a counter, 1.1 to 2.1 for a keeper; ×1.3 for a slow tempo, ×0.78 for a fast one), then plays by his phase. Build-up: the keeper short to a centre-back or full-back (62 per cent; 85 short passing, 35 direct, 15 points fewer against a high press) or long into midfield; the back line circulates it, switches it across, steps in with it or goes long (11 per cent, 24 direct, 5 short). Progression: forward into midfield and beyond, a switch to the far wing (14 per cent, 22 wide, 7 narrow), a ball in behind, a dribble, a ball back to recycle. The final third: wide, a cross (32 per cent), a run down the line, a lay-off inside or a ball back; centrally, a ball in behind (20), a pass out wide, a one-two. Attacks with nothing in the log end the way attacks do: a cross headed clear (the second ball won by the side on top in proportion to its pressure), claimed by the keeper (20 per cent), glanced behind for a corner; a ball in behind collected by the keeper, cut out, or overhit for a goal kick. Between, the side on the ball loses it with probability `base + 0.32 × (1 − share)²` per decision (base 0.004 at the back, 0.01 in midfield, 0.025 in the final third, ×1.5 at the back against a high press, ×0.6 against a low one), to a tackle or the press (55 per cent) or an interception on the way to the man it was meant for; now and then a tackle squirts out for a throw-in near the touchline. Receivers are chosen from where they stand in the shape (`shape.ts`, the same one the dots are drawn in), within 48 units (`MAX_HOP`); a pass over 44 units goes in the air.
  - **Pace.** Durations are in metres at real speeds on screen at 1x (`SPEED` in `timing.ts`): a firm pass 11 m/s, a soft one 8, a long ball 14, a through ball 12.5, a cross 15, a carry 5, a dribble 6.5, a shot 22, a ball fetched for a restart 6. A 20-metre pass rolls for about 1.8 s, the median ground pass of 15 to 25 m for more than 1.4 s (tested), and a hop squeezed to fit before a moment may go at most 1.8 times its speed (1.25 for a cross) or it does not happen.
  - **The attack behind every shot.** Every shot in the log is struck at its event's position on its strike moment (`strikeTimes`, `timing.ts`: the k shots of a minute m start at m + (i + 0.55) / k and are spread apart within their minutes so the ball has time between them; the clock's goal holds use the same moments). The spacing is worked out in picture time (`matchPace`): a minute too crowded to hold its strikes at a walking pace (a goal and two more shots in it: the kick-off and the run to the next strike alone take most of a minute) lasts just long enough on screen, up to three minutes of picture, and the quiet minutes after it in the same stretch (no shot, no card, at most three) each play up to a quarter quicker to pay the time back. The ball path, the fouls, the live clock and the highlights reel all travel in picture time (`toPicture`, `toMatch`), so the ball keeps its speed and the clock on screen slows through the crowded minute instead; every strike stays inside its own minute, in the order it was logged. Its attack is picked from the event (`patternFor`): "heads it home from a corner" (and a third of other headers) a corner, other headers a cross, "rounds" or "chips the keeper" a ball over the top, "a flowing team move" a chain from the back with a switch on the way, "loose ball", "crowd of bodies" and "on the line" a scramble, a tap-in, "close range" and "six yards" a cut-back from the byline, a curler from the edge a lay-off (a direct free kick when nobody assisted it, half the time), a penalty a foul in the box and the spot, and the rest by a hash of the match, the event and the minute (cross, through ball, cut-back, counter, solo run, team move, lay-off). A goal's last pass comes from its recorded assister (the crosser, the corner taker, the man who plays the ball in behind or lays it off; over 70 per cent of assisted goals in 16 seeded matches, tested). The attack is planned from wherever the ball is when it starts (winning it back first when the other side has it), sized to the time before the strike: open play runs until the attack needs the rest, spare time goes to the touches and a set piece's wait (up to twice as long) and then to slower hops (up to a quarter); short of time, the touches shrink to 30 per cent and waits to half, then optional passes are dropped from the front, then the hops go up to 1.35 times quicker; when even that does not fit, the ball is won and played straight in, through the assister when there is time (a penalty still gets its foul and the spot). After the shot: in the net and a kick-off from the centre spot once both sides are back in their halves (0.35 of a minute after the hold); the keeper's save, held and thrown or kicked out, or (half of the brilliant saves) tipped behind for a corner that is set up and swung in; the post or the bar, back into play and cleared; wide or over and a goal kick, always taken by the keeper (quicker, with a shorter wait, when the next moment is close).
  - **Set pieces are staged.** The ball is fetched to its spot (shown dimmed as it is carried), waits there while the shape forms (`setPiece` on the keyframe with the moment it is taken: a corner 0.55 to 0.7 of a minute, never under 0.3, about 1.4 to 3 s at 1x; a free kick to `FOUL_STOP`; a penalty 0.9; a goal kick up to 0.4; a throw-in 0.3; a kick-off 0.18 at the start of a half), then is taken: a corner and a free kick near goal swung into the box (or played short), a goal kick short or long by the side's passing, a throw to a man nearby. Every booking has its foul (`foulTimes`, `timing.ts`): one per minute with a card, at a point of that minute picked by a hash of the minute and the side and moved to the nearest point clear of every shot's build-up (1.4 + 0.75 of a minute before it) and restart (0.7 after); with no such point it is not staged. The fouled side has the ball by then, its man runs at the man about to be booked and is brought down, the free kick is placed (just outside the box when the foul was inside it) and taken after `FOUL_STOP` (0.75 of a minute).
  - `ballAt` moves the ball along a cubic Hermite curve through the keyframes (`smoothPoint`): the velocity through a keyframe is the mean of the hops in and out (no faster than twice the slower), shared by both, so the ball never stops dead at a touch nor turns on the spot; through a strike, a save, a restart or a kick-off each hop keeps its own; each hop adds its sideways bend and lofted hops rise `4·loft·f(1-f)`. It reports the man in control (the carrier through a carry, the receiver as a pass arrives). Over 20 seeded matches the ball is at a man's feet about 42 per cent of the time; over 30 real matches no more than one hop in four matches is faster than 3.5 times the nominal pass speed (`PASS_SPEED`, 46 units a minute; the worst under 8×).
- **The football.** `BallArt` in `LivePitch.tsx`: a white sphere with a radial shading gradient, a gloss overlay and black pentagon patches drawn as a repeating tile inside a circular clip; the loop slides the tile the way the ball travels (55 per cent of the distance, at most 28 per cent of a tile a frame so it never strobes), so it rolls. Radius 1.5 SVG units lying down, 1.75 standing up on a phone, where bigger, fewer patches keep it reading as a football. **In flight** (`src/ui/match/flight.ts`, after Football Manager's 2D view, whose FM26 update 4 notes "ball will now be sized appropriately when the ball is in the air"): each hop is classed by its keyframes (`flightKind`: a carry, a ball fetched for a restart, a pass along the ground, a driven ball (loft up to 0.45), a lofted ball, a floated cross (loft 0.55 and over), a shot) and drawn with a time warp `1 − (1 − f)^k` (`DRAG`: 1.65 along the ground, 1.35 driven, 1.15 lofted, 1.2 a cross, 1.25 a shot, 1 a carry), so a pass is quickest off the boot and slows as it reaches its man while a ball in the air barely slows, always on the path's own point at every keyframe; and a height curve (`flightHeight`: a lofted ball or a cross `4·loft·f(1−f)`, so with the warp it comes down more steeply than it rose; a driven ball low and flat, at most 0.65 of its loft; a shot over the bar rising to the line, a low drive skimming 0.06). The sprite (`ballSprite`) lifts the ball 3.6 radii off its shadow at full height and draws it up to 1.8 times bigger, while the shadow stays on the grass under and a little right of it, shrinks to 55 per cent and fades from 0.5 to 0.18. Only the drawing moves: the dots, the scene and the commentary read the unwarped `ballAt`.
- **Motion.** One `requestAnimationFrame` loop reads the continuous clock (`timeline` in `src/ui/match/clockStore.ts`) and eases everything on screen towards its target, frame-rate independently (`approach`): the ball by 14 ms at 1x (38 at 2x, 85 at 4x, where a hop lasts a few frames and the ball rounds the path's corners instead), the players by a critically damped spring (`follow`, `src/ui/match/follow.ts`, round 9) of 150 ms (up to 300 at 4x) whose speed is capped at `FOLLOW.maxSpeed` 0.045 pitch units a millisecond times the speed played (half for a substitute walking on): a man sets off from the pace he has and gathers speed rather than lurching the frame the ball changes hands, and arrives without overshooting (tested: under three quarters of the old ease's jerk on a real match), so a change of possession, a kick-off, a substitution or a skip never makes anyone jump. Each man's target is the one movement formula the heat maps sample (`dotPosition`, `movement.ts`, on the frame's one `sceneAt`, `shape.ts`), and what is on screen eases towards it, so nobody twitches with each pass. **The shape**: a side's formation slots (its attacking layout, keeper 6 to strikers 86) are squeezed into a block between a back line and a front line that follow the ball along its attacking direction (`block`): in possession the back line at `ab − 30` (18 to 50) and the front at `ab + 22` (58 to 90), so it stretches and climbs, the wide men widen by 10 per cent, the full-backs push 10 further on and wider in the final third, forwards never more than 24 beyond the ball (nor past 91) and the rest never more than 12; out of possession the back line at `ab − 22` (8 to 40) and the front 32 beyond it (38 to 72), narrowed to 72 per cent and slid 30 per cent of the way across to the ball; a keeper comes off his line as his side climbs. A change of possession blends from one shape to the other over half a minute (`settle`, from half-way along the hop that changed hands). The men nearest the ball close it down from the goal side, out of possession (up to 70 per cent of the way, from 20 m), and come short to offer an option in it (never closer than 9 m); everyone sways a little; the man on the ball stands just behind it, the man a pass is meant for runs to meet it, the passer is left where he struck it; a keeper dives at a save. A cross on its way sends the forwards (and one midfielder) into the box and the defenders back to it. **Set pieces**: at a kick-off everyone is in his own half and out of the centre circle but the taker; at a corner the forwards, one centre-back and one midfielder of the attacking side fill the box while the rest wait on the edge and at halfway, and the defending side packs its box but for one striker; at a free kick within 36 units of goal the defending forwards form a wall 9.2 m from the ball between it and the middle of the goal (tested) and the rest mark in the box, while elsewhere the opponents stand ten yards off; at a penalty everyone but the taker and the keepers waits outside the box and the arc; at a goal kick the centre-backs split to the corners of the box, the full-backs go wide and the press waits outside the box. A set piece's shape rises as the ball is fetched and falls once it is taken (`Scene.setPiece.w`). **The caption** over the pitch (`phaseCaption`) names the side on the ball and what it is doing (Building from the back, Through midfield, Switch of play, Final third, Cross, Ball in behind, Counter-attack, Shot, or the set piece: Kick-off, Corner, Free kick, Penalty, Goal kick, Throw-in), set by the loop only when it changes; a faint dashed trail follows a long ball, a switch, a ball in behind, a cross or a clearance while it is in the air; during a goal's celebration the scorer and his team-mates are pulled off it. A substitute walks on from the touchline by the halfway line (eased three times slower for his first 1.5 s). Dots are keyed by side and player-season (`dotKey`), because the same player-season can turn out for both sides when you drafted him from today's opponent: before, one of the two dots stood frozen all match and the other ran as your scorer. Where everything stood on the last frame drawn is kept per match (`frameFor`, the pitch seed), so the next segment (46, 71, the replay) and any re-render of the pitch carry on from there rather than snapping back to the formation; a pitch that starts earlier than the last one (watching again from the kick-off) starts afresh. A new segment that finds the ball far from its kick-off rolls it to the spot over 0.9 s (eased by 160 ms) rather than snapping it there. Time moves things only while the clock runs: stood at a stop or paused, a redraw moves nobody, and the first frame after a sleep takes no time step. Just past a goal's hold the ball is held on the centre spot for the kick-off. React renders only when the whole minute changes, a goal is revealed or a celebration starts (the clock's 40 ms timer sets the store only when one of them changes), never inside the loop; markings and nets are memoised and each dot keeps one ref callback. With prefers-reduced-motion the loop is off: once a minute the dots are drawn where the shape has them at the minute's end and the ball on its last keyframe, with the caption for that moment, and nothing moves in between.
- **Over the play, after Football Manager's 2D view** (`src/ui/match/overlays.ts`, `movement.ts`, drawn in `LivePitch.tsx` by its own helpers: `paintBall`, `paintTag`, `paintPass`, `paintShot`, `NameTag`). The man on the ball has his surname in a dark pill under his dot (`dotName`: the last word, with a particle such as van or De, cut to twelve letters). A pass to a team-mate that moves the play on flashes its line from where it was struck to where it lands, solid on the grass and dashed in the air, fading out over the first 60 per cent of its travel (`passFlash`, `FLASH`). Every shot's line is drawn from the strike to the ball as it flies and fades over 0.7 of a minute, coloured by its expected goals (`shotMarks` matches each shot keyframe to its event by the strike moment; `xgBand`: white under 0.08, amber `--pitch-cover` to 0.25, red `--pitch-bad` from 0.25, the same bands as the highlight levels); a goal's line follows the ball into the net. A corner names its delivery in the caption while it flies ("Corner · near post", far post, six-yard box or edge of the box, `cornerDelivery`). **Runs off the ball** (`runTo`, `RUN`): the man a pass is for sets off towards where it will land in the last 0.22 of a minute of the passer's touch (about a second at 1x), by the time it is struck a share of the way there (a ball in behind 0.5, a cross 0.42, a long ball or a switch 0.38, a forward pass 0.28, anything else 0.15), and runs the rest of the way through the pass to meet it where it lands rather than waiting at his spot; the weight is continuous through the strike (tested). The back line out of possession sways across but holds its depth (85 per cent less sway along the pitch once settled), so it reads as a line. An optional `highlightId` rings one man in `--pitch-highlight` and keeps his name tag on (the hook for the Squad strip's selection). The pass lines and the in-play camera run up to 2x (the speed times the highlights' fast-forward): quicker, a pass is over before its line reads, and a zooming camera repaints the whole pitch every frame (measured: about a fifth more main-thread work at 4x before this limit, the same as before the overlays with it). A device that cannot keep up turns them off while it struggles (`liteMode` in `LivePitch.tsx`: a running mean of the frame time over about thirty frames, on above 40 ms after 90 frames, off again under 26). The loop writes an SVG attribute only when its value changes (`put`).
- **The build-up** (`buildUpAt`, `shooterRing`, `BUILD_UP` in `keyMoments.ts`), at every speed and whatever the Key moments setting: from 1.8 s of real time before the strike of a goal or a chance or save of 0.25 xG or more, the goal mouth under attack brightens (a white glow from the goal line, up to 0.3 opacity) and a dashed white ring closes on the shooter (from 2.4 times his dot's size to his dot, fading in), the last ball to him shows its pass line even at 3x and 4x, and his dot is drawn again above everyone (an SVG `use`) so the men closing him down never hide him as he strikes it; all of it is gone 0.45 s after the strike, off during a goal's hold and its replay, on a struggling device (`liteMode`) and with reduced motion.
- **Key moments** (`src/ui/match/keyMoments.ts`; the per-viewer setting `touchline-key-moments`, `keyMomentsPref` in `viewerPrefs.ts`, on by default, the clapperboard button beside the highlight level; off with reduced motion). The camera leans in on every goal and every chance or save of 0.25 xG or more (`focusMoments`, `focusAt`): from 1.5 s of real time before the strike it scales the pitch up to 1.28 (three quarters of that lean at 2x, none quicker) on a point between the ball and the shot, holds through a goal's celebration and eases out over 1.1 s, always inside the pitch (`cameraTransform`; the turf and the lines are drawn inside the camera's group, `PitchSvg`'s optional `camera`). Every goal's hold gets 3,900 ms more, a penalty's 2,000 (`goalHolds(..., { replay: true })`, `GoalHold.replay`, `replayLength`: the ball path stands the taker over the ball until the strike, so a longer look shows nothing), and once the celebration is over the pitch replays the goal: a 240 ms cut to dark, the last 0.42 of a match minute before the strike played back over 2.8 s (about 0.65x of 1x; a penalty's window in proportion, `replayWindow`), opening a little quicker than that and slowing into the strike to about two thirds of it (`replayEase`, `REPLAY_RAMP` 0.35, round 9) from the same ball path and the same shapes, zoomed to 1.6 on the ball, then the ball into the net over 760 ms and the net's bulge, a REPLAY badge with Skip (`skipHold` re-anchors the clock just past the hold), and a cut back to the kick-off (`replayFrame`). The replay reads only the path and the time, so a second viewing of the match shows the same replay; the quick-season highlights reel gets the camera but no replay (its clock never holds).
- **The clock** (`useMatchClock.ts`, `clockStore.ts`). The dashboard owns a continuous anchor (`at` minute at real time `since`, speed, period, the goal holds) and the pitch and the scoreboard clock read a fractional minute from it every frame (`timeline`), so the ball never waits on a late timer at a minute boundary; a 40 ms timer only refreshes the whole minute React shows, the goals revealed and the celebration on. A stretch ends at the end of its stop minute (46, 71, or the minute after the last of stoppage time). 1x is a console match's pace: a match minute takes 4,500 ms (`PITCH_MS` in `timing.ts`, `PITCH_PERIOD`; 110 ms for the bare ticker; 3,000 before the build-up work), so a 20-metre pass rolls for about two seconds, a man takes a touch and looks up before he passes, a corner stands about three seconds while the box fills, and a half takes about three and a half minutes plus the goals' celebrations; 0.5x takes 9 s a minute, 2x 2.25 s, 3x 1.5 s and 4x about 1.1 s. **Cues**: every strike and every foul behind a card is a cue (`cueTimes`), kept on the clock store (`cues`, `cued` counted by `readout`), and while a cue in or before the minute on the clock is still to come (`cuePending`) the panels, the commentary and the stats read the minute before, as they do for a goal until it is over the line: a chance, a save or a booking reaches the dashboard as the pitch shows it, not at the start of its minute. Pause, resume and speed changes re-anchor where the picture stands (`reanchor`), so nothing jumps at any speed; "Skip to half time", "Skip to 70 minutes" and "Skip to full time" jump the anchor to the end. The speed is a per-viewer convenience kept in `localStorage` under `touchline-match-speed` (read inside try/catch, 1x when missing or unreadable). When the tab is hidden the clock pauses (it runs on real time) and resumes when the tab is back, unless you had paused it yourself. A change in play re-decides the rest of the match: the new goal holds and whistle are taken in without moving the picture.
- **The goal.** When the clock reaches a goal's strike moment it holds (`goalHolds`): 2,800 ms for the user's goal, 1,700 ms for one against him, 2,600 when neither side is the user's, 1,500 ms with reduced motion. In real time during the hold: the ball flies from the shooter's foot into the net (520 ms, `GOAL_FLIGHT_MS`) the way the goal's line says it went in (`finishOf`, `goalFlightAt` in `finish.ts`: a drive into the roof rises all the way to the net, a chip loops high and drops in, a curler bends and dips towards the top corner, a volley goes in high, a finish low into the corner skims the grass inside the post, a tap-in, a pounce or a ball rolled past a rounded keeper stays on the grass, a header goes in low, a penalty goes low or high into a corner by a hash of the minute and side, the same on every viewing), and a ball that lands high drops to the floor of the net over 200 ms; as it crosses the line the goal reaches the dashboard (the panels, the commentary and the scoreboard read the minute before the goal's until then, and the board flashes while it is celebrated: nothing shows the goal before the ball goes in), the net bulges with a damped wobble (`netBulge`), the pitch flashes white (0.42 opacity decaying over 170 ms, 0.16 for a goal against) and, for a goal of ours, shakes for 380 ms; the "GOAL!" banner over the pitch (GOAL! (PEN) and OWN GOAL! with the defender's name and "(og)") bursts out with the scorer, the minute and the new score in the scoring club's colour, with 42 pieces of confetti in that colour, white and a contrast colour; the scorer runs towards the corner flag with a pulsing ring in his colour and his team-mates converge on him (`celebrationEnvelope` rises over half a second and is back to nothing 150 ms before the end, so nobody jumps at the restart); the ball fades out of the net and back in on the centre spot, and play resumes with the kick-off. A goal against is short and subdued: a smaller, dimmer banner, a smaller bulge and flash, no shake and no confetti. Reduced motion: no flight, no shake, no confetti, a still banner for the 1,500 ms hold. All of it is presentation of events the engine already produced; nothing draws from any game generator.
- **Charts.** The momentum bar draws each bin up (home) or down (away) as the clock reaches it, with goal markers. The xG graph story steps each side's expected goals up at every attempt (per-attempt xG, "Match day data" below), with a marker on every goal. (The older xG race, `xgSeries`, shared the match xG over the ninety minutes by the momentum; it is kept with its tests but no longer drawn.) Territory on the report is the mean of the momentum series. The match card (`renderMatchCard` in `src/ui/shareCard.ts`) draws the score, the shot map and the xG story on a 1080 by 1350 canvas, its line on a 0..90 axis with stoppage-time attempts on the ninetieth minute.

### The match-day dashboard

The live match and the replay of a finished one play on one dashboard (`src/ui/matchday/`), built after Football Manager's match screen. The shell (`MatchDashboard.tsx`) builds the match feed (`feedFromLive` / `feedFromResult`) and, once per decision rather than per tick, everything derived from it: the epochs and ball path (`matchPath`), the stats series, the sampled dot positions for the maps, the xG story and the commentary. Per minute it works out the ratings, the round's other scores and the live table. Nothing here draws from the game's generator.

- **The clock** runs from the start of the segment the engine has decided (0, 46 or 71) to the stop (45, 70) or to the whistle (90 plus the added time), as described under "Match detail and the pitch views", with the goal holds of every spell. A reload resumes at the later of the segment's start and the manager's last change in play, so nothing shown before a change is played again with a different ending; since round 5 it resumes where the tab's clock had reached in that segment (`src/ui/matchday/resume.ts`: `sessionStorage` `touchline-match-resume`, keyed by game, match, segment, start and events, so a change in play is a new segment), or at the stop if it was reached, with a toast saying where ("Back at 31', where you left the match"). Display only: the save and the engine are untouched. At 70 "Play to the whistle" holds the last segment live (`advanceLive(changes, true)`); the whistle calls `completeLive()`, then the report shows. A cup tie that follows a league match is held back until the league report has been seen.
- **Layout at 1200 px and wider**: a slim line naming the competition and round (a replay's Live and Report tabs and Continue at its end); the top bar (the scoreboard with club codes on club colours, the score at the clock, the minute, "(+n)" once the board is up, HALF TIME, 70 MINUTES and FULL TIME at the stops and the whistle, a flash for a goal; Pause or Resume, 0.5x 1x 2x 3x 4x and "Skip to half time", "Skip to 70 minutes" or "Skip to full time"; in a live match also "Auto from here" and, while a stop is still to come, "Sim result"); three columns, `minmax(240px, 0.9fr) minmax(0, 2.6fr) minmax(240px, 0.9fr)`. Each side column holds a formation panel on top and a widget slot below that scrolls inside itself (L, R). The centre holds three widget slots (A, B, C), the live pitch flanked by both sides' stats and the momentum strip. Under them run the commentary banner and the squad strip. The board fits the window with no page scroll from 1280 by 720 to 2560 by 1440: the shell measures what stands above and below it and sets its height to `calc(100dvh − that)` (`--md-fit`, again on resize), the widget row takes `clamp(112px, 19vh, 236px)`, the strip, banner and top bar keep their size, and the pitch row takes the rest, the pitch sized by whichever of the row's width and height runs out first (a size container: `min(100cqw, 100cqh × 111 / 74)`). The side columns share that height: each formation pitch is sized by its room and the widget slot under it takes 40 per cent. In a short window (940 px high or less, 1280 by 720 and 2000 by 920 among them) the momentum strip gives its room to the pitch (it stays a widget), the widget row drops to `clamp(96px, 15vh, 160px)` and the squad cards slim down. Panels scroll inside themselves; the page never does, and the stop's pop-up and the substitutions sheet stay inside the window (checked at 1280 by 720, 1920 by 1080, 2000 by 980, 2000 by 920 and 2560 by 1440). At a stop the half-time or 70-minute decisions open as a pop-up over the board (see "Watch, Auto-play and Sim result"); the widget row stays.
- **Formation panels**: each XI on its own pitch standing up, attacking up for both sides, at its slots' points under the instructions in force: a shirt in club colours (the keeper in a contrasting kit) with the man's number, his live rating, the role code and surname; card marks, a sub arrow on a changed slot, and a greyed slot for a man sent off or injured with nobody to replace him. Outfield men stand no deeper than 80 per cent of the panel (`PANEL_DEEPEST`), so a back three on a deep line never sits on the keeper's shirt, and each name is capped at the gap to the nearest team-mate level with him (`labelRoom`), so two centre-halves' names never run into each other at 1280 px. Clicking a man (or his card in the strip) puts him in the heat map.
- **Stats flanks**: shots, on target, xG, distance covered, pass completion, corners, tackles, fouls, yellow cards and possession at the clock (`statsAt`), with "View stats" opening the full table in slot B.
- **Widgets**: every slot's header is a dropdown of every widget, remembered per browser under `touchline-matchday-widgets` (validated against the ids, never in the save). Defaults: A heat map, B match events, C xG graph story, L live table, R latest scores; a friendly puts matchday info in L and the dugout in R. Match events lists goals with assists, cards, injuries, substitutions, the whistles and every change of instructions, home left and away right. Commentary is the full feed to the clock. The heat map follows one man (both XIs and the substitutes in a picker) with a Low to High legend, attacking up. Average positions shows both sides or either. Pass maps join one side's men at their average positions, weighted by the passes between them on the ball path. Action zones gives one side's share of the ball by third and channel. The shot map sizes each attempt by its xG. Match momentum names the spells of two or more bins with one side at 60 per cent or more. Match stats adds passes attempted and completed, offsides, red cards and territory. Latest scores shows the round's other matches with their scores, the minute, HT or FT, the last scorer and penalties at FT in a knockout. The live table counts every match of the round at its current score, with movement arrows against the table before the round and a dot on the sides playing (a knockout tie shows the league table as it stands; a friendly has none). The dugout lists the bench (ready, on, unavailable), the changes made, subs and stoppages left, the talk and shout as they landed and the assistant's notes. Matchday info gives the competition and round, the venue, the expected goals before kick-off, both sides' unit ratings and their positions and form before the round, an earlier meeting this season and our captain.
- **The commentary banner** shows the latest line by priority (a goal over a card over an injury, a substitution, a change, a goal elsewhere, a chance, the whistles, the board and a line of play); the moments slide in and are announced politely to screen readers, the lines of play change quietly.
- **The assistant's own changes** (`src/ui/match/assistantSubs.ts`, display only). The engine still makes the routine and injury substitutions for the user's side between the stops (reasons `tired`, `tactical`, `injury` in the side's substitution list, as for the AI side); the commentary words each as "Your assistant brings on X for Y." ("for the injured Y") and the dashboard toasts it when the clock reaches its minute (several at once after a skip read as one toast). The manager's own changes (half time, the 70th minute, a change in play) keep their text. Nothing here decides when or whether a change happens.
- **The squad strip**: our XI by starting slot with the position code in the game's own words (GK, CB, RB, DM, CM, AM, LW, ST and the rest, as the Draft and every player card print them; Football Manager's DC, MC, AML, STC until round 10), the shirt and number, the surname, a condition heart that empties as the legs go (live only: a replay has no starting condition), the rating pill, a morale face (live only), card marks and a sub arrow with the minute. The Squad control before it counts subs left of five and, in play, stoppages left of three, and opens the substitutions drawer. Tapping a card puts the man in the heat map (and offers him first when making a change).
- **Under 1200 px** (`CompactLayout.tsx`, `PhoneTabs.tsx`): the pitch first (standing up on a phone at 720 px and narrower, lying down on a tablet, capped at 860 px wide (1120 px on a landscape tablet from 1000 px) and sized from its measured top on the page (`--md-above`) so the pitch, its legend and the commentary line fit the window), the commentary banner, a pulse row that is always on (the momentum strip and the xG graph story), then a tab strip (class `md-tabs`, a proper tablist: arrow keys, Home and End) with every panel: Events, Stats, Line-ups, Table, Scores, Heat map, Avg positions, Pass map, Shot map, Zones, Momentum, Dugout, Info and Commentary (a friendly has no Table or Scores). The tab is remembered per browser under `touchline-matchday-tab`, read and written inside try/catch and validated. A tablet shows the chosen panel and the one after it side by side. Our XI runs along the bottom as a scroll-snap sideways scroller; every sideways scroller sits in a box that may shrink, so the page itself never scrolls sideways. The top bar puts the scoreboard on its own row; the tools and playback wrap under it on a tablet. On a phone the scoreboard is the top bar's last row, just over the pitch, and the bar sticks under the app's bar with only the scoreboard showing (`--md-stick`), so the score and the clock stay in view while the page scrolls; the stop pop-up lists the substitutions straight after the talk or the shout (on a tablet they sit under the talk, beside the instructions). The page scrolls on a phone; the stop's pop-up is centred in the window all the same, so it is in view without scrolling.
- **States**: a friendly (no table, no other scores), a cup tie (the league table frozen, the round's other ties), an old save (attempts without positions worth 0.31 each, no timeline lines), the replay (a "Replay" chip; everything moves the same). With prefers-reduced-motion nothing slides or flashes; the numbers still follow the clock.
- **Sound** (`src/ui/sound/match.ts`, `useMatchSounds.ts`; the palette in `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` section 14): the referee's whistle at a kick-off from 0 or 46, at half time and at full time (none at the 70-minute stop); a goal's net and roar as the ball crosses the line in its hold and again, smaller, at the replay's net; the crowd's "ooh" at a chance of 0.08 xG or more and the gloves at a save, at their strike times; the whistle and the card at a booking's foul; a chime at a substitution. Read every frame off the same clock as the pitch, so a pause, a speed, the highlights' fast-forward or a skip moves them with the picture. Presentation only, under the Sound and Match sounds switches **The mix** (`src/ui/sound/mix.ts`, pure, shared by `useMatchSounds` and `useBallSounds` as `matchMix`): the crowd's bed (a new one every 3.6 s) takes its level from `crowdMood(path, t)`, 0.1 to 1: a floor of 0.3, up to 0.25 more as the ball goes deep into a half, 0.5 after a goal falling away over about 2.5 minutes, 0.22 after a save or a miss over 0.8, and from 70' up to 0.2 more in a level or one-goal game; `bedLevel` maps it to 40 to 100 per cent of the screen's bed, 80 per cent of that at 3x and up. `admit(state, kind, now, speed)` decides every match sound: a whistle and a goal always play; a goal ducks everything else (and hushes the bed, which comes back roused) for `MIX.goalDuck` 3.2 s; a chance or a save keeps 350 ms from the last (900 ms at 3x and up); a swell keeps 1.6 s clear of them and is off at 3x and up; and the ball's ordinary touches and the swells give way once a real second holds five sounds (three at 3x and up), so nothing stacks at 4x (tested over six matches at 1x, 2x and 4x). The ball's levels sit about a quarter under round 8's, and a goal for the user's side or a neutral one adds a low boom under the net and a fuller roar.

### In-play changes

The manager can change the match at any minute, not only at the two stops. A literal split of a segment cannot keep a seed's match identical: `playSegment` draws each segment as totals (one Poisson goal count spread over the segment, one card and injury roll per player scaled by its length, at most four chances, the routine substitutions drawn once at 46, one detail fork), so stopping 46-70 at 58 would draw everything twice. Instead the engine decides ahead and cuts on change (`changeInPlay` in `src/engine/match.ts`):

- **Decide ahead.** The three canonical segments are played exactly as before, ahead of the clock. The dashboard's clock can stand anywhere; an untouched match never calls anything new, so it is identical to `simulateMatch` by construction, and a change that changes nothing is refused (`no-change`) before the match is touched. `ENGINE_VERSION` did not move for this: built on `'3'` it stayed `'3'`, and merged onto the depth attributes it stayed `'4'`, since no existing draw moved (the 2,000-match window baseline, the detail pin, `{ goals: 404, chances: 774, state }` on `'3'` and `{ goals: 432, chances: 769, state }` on `'4'`, and the 40-seed stop identity test are unchanged, and a new test checks 40 seeds × ten minutes through JSON round trips).
- **The late fade and a cut.** The depth attributes' late fade (`applyLateFade`, the starters' legs going after 70 by stamina) is applied once, when `advanceMatch` plays 71-90: it cuts the effective ratings of the men on at 70 and moves both sides' expected goals for the segment. A full-detail match stores each side's fade on its snapshot (`TeamSnapshot.lateFadeAt70`, optional); a cut in 71-89 re-applies that stored value to the replay's expected goals (after the shouts, in the same order as the canonical call) and never cuts the legs again, so a man on since kick-off keeps the effective rating the fade left him however many changes follow (tested over 20 seeds and three cuts each). A man brought on in a cut is fresh, as one brought on at 70 is. A match held at 90 from before the field existed falls back to the fade computed on the pitch as it stands.
- **Cut on change at minute k.** The segment in play is rolled back to the end of minute k: every event after k is removed and undone (goals, assists, saves, cards, injuries, substitutions), except the half-time whistle and a substitution replacing a man whose injury at or before k stands; a routine change removed goes back into the parked changes at its minute. The manager's change is applied at k (instructions and set-piece takers; substitutions at k with reason `manager` and the text "Change on k minutes: X replaces Y."; a shout from 70), strengths, modifiers, marking and expected goals are recomputed by the same helper the stops use (with the shouts in force re-applied after 70), and k + 1 to the segment's end is replayed from `Rng.fromState(mp.rngState).fork('cut-<k>')`. The match's own stream never moves, so the next segment and the report draw what they would have; the momentum keeps every bin up to the one holding k; a first-half cut rewrites the half-time score. A cut is close to a fresh segment of the remaining length (Poisson additivity for goals, card and injury rates pro rata, the four-chance cap and a fifth of the last stretch's goals in stoppage time applying again; injuries fall in minutes 3 to 88 as always, so a cut at 88 or 89 replays none rather than landing one at or before the change), and it only touches matches the manager touched. The same change at the same minute always gives the same match.
- **Rules.** Full-detail matches only (`summary-match`); a save made before in-play changes has no `segment` and the change waits for the next stop (`no-segment`, with `nextStop`); no changes in stoppage time (`too-late`, minute 90 on); a minute outside the segment in play, the stops 45 and 70 included, belongs to `advanceMatch` (`outside-segment`); a minute before the side's last change is `in-the-past`. A shout before 70 is refused on its own (`shoutRefused`) while instructions with it still apply. A substitution is refused at minute 0 (`before-kick-off`: the team is picked before the match, so the starters stay the eleven who kick off), when the man coming off is not on the pitch at k or came on that same minute, the man coming on has already played or is injured or suspended, the side has made five changes (`MAX_SUBS`; every change counts, routine, injury and the manager's) or the manager has used his three windows (`MAX_WINDOWS`: a window is a minute other than 45 at which he made at least one change, the 70 stop included; half time is free; more changes at the same minute spend no new window). The instructions of a change apply even when its substitutions are refused. A manager's substitution drops his side's parked routine changes, as at 70. The AI reacts only at 45 and 70, never to an in-play change.
- **The last twenty minutes.** `advanceLiveMatch(game, u, changes, autoManage, hold)` with `hold` plays 71-90 into the paused match and keeps `game.live` (clock 90) so the manager can still change it; `completeLiveMatch` writes the report and carries the week on. Held and then completed, the game ends JSON-identical to the unheld path. `changeLiveMatch` makes a change for the user's side and writes the substitutions made into the lineup and bench and the instructions into `game.tactics`; the store actions are `advanceLive(changes, hold)`, `interveneLive(change)` and `completeLive()`. The match-day dashboard holds it: the 70th-minute panel plays on with `hold` and the whistle completes the match.
- **What is kept.** Full-detail matches carry `segment` (the canonical segment last played and the index of its first event), `timeline` (kick-off instructions for both sides and every change since: the manager's at the stops and in play, the AI's at the stops, with only the fields that changed, set-piece takers, the shout and the number of substitutions) and `seedKey` (read off the match stream at half time, never drawn), and the result keeps `timeline`, `seedKey` and `neutral` (when true). The result's timeline is compacted (`compactTimeline`): a side's kick-off entry keeps only the instructions a later entry changes, and goes when nothing is left, since the final tactics hold the rest; the match feed reads it back whole. `TeamSnapshot.shout` holds the shout in force from 71 so a later cut plays on under it. The timeline and the key come to well under half a kilobyte a result (about 250 bytes over 60 seeded matches, never 512); summary matches carry none of it. A whole season's save grows by about 0.2 per cent (11 KB on 5.8 MB in a 38-matchweek probe).

### In-play controls (the manager's side)

The top bar and the squad strip are the manager's hands on the match (`TopBar.tsx`, `drawers/Drawers.tsx`, `SquadStrip.tsx`, the pure helpers in `controls.ts`, the shared state in `useManagerHand.ts`).

- **The top bar.** On the left Tactics (the shape, each slot's position code, role and man, and the man-marking job: read-only, since formation and roles cannot change mid-match, the lineup being keyed by slot), Instructions (tempo, width and passing in possession; pressing and the defensive line out of it, with the usual help lines), a Mentality dropdown that applies the moment it changes, Shouts (all-out attack, shut up shop, keep shape: from the 70th minute only, "Touchline shouts open at 70 minutes" before) and Tweaks (penalty, free-kick and corner takers among the men on the pitch, or the best on the pitch). In the middle the scoreboard with a running mm:ss clock (the t-th minute plus its fraction reads (t − 1):ss, "44:07"; "(+n)" once the board is up; HALF TIME, 70 MINUTES, FULL TIME). On the right the playback.
- **Where a change lands** (the chip under the tools): "Changes apply now" (in play: one `interveneLive` at the clock's minute, applying from the next); "At the break: changes apply when you play on" and "At 70 minutes: …" (at a stop the drawers edit the stop panel's own pending changes, which the panel then sends); "Changes wait for half time" or "for the 70th minute" (a save from before changes in play, or a summary match: the change is queued into the next stop's pending changes and shows there when the stop comes); "Too late for changes" (stoppage time); "Replay" (a finished match: every control but Tactics is off).
- **Drawers.** Opening one pauses the clock and closing it resumes it if it was running (switching between drawers keeps the first one's choice). Apply sends everything changed as one change and closes. A drawer takes the focus, keeps Tab inside, closes on Escape or a click outside and gives the focus back to the control that opened it; on a phone it is a bottom sheet.
- **Substitutions.** The Squad control (and the dugout's "Make a change") opens the substitutions sheet, a substitution board (`drawers/SubBoard.tsx`, its pure model in `src/ui/matchday/subBoard.ts`): our XI on a small pitch in its formation slots (shirt number, surname, role code, condition) and the bench men who can still come on (`benchAt`) as cards beside it, under it on a phone, where the sheet is full height. Drag a bench card onto a starter (or a starter onto a bench card) to stage a change, with pointer events so a mouse, a pen and a finger all work; or tap one and then the other (the man selected on the strip starts picked); or use the keyboard (Enter or Space picks up, the arrows move to the nearest slot that way, or along the bench, Enter drops, Escape lets go), each step announced politely. While a man is held every slot glows for how he fits it, the slot picker's own split: green for his natural position, amber for accomplished cover (`canCover`), red for out of position, with his `roleRating` in that slot's role on it. Dropping a man on one already staged swaps the incoming man without spending another change; a man staged cannot be staged twice, and nobody who has played comes back on. The staged changes list as "on ↔ off" with Undo, and the board previews the team rating (`computeTeamStrength` of the lineup after them, the men's live condition read in) and, over a slot, both men's role ratings there; the numbers show in blind mode too, since these are the user's own men (`ratingShown(rules, 'squad')`). The sheet stages at most what the window takes (`subCap`: the subs left in play, the stop's room at 45 and 70) and nothing in play once the three stoppages are spent, unless a change was already made at that minute (its window is open); it shows the counters after the staged changes. "Make changes" sends the staged list, in one stoppage, through the same `submit` as before (`interveneLive` in play, the stop's pending changes at a stop), so the engine's rules and refusals are untouched. The counters are the engine's: subs left `MAX_SUBS − subsMade` up to the clock (every change counts, the assistant's routine ones and injury replacements too, each from its minute: a routine change the engine has decided for the 62nd minute is not spent at 50, because a cut at 50 would roll it back; the strip and the drawer count the same ones), stoppages left `MAX_WINDOWS − managerWindowsUsed` (a minute other than 45 at which the manager made a change; half time is free, up to three changes at the break as before). A fourth stoppage, a sixth change, a man who has already played, a substitution at kick-off and the rest of the engine's refusals come back as a toast naming the reason; a change made is confirmed with a toast and shows on the pitch, in the strip and in Match events from its minute.

### Match day data

Everything the match-day dashboard shows is derived from one feed (`feedFromLive` / `feedFromResult`, `src/engine/matchFeed.ts`: the decided events sorted, the momentum, the timeline, both sides with everyone who has appeared, and the key) without touching any generator; anything random comes from a stream keyed by the match and the minute.

- **Added time.** Two minutes, plus one for every three goals, cards, injuries and substitutions in the second half, at most six, and always enough to reach the last event; the first half has none. The full-time whistle in the feed stands at 90 plus that.
- **Tactics at a minute.** Formation and roles as the side stands; instructions and set-piece takers from the kick-off entry and every later entry before the minute (an entry at k applies from k + 1). A result without a timeline reads its final tactics throughout.
- **Shots and xG** (`src/engine/matchStats.ts`). Shots are the side's goal, penalty, chance and save events; on target goal, penalty and save; own goals count for the score, never as a shot. xG per attempt: a penalty 0.76, an own goal 0, an attempt without a position (an older save) 0.31, otherwise `min(0.92, 1.55 × q0)` with `q0 = 1 / (1 + exp(1.1 + 0.11 d − 1.6 θ))` from the distance d in metres and the angle θ the goal mouth subtends, × 0.65 for a header. The constant is fitted on 400 seeded full matches (seed `xg-calibration`): 1.545, pinned at 1.55, gives 1.870 xG a side against 1.865 goals (own goals aside) and 1.892 pre-match expected goals. A 600-match probe found 5.8 attempts and 3.5 on target a side, 53 per cent of attempts close in, 28 in the box, 16 outside and 3 penalties; sharing each segment's expected goals over its attempts was rejected because 37.7 per cent of side-segments log none.
- **Possession and territory.** The base is the engine's possession formula without its noise: `clamp(50 + (home midfield − away midfield) × 1.1 + passing (short +4, direct −4, home minus away) + 2 at home, 28, 72)`. The home share of minute m is `clamp(base + 35 × (momentum − 0.5), 20, 80)`, and possession to t is the mean over minutes 1..t. Territory to t is the mean momentum over the minutes played, so at the whistle it is the report's territory.
- **Modelled per minute** from `new Rng('stats-<key>-<minute>')`, home then away: passes `Poisson(10.4 × share × tempo (slow 1.12, fast 0.9) × passing (short 1.15, direct 0.8))`, each completed with `clamp(0.8 + (mean outfield passing − 72) × 0.006 + short 0.04 / direct −0.07 + slow 0.015 / fast −0.025 − their high press 0.03 / low −0.02, 0.55, 0.94)`; tackles `Poisson(0.2 × opponent share / 0.5 × press (high 1.3, low 0.78) × defending factor)`; fouls one per card and per penalty given away plus `Poisson(0.1 × press (high 1.2, low 0.85) × aggression × 1.15 in a derby × √(opponent share / 0.5))`; corners one per attempt from a corner plus `Poisson(0.05 × (pressure / 0.5)^1.5 × mentality × width)`; offsides `Poisson(0.022 × their line (high 1.6, deep 0.6) × tempo × passing × pressure / 0.5)`; distance 0.055 km a minute for the keeper and `0.119 × work rate × press × tempo × late fade × (1 + N(0, 0.03))` for each outfield man (stamina, work rate and aggression come from the data when it has them, else from physical, mentality and professionalism). A man coming on at k plays from k + 1 and the man he replaced plays minute k, so a minute's numbers depend only on that minute and a change at k leaves every minute up to k as it was shown. Over 200 even seeded matches a side averages about 520 passes at 79 per cent, 16 tackles, 12 fouls, 5 corners, 2.2 offsides and 104 km. The foul and distance bases were 0.115 and 0.113 while the data had no depth attributes (the proxies stood in); with the real aggression, work rate and stamina of format 3 packs (and engine `'4'`'s extra cards) they read 13.9 fouls and 99 km, so both were retuned on the merge to keep the per-ninety ranges. These numbers come from keyed streams of their own and never touch a match's draws.
- **Ratings and condition** (`src/engine/liveRatings.ts`). In play, the engine's rating formula on the events so far without its noise (the clean-sheet bonus pro rata, a substitute under thirty minutes damped); over 200 matches the whistle's live figures sit 0.24 on average from the engine's. A replay leans each man towards his final rating by his share of his own minutes, so at his last minute it is exactly the report's. Condition is the starting condition less the engine's condition loss pro rata, equal to it at the whistle.
- **The rest of the round** (`src/game/logic/matchday.ts`). The other fixtures are decided before the user's match is shown; `latestScores` reveals each by its own goal minutes at the user's clock (HT while the user's match stands at the break, everything and penalties once it reaches its whistle) and `liveTable` counts every match of the round from its first minute at its current score for points and goal difference, the user's at his, with arrows against the table before the round; P counts a game once it ends (round 5: at 1' the round read P 1), the playing dot marks who is on. A knockout round shows its other ties and the league table as it stands; a friendly has neither.
- **The pitch's spells** (`src/ui/match/epochs.ts`). One placement per spell of unchanged instructions (kick-off, 46, 71 and the minute after every change) and one ball path through them, each spell's ball carrying on from where the last left it (the second half kicks off from the centre spot). Heat maps, average positions, pass networks and action zones (`heat.ts`) sample the same path through the same dot formula the live pitch draws (`movement.ts`). The commentary (`commentary.ts`) uses every event's text, the timeline's changes in words, goals elsewhere, the added-time board and a line of play for each quiet minute read off the ball path from half a minute before the minute to a fifth into it (`PLAY_LOOKBACK`, `PLAY_LOOKAHEAD`), so it describes what the pitch has just shown, and only what came after a shot in that window: a set piece first (a corner, a free kick after a foul on a named man, a throw-in, a goal kick), then a switch, a ball in behind, a cross headed clear, a claim, the press, a tackle, an interception, a long ball, a dribble, a keeper's ball out, a ball back (`PLAY_LINES`, `PLAY_RANK`), else who found whom and where; its template picked by a hash of the key and the minute. Shirt numbers (`shirts.ts`) are classic by position (keeper 1, right back 2, the first striker 9, the bench from 12), roles carry short codes of up to three letters (CF, DLP, BPD) and slots Football Manager position codes (GK, DC, WBL, DM, MC, AMC, ML, AML, STC); a keeper wears the first of gold, purple, black and orange that stands apart from both kits.
- **The commentary's colour** (`src/ui/match/commentaryColour.ts`, display only). Every event keeps its own text first (a goal with the new score) and may gain one short sentence, read off the match so far and nothing later (so a line written at its minute reads the same once the match is known): a goal by its story first (four for one man, a hat-trick, a comeback from two down to level or ahead, a late winner or leveller from the 85th minute, stoppage time, a brace), otherwise by one of a few angles turned by the hash (the situation: an early or ordinary opener, an equaliser, back in front, two clear, a rout, a goal back, a consolation; its type read off the engine's words: a header, from distance, a volley, a tap-in, a chip, a team move, round the keeper, a scramble, a penalty, an own goal; a derby; the scorer's season goals and form); chances and saves two times in three (the woodwork, the keeper, the line, a miss, or a word for the score from the 75th minute); every red (a second yellow, straight, nine men) and half the yellows (early, tactical late on while ahead, or plain); changes (chasing, holding on, at the break, or routine half the time; an injury's replacement says enough); every injury; half time and full time (a comeback, a late winner, a derby win, a thrashing, a clean sheet, a goal fest, the plain result, a goalless or score draw). After `Kick-off.` a line names the occasion (from the feed's importance label: a derby, a title decider, a final, a semi-final, a European night, a cup tie) or the table when it tells a story (top against the bottom three, a six-pointer, the leaders), and about one untagged quiet minute in four (never two within `MOOD_GAP = 8` minutes, never over a set piece or a tagged play) reads the mood instead: cagey early, still goalless, a second-half start, nervy late with one goal in it, level late, cruising, ten men, and since content round 2 (r13) a two-goal cushion from the 55th minute and an open game (level with two goals or more, minutes 20 to 79); round 2 also gave every mood bank two or three more wordings (the moods now 61 lines, `MOOD_LINES`, mirrored as `mood_*` in the `commentaryText` catalogue). The new moods only fill quiet minutes `commentaryUpTo` already offered the mood (the one-in-four chance and `MOOD_GAP`), so no new kind of line appears and the event text, the corner lines and "never twice" are untouched; the English of a seeded match moved only in those minutes (`commentaryMirror.test.ts`'s hash re-baselined once for it). The dashboard passes `commentaryContextFor(game, home, away, liveLeague)`: the table's places (once five rounds are in) and our men's season goals and recent rating average, only for a live league match, whose squad stats still hold the pre-match numbers. Every line is picked from a hash of the match key, the minute and the event (`pickFresh`, which is `pickVariant` of `src/game/logic/flavour.ts` on its own); nothing draws from a generator. **No line twice in a match** (round 9): `commentaryUpTo` keeps the colour lines said so far in event order and the quiet minutes' lines in minute order (each a prefix of the match, so a line once shown never changes as the clock runs on); `pickFresh` walks from the hashed line to the first one not yet said, a spent bank says nothing rather than repeat (a goal falls back to its type, then a word on the finish), and an empty quiet minute falls through added time's own lines, a lull, and the other side keeping the ball (tested over 124 matches: no colour sentence and no line of play said twice). About 380 colour lines in 12 banks (and seven season-goal lines), with about 75 more quiet-minute lines in `commentary.ts` (round 9 widened every bank) (`PLAY_LINES` and the pass, keeper, turnover and quiet lists, every corner line still starting "Corner to"). Tested: every bank is reached over 400 keys, the same input reads the same, the event text stays first and a line never changes with what comes after it.

## 9. Tactics and instructions

On the Tactics tab each shape is a tile with its mini pitch (`FormationPicker`), each instruction is a label and its control with what the current setting does behind an info button, and each role row shows the rating in role as a badge with the role's blurb behind an info button.

Six instructions plus a role per slot (`src/engine/tactics.ts`). `attack` multiplies your λ; `defence` multiplies the opponent's λ against you (lower is better); `fatigue` is extra condition lost per 90.

| Instruction | Setting | Attack | Defence | Fatigue | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mentality | Attacking | ×1.12 | ×1.10 | | |
| | Defensive | ×0.86 | ×0.86 | | |
| Pressing | High | ×(1.05 + edge + bite + 0.1 × wr) | ×(1.03 − edge / 2 − 0.05 × wr) | 7 at outfield stamina ≤ 60 down to 4 at ≥ 80 | `edge = clamp((78 − opponent composure) / 100, −0.06, 0.08)`; `bite = clamp((avg aggression − 60) / 1000, −0.015, 0.015)`; `wr = clamp((avg work rate − 55) / 100, −0.2, 0.25)` |
| | Low | ×0.96 | ×0.95 | −2 | |
| Tempo | Fast | ×(1.04 + 0.08 × wr) | ×1.03 | +2 | quick counters need runners: `wr` as above, so ±2 points |
| | Slow | ×0.97 | ×0.96 | | |
| Width | Wide | ×1.04 with two wide forwards, else ×0.98 | | | formation has ≥ 2 LW/RW slots |
| | Narrow | ×1.04 with at most one wide forward, else ×0.98 | ×1.03 if opponent is wide | | |
| Defensive line | High | ×1.03 | ×0.98 if CB pace ≥ min(84, opponent forward pace − 2), else ×1.08 | | centre-back pace |
| | Deep | ×0.96 | ×0.95, ×1.02 more if opponent plays direct | | |
| Passing | Short | ×1.04 if midfield passing ≥ 80, ×1.0 if ≥ 72, else ×0.96 | | | DM/CM/AM passing |
| | Direct | ×1.04 if striker physical ≥ 80, ×1.0 if ≥ 72, else ×0.96 | | | ST physical |

Role effects: `attack × (1 + Σ creativity / 100)`, `defence × (1 − Σ solidity / 100)`, `fatigue + Σ fatigue / 11`.

Work rate and aggression are bounded on purpose: a pressing, fast side with every man at work rate 80, aggression 80 and stamina 85 gets ×1.471 attack against ×1.328 for the same men at 35, 40 and 55 (the press and the counters each take their share, 1.074 and 1.035), with the notes "A hard-working side makes the press stick" and "Low work rate: the press is half-hearted" written into the report.

Match-ups against the opponent's instructions: a low block with a defensive mentality cuts your attack ×0.92 ("parking the bus"); fast direct play against your high line raises their chances ×1.05; slow short passing when you do not press high cuts your attack ×0.97. Every triggered rule writes a note into the match report.

AI clubs play a fixed style from the club master list:

| Style | Formation | Instructions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Possession | 4-3-3 | balanced, mid press, slow, standard width, high line, short |
| Counter-attack | 4-2-3-1 | balanced, low press, fast, standard, deep, direct |
| Direct | 4-4-2 | balanced, mid, fast, wide, standard, direct |
| High press | 4-3-3 | attacking, high, fast, standard, high, mixed |
| Defensive | 5-3-2 | defensive, low, slow, narrow, deep, direct |
| Balanced | 4-2-3-1 | all defaults |

## 10. Season systems

### Setup

Your club replaces the host club in the chosen real season. The other nineteen are built from their real squads, each with live state so they too suffer injuries and suspensions. **No human plays for both sides** (engine 6, E6-2; `buildAiClubs(…, taken)`, `aiTeamInput(…, taken)` and `aiFixtureInputs` in `season.ts`): the AI clubs are built without the real players (`playerId`, any season of him) your squad holds, and every match leaves out the ones it holds at kick-off, so a signing's old club fields him no more; two AI sides that list one man (a January move inside a league season, one human's two clubs in the Super League, two seasons of a club in the arena) give him to the home side. The calibration is still the league's own, from full squads. The same rule holds in the quick season (the January signing joins for the second half), its cups, the Run-in, head-to-head (both managers' men), the Continental Cup, friendlies and the arena, whose field is still drawn on full squads whatever was drafted (`bothSides.test.ts` wraps the match engine and checks both sides of every match in every mode). On engine 5 one human was on both sides of 11,122 of the sweep's matches, the same player-season on both of 1,759 (Eze scoring for Arsenal and QA XI; Fàbregas taking a kick for each side of an arena shoot-out). Fixtures are a double round robin (`generateFixtures`): 38 matchweeks, 380 matches. The seed shuffles the clubs onto the places of the circle method's canonical schedule, whose venues alternate so that no club has more than one pair of games at the same venue running in a half; the second half is the first with the venues swapped and its first round moved to the end, so no club is home or away more than twice running all season and no rematch follows straight on (every size from 2 to 24 clubs, odd fields with a bye included, `season.test.ts`). Every league mode shares it: Season, Dynasty, Quick Draft and the Daily, the Weekly, the Run-in and head-to-head. Engine `'4'` and before shuffled the rounds of a circle whose venues alternated only along each side of it, so a club could be away for a whole half and at home for the other: the playtest's head-to-head (seed QEWA-3V9H) had Manager 2 away in matchweeks 1 to 19 and at home in 20 to 38 (TL-05, `fixtureVenues.test.ts`). Board confidence starts at 60.

### Pre-season

Up to three friendlies against a random club-season that finished 8th or lower (or is in progress), at a neutral venue with importance 0.9. Each friendly costs 60 per cent of normal condition, adds 9 sharpness and 8 familiarity to those who played, then everybody recovers 14 condition. Statistics are not counted; injuries are.

**The Dynasty opener** (`startSeasonProper` -> `dynastyOpener` in `src/game/logic/dynasty.ts`, Dynasty only, no draws). A Dynasty pre-season opens with the summer's renewal list (section 14, step 3): an Inbox item ("Contracts up: N to renew"), a "Contracts: N out" line in the briefing, an Out of contract chip on the Squad tab, a Talks chip on the contract view and the Planner's Contracts box, which lists each man with Offer terms (his card's contract talks) and Let go (after a question, `letContractGo`: he leaves on a free at once, while the summer window is still open). Leaving pre-season (Play matchweek, a sim) first settles whoever is still out (`settleRenewals`): the assistant offers his own ask at his status, two seasons (one from 32), to a man who is willing (morale 50 or more, talks not closed after three refusals), 34 or under and worth keeping (`RENEW_TOP` the best 18 of the squad by overall, `RENEW_YOUNG` 23 or under, or one of the two best keepers), through `proposeContract`, so the wage cap still binds; anyone else leaves on a free, and an Inbox item "Contracts settled" names both lists. Then the cover (`coverSquad`): while the squad has fewer than `MIN_SEASON_KEEPERS` 2 keepers or `MIN_SEASON_SQUAD` 16 men, the assistant signs a free agent on a one-season backup deal: a real player of the wheel leagues (the current league season first), 55 to 70 overall, worth £3M at most, aged 19 to 33, nobody held, retired or a younger season of a man who left, within one-nation mode; keepers first, then the outfield position the formation is shortest of in natural men. The order is a pure hash of the seed, the year and the player-season, and he leaves every AI squad holding him, so no man plays on both sides.

### The weekly loop

`playMatchweek` (`src/game/logic/week.ts`) runs in this order:

1. If the assistant is managing (Sim 5, Sim to end), any pending event is resolved with its second option and the team is re-picked; otherwise the lineup is repaired only if it has problems (empty slot, duplicate, injured or suspended starter).
2. Your match, with the training focus and any one-off boost applied to λ, then the other nine fixtures.
3. Any cup tie scheduled after this matchweek.
4. Training injuries, weekly recovery, wages paid out of the budget.
5. Board confidence update and, with job security on, a possible sacking.
6. Transfer window open, advance or close.
7. A 42 per cent chance of a dressing-room event (not after matchweek 38).

Your match's other fixtures are already decided when it starts (step 2 plays them in the same loop that begins yours; a live cup tie starts after the round's other ties), so the match-day views reveal them minute by minute by their own goal minutes rather than simulating anything. With `hold`, the last twenty minutes of a live match stay open (clock 90, `game.live` kept) for changes with the ball in play until `completeLiveMatch`, which does exactly what the unheld path does; a held match left open is completed first by the next `playMatchweek`. When the last match of the season is played live, the match screen keeps the final whistle until you continue; the season closes (`seasonEnding`) when you leave it, or when the assistant plays on. Substitutions made at half time and at the 70th minute are written back into the lineup, so the kick-off XI is snapshotted (`onAt === 0`) before the result is applied, for the appearance and promise bookkeeping.

**The inbox's flavour** (`src/game/logic/newsFlavour.ts`, display only). The loop stores a stock body for a result ("Three points.", "A point apiece.", "A defeat to learn from."), a friendly, a free weekend and the January window shutting; the Inbox and the "while you were away" digest show `newsBody(item)` instead: a result's line fits its score (a thrashing of three or more, a clean sheet, a high-scoring win with two or more conceded, a narrow or wider win, a goalless, score or thrilling draw (3-3 and up), a narrow or heavier defeat; 63 lines since content round 2, each old bank keeping the stock line) and the routine items have four to six each (18: a friendly, a free weekend, the window shutting, a five-yellow ban), and a red card's ban ("Suspended for 2 matches.") one of four wordings, the stored one among them (`RED_CARD_BODIES`), picked by a hash of the item's matchweek, kind and title. The lines are mirrored for translation in the `logicText` catalogue (`newsFlavour_*`). What is stored never changes and every other item reads exactly as stored.

### Season Highlights: sim to the next big match

The Dugout's first sim button plays routine weeks with the assistant and hands the manager the ones that matter (`src/game/logic/seasonHighlights.ts`, store `simToNextBigMatch`). Each week is Sim 5's step (`playMatchweek(game, u, true)`, phase back to `week`), so the run plays exactly the weeks `simWeeks(k)` would and draws nothing of its own.

Before each week, `nextStop` (pure) stops the run, leaving that week unplayed, for, in this order:
- a **final** or **semi-final**: `upcomingCupTie` is round F or SF, in the cup or the Continental Cup;
- a **title decider** or **Derby day**: `describeImportance` gives 1.2 or more for the league match (Title decider 1.3, Derby day 1.25);
- a **run-in decider** (the Run-in mode only): one of the last 3 matchweeks while the brief can still be met or missed (`briefOpen`, `src/game/logic/runin.ts`), labelled "Stopped for the run-in decider at Spurs, MW 36: 18th, 1 point behind safety";
- the **title run-in**: one of the last 5 matchweeks, top by 3 points or fewer, or within 3 of the top;
- the **survival run-in**: one of the last 5 matchweeks, in the bottom three with safety still within reach (points plus three per fixture left at least the last safe club's points), or no more than 3 points above the first relegation place.

After each simmed week, `stopAfterWeek` stops the run when the January window opened that week (`window.kind === 'january'`, `day === 0`), or, with job security on, when board confidence crossed from 25 or more to below 25. A sacking or the season's last week ends the run as before.

The button names what it can see coming (`foreseeStop`: the next derby, a drawn semi-final or final, the week the January window opens): "Sim to Derby day (MW 12)", otherwise "Sim to the next big match". Title deciders, the run-in and the board depend on results still to come, so the run can stop sooner. When the next week is itself a stop the button reads "Derby day next" and is disabled: play it. The stop is printed above the fixture ("⏸ Stopped for Derby day v Liverpool, MW 12"); it is transient store state (`simStop`), never saved, and shows only while the game stands at the matchweek it stopped at.

Measured over 108 auto-managed England 2025-26 seasons (seeds `rv3-0` to `rv3-107`, six mid-table hosts with 18 seasons each, Calibrated, ENGINE_VERSION `'4'`, the stop weeks played by the assistant, job security off): 5.0 stops a season (p10/p50/p90 1/5/9; the three blocks of 36 seasons gave 5.5, 5.0 and 4.6, so a 36-season figure moves by about half a stop). It depends on the host's rivals: 3.1 a season for Burnley and 3.6 for Nottingham Forest, whose rivals (Blackburn, Leicester) are not in that league, against 6.9 for Fulham and 6.6 for Brentford (three and two London rivals). Per season: 1.6 derby days, 1.0 January windows, 1.0 title run-in weeks, 0.6 title deciders, 0.5 cup semi-finals, 0.4 cup finals and no survival run-in weeks (an auto-drafted side rarely fights the drop). With job security on (36 seasons) board alarms add 0.2 a season. The button's label never named a week the run went past (445 labelled runs). A test keeps the mean between 2.5 and 8.

### The Run-in
