Issue #10's scenario: take over a real club late in a real season and save it, in about ten minutes. Engine `src/engine/scenario.ts` (pure), logic `src/game/logic/runin.ts`; mode `runin` with its own save slot (`slotOf`), so a Season under way is parked, never overwritten.

- **The catalogue** (`runInCatalogue(u, template)`): league seasons whose real final table is complete (every club played the full programme) and whose every club has a complete club-season of 16 or more players, so England, Spain, Italy and Germany; France, the Netherlands and Portugal have no tables and offer none. A club-season is a job when its real finish fits the brief:
  - **Title race**: 2nd or 3rd, within 6 points of the champions;
  - **Chase for Europe**: 5th to 8th, within 5 points of 4th;
  - **Survival**: in the bottom three, within 6 points of the last safe place.

  On the 2026-10-25 packs (main's historic overrides included; classic seasons are never jobs) that is 28 title jobs (England 9, Spain 9, Italy 7, Germany 3), 71 Europe (23, 9, 15, 24) and 105 survival (29, 24, 19, 33), pinned to the data stamp in `scenario.test.ts`. The setup suggests three drawn from the setup seed's own stream (`suggestRunIns`, `${seed}#runin`).
- **The takeover** is after matchweek `N = round(matchweeks × 28/38)`: 28 of 38 (ten to play), 25 of 34 (nine). The data keeps no matchday standings, so the table at `N` is a **projection from the final table**, and every screen says so: `scaleRow(row, played, N)` scales won, drawn and lost by `N / played`, floors them and gives the matches still missing to the largest remainders (ties to won, then drawn, then lost) so they add up to `N`; goals are rounded and points are `3w + d`. The whole table is then reconciled (`scaleTable`, engine 6, QA O-1) so it adds up as a played one would: while the league has more wins than losses (or fewer), the row whose rounding gave it the most turns one into a draw; while its goals for and against differ, the row whose rounding gave it the most loses a goal (never below its wins or losses). Each row stays within one result and one goal of its own scaling; every catalogue season's carried table has wins equal to losses, goals for equal to against and an even number of draws (`scenario.test.ts`). Engine 5 scaled each row on its own, so Hull 2014-15 carried 210 wins against 211 losses. Example: West Ham 2025-26 finished 18th on 39 (10-9-19), a point behind Tottenham's 41; at MW28 the projection has them 18th on 28 (7-7-14) against Tottenham's 30 (two points; engine 5's per-row scaling had 29).
- **The season** (`beginRunIn`, called by `finishDraft`): the draft into the squad as `beginSeason` does it (`moveDraftToSquad`), the real other clubs (`buildAiClubs`), the full fixture list from `generateFixtures` (matchweeks up to `N` carry no result: "before you arrived"), `season.matchweek = N`, the projection as `SeasonState.carryIn` (the host's row under `USER_TEAM`) with `takeoverAt = N`. Every table is read through `seasonTable(season)`, which starts each row from its carried one. No cup (`cup = []`, `cupEliminated`), no Continental Cup, no January window (it opens at the halfway week, long gone), no friendlies (`friendliesPlayed = 3`, phase `week`). Finances come from `startingFinances` (unused, but every season carries them). Then `playMatchweek`, `afterMatchweek` and `finishSeason` run unchanged.
- **The squad** (`seedRunInDraft`, run by the store right after `createGame`): the host's real club-season, the XI by `autoPickLineup` in the chosen shape (default `formationForStyle(host.style)`, with the style's tactics), the rest by overall up to 18. Each at familiarity 70, sharpness 85, condition 88 to 97 (`rng.int` on the game's stream) and the brief's morale: 72 for the title, 62 for Europe, 45 for survival. Statuses from `defaultStatuses` when the job is taken.
  - **The signings on the pitch** (round 5, `runInProjectedLineup`): the four reinforcements go to the bench in the draft (the club's XI is already full) and `beginRunIn` picks the XI again, so while they are drafted the Draft's pitch shows the XI the job will start with: the same two steps (`moveDraftToSquad`, `autoPick`) on a copy, tested equal to the lineup after `beginRunIn`. Pure; the game is untouched.
- **Reinforcements**: four spins of the league's wheel through a draft seeded with the inherited squad, the Dynasty summer's pattern (lineup the inherited XI, bench the rest, `takenPlayers` marked, `squadSize = 18 + 4`, one re-spin, no twists). The finish button reads "Take the job".
- **Difficulty**: Realistic. The squad is real, so the rivals are too; Calibrated would hand a real squad handicapped opponents.
- **The board**: target 1, 4 or `clubs − 3` (17th of 20); confidence 70, 60 or 50; job security on, and the sack's matchweek-10 floor is long past, so a bad run ends the job from the first week. `season.objectives` is the brief alone (`ObjectiveId` `title`, `top-four` or `survive`, with the place in `target`), judged met or missed on the final position by `evaluateObjective`.
- **Highlights** is the way to play it: besides the usual stops, each of the last three matchweeks is a stop while the brief is still open (`briefStanding`: met is certain when fewer than `target` other clubs can still reach the user's points; missed when `target` clubs are already past the user's ceiling).
- **The end**: `finishSeason` runs unchanged, but a run-in never goes in the record books (`inRecordBooks`: no benchmark, no `record-points`) and earns none of the season achievements (Champions, Centurions, 38-0 and the rest): its first 28 weeks are a projection. It earns its own: **The Great Escape** (keep up a side that really went down: the bottom three, but in the Bundesliga only the 17th and 18th, since its 16th played a relegation play-off) and **Late Show** (win the title from matchweek 28); Survivor still counts. `recordResult` files a `RunInEntry` in `profile.runIns` (newest first, at most 50) and credits the cabinet's run-in shelf (`creditRunIn`: played, briefs met, which the Survival Artist badge counts), never the leaderboard, the league titles or the seasons simulated.
- **How close** (r9, `marginText` in `src/ui/runInText.ts`, display only): under the verdict, the gap to the club across the brief's line, "3 points clear of Fulham in 18th" or "2 points short of QPR in 17th", and when the points are level, "Level on points with QPR in 17th: out on goal difference"; none for a sacked manager or a play-off. The Run-in of the week names itself on the Dugout banner and the summary ("Run-in of the week · Title race").
- **The verdict** (`runInVerdict`) is against the real club: "You kept West Ham up on 41; the real West Ham went down on 39", with a chip of SURVIVED, TITLE WON, TOP FOUR or MISSED. A Bundesliga 16th played a relegation play-off whose result the packs do not keep (`PLAYOFF_LEAGUES`, `relegatedDirectly` in `scenario.ts`), so its real line reads "finished 16th on 27, in the relegation play-off place", never "went down"; the survival brief is 15th, and from engine 7 a run-in that finishes 16th plays **the relegation play-off** (`playRunInPlayOff` in `week.ts`, run by `finishSeason`): two legs, at home first, against `playOffOpponent` (`scenario.ts`: the packs hold no 2. Bundesliga, so the weakest by real team strength of the clubs promoted for the next season stands in, at the strength of its first season up), with the XI and tactics the season ended with, extra time and penalties after the second leg if level (`settleTie`), drawn from the game's stream after the last matchweek and counted in nothing else (no statistics, no morale). Won, the brief is met (the chip SURVIVED, the objective met, the Great Escape for a side that really went down): "Hamburg finished 16th on 28 under you and stayed up through the relegation play-off, 2-1 and 1-1 against Fortuna Düsseldorf, 3-2 on aggregate"; lost, "…went down in the relegation play-off…". Kept as `SeasonState.playOff`. Measured over every German survival job six times (198 run-ins, assistant-managed): 34 finished 16th and played it, 26 won it (76%: the promoted stand-in is often weaker than a real 2. Bundesliga third), and the brief was met in 124, against 98 on the league table alone. The share text (`runInShareText`, the product name through its `appName` parameter):

  ```
  TouchLine · Run-in · West Ham 2025-26
  Took over at MW28: 18th, 28 pts (projected)
  ✅ SURVIVED · 16th on 41
  🟩🟨🟥🟩🟩🟨🟩🟥🟩🟨
  📊 The real West Ham went down on 39
  Seed …
  ```
- **Screens**: Home's casual row has the Run-in tile (`runin-card`, "Take over at MW 28, ten games left"). `RunInSetup` (`src/ui/screens/RunInSetup.tsx`): the brief (Title race · Chase for Europe · Survival), the league (the four with tables, loaded on choice), the seed, the shape (the club's own by default), three jobs the seed suggests and "Browse all", grouped by season, each "18th, 28 pts at MW28 (projected), 1 point behind safety" with the brief; one line above "Take over West Ham United" states every choice, and starting over an unfinished run-in asks first. The draft is titled Reinforcements. The side's code is its club's three-letter code (`shortName` is `club.code`: before, the club's short name made the live table read "Blackpool Blackpool"). The Dugout shows `RunInBanner` (the takeover, projected, the brief and where it stands now), the Fixtures tab folds matchweeks 1 to 28 into one "Before you arrived" row, the Table tab says "projected from the final table", and there is no Cup tab. `RunInSummary`: the chip (SURVIVED, TITLE WON, TOP FOUR or MISSED), the verdict, the ten games with the position after each, the share text and the final table. The Profile page adds Run-in briefs to the cabinet row and a Run-ins table (newest first, the brief met or missed, the real finish).
- **The Run-in of the week** (`src/game/logic/runinWeekly.ts`): one job for everyone each ISO week, seed `runin-YYYY-Www`: the league `RUN_IN_LEAGUES[n % 4]` and brief `RUN_IN_TEMPLATES[(n × 5) % 3]` with `n = year × 100 + week`, one job of that league's catalogue (sorted by season, then club id) at `hashString(seed) % count`, the club's own shape (frozen like the Daily mapping). Run-in setup's "Run-in of the week" and the World leaderboard's Run-in tab start it. Its draft rows are kept on the game at the takeover (`Game.runInDraft`, no draw), so a finished run builds a short payload (`RunInPayload`) the World leaderboard's server replays: the inherited squad, the four picks, then the assistant through every matchweek exactly as Sim to end does (`simRunIn`, `playMatchweek(..., true)`). A run-in managed by hand (tactics, a watched match, an event's choice) plays another season, so it does not rank; the device checks by replaying it (`runInReplayProblem`) before filing it (docs/ONLINE.md section 5g).
- **Balance** (England catalogue, the assistant drafting and managing, ENGINE_VERSION `'4'`): over 400 run-ins per template (seeds `wide-0` to `wide-399`, the jobs in turn) the brief was met in 21.8% of title races, 21.3% of chases for Europe and 40.5% of survival fights (3 spins: 35.8%, 5 spins: 40.3%); points per game matched the real clubs' over the same weeks (2.31 against 2.32, 1.74 and 1.74, 0.91 and 0.90). The balance guard in `runin.test.ts` (100 survival run-ins, seeds `runin-guard-*`, 36 met) holds it between 20 and 60%; tune `RUN_IN_SPINS` (3 to 5) before anything else.

### The assistant's briefing

`weeklyBriefing(game, u)` (`src/game/logic/briefing.ts`) is a pure, RNG-free function that turns the game state into at most `BRIEFING_CAP` (6) lines, each a `BriefingItem` with a level (`block`, `warn`, `info`, `ok`), a sentence and an optional action (a dugout tab to open and, where needed, an op: repair the lineup, open a player, scroll to the pending decision, play a friendly). The dugout renders it inside the next-match panel in place of the old chip row; it is empty once the season is over or the manager is sacked.

In season the lines come in this priority order: the pending decision (`block`); the board's stakes; what waits in the Office (r10: `officeLine`, the items the Office tab's badge counts, `officeItems`: one request to leave named with its matchweeks left, `R. Name has asked to leave: answer him in the Office (2 matchweeks left)`, personal terms after an agreed fee, a promise falling due, or `3 things wait in the Office: ...` for several; a warning when a request or a promise has `OFFICE_URGENT_WEEKS` (1) or less left, opening the Office); each `lineupProblems` entry by name with an Auto-pick button, collapsed into one `n lineup issues: ...` line when there are three or more; starters below `BRIEFING_CONDITION` (60) condition or, failing that, below `BRIEFING_SHARPNESS` (50) sharpness, opening the player (injured and suspended starters are left to the lineup line); a starter whose yellow cards stand one short of a multiple of `YELLOWS_PER_BAN` (5); a promised start who is not in the XI; the window as `info` (`January window open, day 3 of 7 · 2 offers for your players`, or `Deadline day ...` on the last day) opening the Market tab; the midweek tie (`Fourth round v Luton midweek. Rotate, you play again Thursday`) opening the Cup or Europe tab; each board objective at risk by `objectiveRisk` (home form one defeat before the limit or past it, a derby lost, a cup exit short of the round, and from `PACE_FROM_MATCHWEEK` (8) a goals or youth-starts projection, `(goals so far / matchweek) × matchweeks` rounded, below the target); and finally one `ok` line, `Training Balanced, team talk Encourage for Arsenal (1st)`, opening the Tactics tab so those knobs are seen to exist. `ok` lines are dropped as soon as any `warn` or `block` line exists, then the cap is applied, so the briefing can only grow with real problems.

In pre-season the same function returns a checklist with `done` marks: three friendlies played (with a Play friendly action), a captain named (the action opens the suggested leader, `suggestedCaptain`: the highest `(mentality − 75) × (0.6 + 0.8 × professionalism)`, plus 10 for a starter, which is the engine's `leadership` modifier with its pivot at `LEADER_MENTALITY` (75), so a man under 75 mentality is never suggested over one above it), roles reviewed (any `tactics.roles[slot]` differing from `DEFAULT_ROLE`), staff hired (the line states the wage room under the cap and that the cheapest hire, a senior physio, costs £15k a week), set-piece takers named, and in Dynasty the youth intake answered. Each line is a short label ("Friendlies 0/3", "Captain", "Roles", "Staff 1/4", "Set pieces", "Youth intake") with its explanation in `hint`, shown behind an Info button; the friendlies line has no button of its own because the panel's Play friendly button does it. Done lines are `ok`, undone lines `info`, so nothing hides them.

On screens of 720px or narrower the panel's Play, Sim 5 and Sim to end buttons (and Play friendly in pre-season) move into a fixed bottom bar (`.cta-bar`, padded for the safe-area inset) with the briefing's headline (`briefingHeadline`: the first undone line) beside them, so the reason a disabled Play button is disabled is next to it. The page carries `has-bottom-bar` and its bottom padding is the bar's measured height plus 20px, so the last panel and the event card's choice buttons are never under the bar; the bar renders only in the dugout (phases `preseason` and `week`) while the season is live, never on the match screen.

### Condition, sharpness, familiarity, morale

| System | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Condition recovery | `(31 + (physical − 70) × 0.12 + training recovery) × 0.93 if 30+ or 0.85 if 33+` per week, capped at 100. A week's rest covers one match for a fit player; two matches in a week do not |
| Sharpness | +12 for a start, +6 for a substitute appearance, −6 for a week without a match (floor 30), −10 on return from injury (floor 40), plus the weekly training delta (clamped 20 to 100) |
| Familiarity | +4 for a start, +2 as a sub, each multiplied by `clamp(1 − (55 − work rate) / 100, 0.8, 1)` and rounded (a start is +3 at work rate 35, +4 from 55 up; a sub stays +2), plus the training delta; −12 for everyone on a formation change mid-season |
| Morale after a match | +5 win, 0 draw, −5 defeat, plus `(match rating − 6.8) × 2`, clamped 10 to 100 |
| Playing-time morale | Weekly drift against the squad status (next section): short of the expected share by more than 0.15, `(gap + 0.15) × 12 × (1.3 − 0.6 × professionalism)`; above it by more than 0.15, `(gap − 0.15) × 4`; clamped ±5 a week. This replaced the flat bench penalty |
| Promises | A promised start kept: +5; broken: −16 and an inbox item |
| Suspensions | Every fifth yellow card: one match. Red card: three matches if the match rating was under 5.5, otherwise two. Served one match at a time while he sits out |
| Injuries | Countdown one week per matchweek; cup weeks do not count extra |

### Squad status and playing time

`src/engine/status.ts`. Every contract carries a status, and a player's morale follows the minutes he gets against what the status promised.

| Status | Expected share of minutes | Wage multiplier on signing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Key player | 0.85 | ×1.25 |
| First team | 0.65 | ×1.10 |
| Rotation | 0.35 | ×1.00 |
| Backup | 0.15 | ×0.90 |
| Prospect | 0.10 | ×0.80 |

- **Defaults.** When a season begins (`beginSeason`, after the opening eleven is auto-picked) every contract without a status gets one by rank in the squad: the top four by overall are key players, the next seven first team, the next six rotation, the rest backup; anyone aged 21 or under who rates below the squad median is a prospect. A player outside the opening eleven starts one step lower than his rank says (a second striker rated fourth in the squad is first team, not a key player), because the shape, not his rating, decides how much he can play. A contract written before statuses existed reads as rotation.
- **Minutes.** `SquadPlayer.recentMinutes` keeps the last six matchweeks for the user's squad only (AI clubs do not track it). League and cup minutes in the same week add to one entry, so a two-match week can exceed 90; a week without an appearance records 0 when he was available, nothing when he missed it injured or suspended (engine 6, item 4: `Game.weekUnavailable`, filled at the match and cleared with the week, absent in older saves), so a key man back from injury no longer knocks on the door about minutes; a bye week records nothing. The run of missed matches behind the wants-to-talk event does not grow while he is out either. The share is `min(1, total / (90 × weeks recorded))`.
- **Drift.** In the weekly recovery, with at least one week recorded: `gap = share − expected`. Below −0.15 the player loses `(gap + 0.15) × 12 × (1.3 − 0.6 × professionalism)` morale; above +0.15 he gains `(gap − 0.15) × 4`; the weekly change is clamped to ±5. A key player who does not play loses 5 a week; a backup who starts every match gains 2.8; a rotation player on anything from 20 to 50 per cent is left alone. The flat bench penalty this replaced is gone, not stacked under it. On seed `status-flow` (Burnley 2025-26, 4-3-3, 20 auto-managed weeks) the mean squad morale is 94.7 against 92.2 before; the drain now lands on the one 85-rated striker stuck behind a 90 rather than on whoever last missed two matches.
- **Events.** The wants-to-talk event also fires for a key or first-team player whose share over the last three recorded matchweeks is more than 0.25 under his expectation, at weight 6 instead of 3, with the shortfall spelled out in the text and a fourth choice, "tell him he can leave": +6 morale and the transfer list. The contract event now offers three answers: renew as first team (a key player stays key) on +10 per cent wages (+10 morale), renew as rotation on the same money (+8 morale for a rotation or lower player; a key or first-team player refuses with probability `0.7 − 0.5 × professionalism` and runs his contract down, otherwise signs at −6), or let him go.
- **Market.** A bid carries a status (the market suggests the one his rating would earn in your squad). The player refuses rotation or lower when he would be among your four best, backup or lower when he is at or above your median, and prospect past the age of 21; the refusal names the lowest status he would take (`counterStatus`). His wage is the listed wage times the multiplier, and the wage-cap check uses that figure. A signing's wage is written into `customPlayers`, the pattern renewals already used; loans keep the parent club's wage and sign as rotation. The Club tab changes a status at any time: a promotion is +6 morale and the wage scaled by the ratio of the two multipliers, a demotion −6 morale with the wage kept. The wage is paid for the highest status reached (`Contract.paidStatus`, optional, carried into the next season, cleared when contract talks agree a new wage), so a man moved down and back up is raised only past it (until 2026-10-28 each round trip raised it again).
- **Dynasty and academy.** Statuses travel through the summer in all three contract branches; after ageing recomputes the wage from the new value the status multiplier is applied again. Academy signings arrive as prospects.

### Training

One focus per week, applied to the next match and to the week's recovery (`src/engine/training.ts`):

| Focus | Attack λ | Opp. λ | Recovery | Sharpness | Familiarity | Injury risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Balanced | ×1.00 | ×1.00 | 0 | +2 | +1 | ×1.0 |
| Attacking | ×1.04 | ×1.01 | −2 | +3 | +1 | ×1.0 |
| Defensive | ×0.99 | ×0.96 | −2 | +2 | +1 | ×1.0 |
| Set pieces | ×1.03 | ×0.98 | 0 | +1 | 0 | ×0.9 |
| Fitness | ×1.00 | ×1.00 | −4 | +4 | 0 | ×1.35 |
| Recovery | ×0.99 | ×1.00 | +9 | −3 | 0 | ×0.6 |
| Tactical | ×1.01 | ×0.99 | 0 | 0 | +5 | ×0.8 |

Training injury per player per week: `0.006 × injury risk × (0.5 + injuryProneness)`, out for one to three weeks.

### The cup

Sixteen of the twenty clubs are drawn (you are always in). Rounds are played midweek after matchweeks 6 (labelled Fourth round), 14 (quarter-final), 24 (semi-final) and 36 (final, neutral venue). Drawn ties go to penalties: each kick scores with probability `0.72 + (team overall − 70) × 0.003`, five each then sudden death. Cup matches use your current lineup, so rotation is on you; knocked-out teams get free midweeks. The Dugout's Cup tab draws the competition as a bracket (`seasonCupBracket` in `src/game/logic/bracket.ts`, the same `components/Bracket.tsx` as the Quick Draft's and the arena's cups): every round side by side, ties in tree order so the lines join the two ties that meet next, your route in the primary colour, scores and shoot-outs, the winner beside the final, and a round still to be drawn as empty slots with "Drawn after matchweek 14". It reads only scores, penalties and winners, so a compacted save draws the same bracket.

### The Continental Cup

Optional at setup for Season and Dynasty (`settings.continental`), and earned by right in later Dynasty seasons by finishing in the top four or lifting the trophy (`game.qualifiedContinental`). `buildContinentalClubs` takes up to four clubs per other loaded league in the same season (nearest season if that league lacks it), ordered by final position then the average of their top fourteen ratings, tops up from the strongest remaining clubs when fewer leagues are loaded, and keeps the fifteen strongest. You are the sixteenth entrant.

**The format from engine 7** (`src/engine/continental.ts`, `SeasonState.continental.groups`):

- **The draw** (`potOrder`, `drawGroups`): four pots of four by strength (the field strongest first, you at the head of the second pot, so you never meet more than one of the four strongest in the groups), each pot shuffled once, group A to D taking one side from every pot. Four shuffles, nothing else.
- **The groups**: each side plays the other three at home and away (`GROUP_SCHEDULE`: three home games apiece, at most two running at home or away, matchdays 3 and 4 the same pairs), ordered by points, goal difference, goals scored, then the side's id (`groupTable`, the league's own ordering). The top two go through.
- **The quarter-finals** are fixed by the groups, no draw: A1 v B2, B1 v A2, C1 v D2, D1 v C2, the runner-up at home in the first leg and the group winner at home in the second. The semi-finals are drawn from the winners (one shuffle).
- **Two legs** in the quarter- and semi-finals: the aggregate decides, with **no away goals rule**; level after the second leg, extra time on the second leg's expected goals (`extraTime`, the cups' 0.85 / 3 rate), then a shoot-out (`shootout`, the second leg's sides' nerve), both drawn only when needed (`settleTie`). The **final** stays one match at a neutral ground, with extra time and penalties after a draw. A tie's score is read through `tieScore` (the aggregate in `CupTie.agg`, each leg from `CupTie.firstLeg` and `result`; the second leg's `result` keeps that match's own sides).
- **The calendar** (`continentalPlan`): group matchdays after matchweeks 3, 5, 8, 10, 12 and 16 of 38, the quarter-finals after 19 and 21, the semi-finals after 27 and 29, the final after 33, scaled to the league's length and moved a week on where a short league would put two together or on a domestic cup week (6, 14, 24, 36). Every one is its own midweek before the last matchweek for any league of 26 to 46 matchweeks (`continental.test.ts`). Eleven midweeks for a finalist, against four before.
- **Playing it** (`continentalMidweek` in `week.ts`): the AI matches of the matchday or the legs first (each two-legged tie settled when its second leg is in, on the week's stream), then yours, live with the stops like any cup tie (`LiveMatch.group` or `leg`), or played through by the assistant. After the last matchday the quarter-finals are made and third or fourth is out (`closeContinentalWeek`); after a round's second legs the next round is drawn. Importance 1.15 (European night, the group matches too), 1.35 (semi-final), 1.5 (final).
- **What it shows**: the Dugout's Europe tab draws the four groups (`components/GroupTables.tsx` from `seasonGroupViews`: a small table each, the top two marked through, your group outlined with its fixtures) above the bracket from the quarter-finals (`seasonCupBracket`, aggregates with each leg under the tie, "agg", "aet" or "pens"); the match heading says "Continental Cup, Group B, matchday 3" or "Continental quarter-final, second leg (first leg 1-0)"; the calendar strip and the "while you were away" digest list every group match and leg (`userCupMatches`, `src/game/logic/cupMatches.ts`); the Season summary has a Continental Cup box with the groups and the bracket. A compacted save keeps every score (`compactGroups`, and both legs in `compactTies`).
- **Money and objectives**: prize money by the stage reached (`CONTINENTAL_PRIZE`, engine 6's figures): £2M out in the groups (as the round of 16 paid), £4M quarter-final, £7M semi-final, £11M final, £18M winners. "Reach the Continental Cup quarter-final" is met by coming through the group. The history row's `continentalRun` reads `G` for a group-stage exit.
- **Saves**: a Continental Cup drawn before engine 7 has no `groups` and plays on as the single-tie knockout it was drawn as: rounds after matchweeks 10, 19, 29 and 33, drawn ties to penalties, round of 16 to final (tested from a normalised engine 6 save).

Winning unlocks Kings of the Continent; league, cup and Europe together unlock The Treble. `clubOf` resolves an opponent from the league or the continental field.

**Calibration (engine 7).** Measured on 40 seeded Seasons each (England 2018-19 to 2024-25, six hosts, the assistant drafting and managing, Calibrated, the Continental Cup on), engine 6 then engine 7: league goals a game 2.84 then 2.82 and draws 22.1% then 23.0% (the targets 2.6 to 2.9 and 23 to 27% hold); the user's league points 95.4 then 90.1 and the champions' 96.0 then 93.4 (eleven midweeks for a finalist, against four, tire the user's squad); Continental Cup goals a game 3.23 then 3.10, 15 then 61 continental matches a season. How far the user went: engine 6 out in the round of 16 16, quarter-final 6, semi-final 5, final 3, winners 10; engine 7 out in the groups 19, quarter-final 6, semi-final 1, final 5, winners 9. The Quick Draft's post-season run over 80 seeded top-seven seasons: engine 6 round of 16 29, quarter-final 21, semi-final 17, final 4, winners 9; engine 7 groups 18, quarter-final 21, semi-final 18, final 13, winners 10 (`cupBracket.test.ts`'s Treble seed is now `treble-1`).

### Man-marking

`markTarget` on the user's match input (set from the opposition report, cleared after the match). At kick-off the target is flagged as marked and his effective rating drops by three; while the flag holds, his weight as a scorer and as a creator from attack is multiplied by 0.6 and from midfield by 0.85 (the shares themselves are untouched, so a substitute who takes his slot inherits a clean copy), the opponent's expected goals are multiplied by 0.93 and the marking side's attack modifier by 0.97. The report notes it, and the modifier is re-applied after the half-time recompute.

### Press conferences

Three media events join the deck, rolled like any other after a matchweek: a post-match conference after a league win (single out the man of the match or scorer: +7 for him, −3 for up to two unprofessional stars; credit the squad: +2 all; promise more: +2 board confidence) or defeat (take the blame: +3 morale, −2 confidence; call it unacceptable: +2 for professionals, −5 for the rest, +2 confidence; blame the referee: 40 per cent chance of a £0.5M fine), and the job question from matchweek 12 when you sit more than four places below the board's target with confidence under 45 (claim the board's backing: a coin flip between +6 and −6 confidence).

### Captain and set pieces

`captainId` is passed into the match engine. A captain on the pitch contributes `leadership = clamp((mentality − 75) / 100 × (0.6 + professionalism × 0.8), −0.25, 0.25)`: his side's expected goals are multiplied by `1 + 0.06 × leadership` and the opponent's by `1 − 0.04 × leadership`. Off the pitch, a captain with professionalism ≥ 0.6 and mentality ≥ 72 lifts every squad player below 70 morale by one a week (two below 50).

`tactics.setPieces` names a penalty taker (he takes every penalty while on the pitch), a free-kick taker and a corner taker. The specialists add `((passing − 72) / 100) × 0.06` for corners and `((shooting − 72) / 100) × 0.05` for free kicks to the side's expected-goal multiplier, clamped to −0.04..+0.07, only while they are on the pitch. AI clubs use the automatic choice.

### Player conversations

`src/game/logic/talks.ts`. One conversation per player per matchweek. Form is the mean of the last three ratings (needs two).

| Talk | Condition | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Praise | form ≥ 7.2 | +8 morale (+10 for a maverick) |
| Praise | form < 6.5 | −4: he thinks you are being sarcastic |
| Demand more | form ≤ 6.6, professional | −2 morale, +6 sharpness, +2 familiarity |
| Demand more | form ≤ 6.6, maverick | −8 |
| Demand more | form ≥ 7.2 | −10: he has been one of your best |
| Promise a start | one promise at a time | +6 now; +5 if kept, −16 if broken (`promisedStart`) |
| Reassure | on the bench, morale < 60 | +7 |

Negative swings are scaled by `1.2 − 0.5 × professionalism`.

### In-season development

`src/game/logic/development.ts`, run after matchweeks 5, 10, 15 and so on (never the last). Growth per block for a player who played every minute: +0.9 at 20 or under, +0.55 to 23, +0.2 to 26, 0 to 29, −0.15 to 32, −0.3 from 33. Positive growth is scaled by the square root of the share of available minutes he played, by `1 + 0.25 × coach tier`, and capped at `potential − overall` with `potential = overall + max(0, 27 − age) × 1.3`. Each attribute moves by the probabilistically rounded delta, the five depth attributes with the rest (keepers: goalkeeping, mentality, passing, physical, stamina, strength, jumping); pace, physical and stamina lose an extra point sometimes when fading. The overall moves by exactly what the change moved the overall derived from the attributes (`developedOverall`: the shown overall plus `overallOf(new attributes) − overallOf(old attributes)`, 1 to 99), and value scales by 1.21 per point; the dynasty summer (`ageOneYear`) moves it the same way. Development is a delta from the rating the player shows, never a recomputation, because an imported overall (EA's, or the classic estimate) is not `overallOf` of the imported attributes: over the 78,058 player-seasons of the seven packs the derived figure is 2.6 points lower on average (median 2, 5 per cent 7 or more, 47 at most), and more so for older men. Engine `'4'` and before stored `overallOf` after the first change, so a man's first update reset him to that figure (INV-01): the playtest's run-in (seed TX3U-SU25) reported R. Bennett 72→63, A. Doucouré 81→77 and H. Kane, 23, 89→84, exactly each man's derived overall, and over 30 seeded Season squads 39 to 97 per cent of each age band fell 3 or more at the first update; now none falls at all under 30 and the veterans lose 0 or 1 (`development.test.ts`). The developed player is stored in `customPlayers` under the same id, so lineups and saves stay valid, and the modal shows the change since the start of the season. A development report lands in the inbox.

### Youth intake

`src/game/logic/youth.ts`, Dynasty only, built in `beginSeason`. On the Squad tab it is a panel over the squad: the candidates as cards side by side on a desktop, or, on a desktop window up to 940 px tall, one 60 px line of chips in the panel's head (position, name, age, rating, Sign, a gold edge for a homegrown one; the terms in the tooltip); a phone stacks them. Candidates are real player-seasons aged 20 or under rated 58 to 76 within the era filter and not already owned: up to two from any of the host club's real squads across the seasons (tagged Homegrown), topped up to three from the wheel leagues. Signing one costs nothing and gives a three-year contract, and is refused while the squad holds 23 (`SQUAD_CAP`, engine 6); passing records the decision. The offer lasts through pre-season.

### Club legends

`finishSeason` accumulates every squad player's appearances, goals, assists, man-of-the-match awards, seasons and titles into `game.careerStats` keyed by the real player id, so a player who ages through several dynasty seasons keeps one record. The Club tab lists the top twelve, each with his position, his best overall with us (peak), his average match rating, his first season (the tooltip: his age and overall then) and a crown for the seasons he ended as captain; a man still in the squad is in bold.

### The club's history (round 9)

`src/game/logic/dynastyHistory.ts`, Season and Dynasty, display only. When a season is recorded the store's `recordResult` runs `noteSeasonEnd` once: the season's line in `game.history` gets `notes` (the position the board asked for, the objectives met of how many, the end confidence, the sack, the captain, the young player of the season), and every man who played that season gets his career details on his `CareerRecord` (`pos`, `peak`, `firstYear`, `firstAge`, `firstOverall`, `lastAge`, `lastOverall`, `ratingSum`, `captainSeasons`). All optional: an older save reads them as absent (a legend from before gets `firstYear` from his seasons, nothing else he cannot know). Nothing draws: `dynastyHistory.test.ts` plays the same Dynasty with and without it and finds the same game, byte for byte, but for those fields.

- **Club history** (the Club tab's box `history`, from the second season): Seasons (every line, newest first: the league and the club replaced, the finish, the finish asked for with a met or missed mark, W-D-L, goals, Champions, Cup and Europe chips, Sacked, our top scorer), Honours (`honoursOf`: seasons, league titles, cups, Continental Cups, Doubles (a Treble counts too), Trebles, the longest run of titles, the best finish, relegations, board targets met) and Best XI (`bestXI`: each slot of the side's formation filled by the legend with the best `legendScore`, his peak plus twice his average rating plus up to ten for appearances (one per 19), naturals first, the scarcest positions first, then cover at a position factor of 0.8 or more, each man once; at least `BEST_XI_MIN_APPS` = 10 appearances). The board box says how last season went against its target.
- **The summer's stories** (`summerSnapshot` before `advanceToNextSeason`, `summerStories` after, both in the store's `nextSeason`; transient, on `offseason.stories`): the farewells (every man who retired, with his seasons, appearances, goals and titles for us; the captain marked, "the armband needs a new owner"), the breakthroughs (23 or under after the summer and up two or more, with his overall when he joined when lower) and the milestones the season took a man past (100, 150, 200, 250, 300, 400 and 500 appearances; 50, 100, 150 and on goals).

### Backroom staff

`src/engine/staff.ts`. Four roles, three tiers (none, good, elite), wages in £k/week counted in `squadWages` and therefore against the cap.

| Role | Good | Elite |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Assistant (£25k / £60k) | +1 familiarity a week, team talks ×1.15 | +2, ×1.30 |
| Coach (£20k / £50k) | +1 sharpness a week, training boosts +1% | +2, +2% |
| Physio (£15k / £40k) | training injuries ×0.75, +2 condition a week, injuries of 3+ weeks heal a week sooner | ×0.55, +4, every injury a week sooner |
| Scout (£15k / £40k) | +1 scouted listing a market day, hidden traits tagged on the market | +2 a day, and the opponent's weak link in the report |

### Board objectives

`src/engine/objectives.ts`. A run-in's only objective is its brief (`title`, `top-four`, `survive`; see "The Run-in"), never drawn from the pool. Two per season, drawn at kick-off from a pool shaped by the board target: a cup quarter-final (semi-final for a top-four target), a league goals tally (1.5 × matchweeks, 1.9 for the ambitious), at most four home defeats (two), ten league starts for a player aged 21 or under, a Continental Cup quarter-final when entered, and no derby defeats when the host has rivals in the league. Progress shows live in the Club tab; at the end of the season each one met adds £5M to the budget and +8 confidence, each one missed costs 6 confidence, and the inbox gets the verdict.

### Board, confidence and the sack

- **Target.** Count the AI clubs whose (calibrated) overall exceeds yours minus 1.5, add one, clamp to 1 to 17. The inbox welcome message states it.
- **Confidence** per matchweek: +2.5 for a win, −3 for a defeat, 0 for a draw; from matchweek 6, +1.5 if you are at or above the target, else `−min(4, (position − target) × 0.8)`. Clamped 0 to 100.
- **Sacked** if job security is on, matchweek ≥ 10, confidence < 12 and the season is not over. The season ends immediately with tier RELEGATED and no prize money.
- Dropping below 20 at any point and surviving unlocks the Survivor achievement.

### Dressing-room events

Rolled from a weighted deck when conditions are met (`src/engine/events.ts`). The Play button is disabled until you answer.

| Event | Trigger | Choices and effects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A star wants to talk | A player rated 78+ has missed 3+ matches, or a key or first-team player is 0.25 under his expected share of minutes over the last three matchweeks (weight 6 instead of 3) | Promise a start (+12 morale, promise tracked); tell him to earn it (40% + 0.4 × professionalism: +4 morale, +3 condition; else −10 and team −1); hear him out (+4); tell him he can leave (+6, transfer-listed) |
| Contract running down | Matchweek ≥ 24, a contract in its last season not yet answered | Renew as first team or key (+10% wages, +10 morale, signing-on fee 8% of value); renew as rotation (same money: +8 for a rotation or lower player, a senior player refuses with probability 0.7 − 0.5 × professionalism, else −6); let him go (−8, leaves in the summer) |
| Press: can you go unbeaten? | Unbeaten, matchweek ≥ 10, 8+ wins | Embrace (team +5); one game at a time (nothing); laugh it off (team −2, confidence +1) |
| Training-ground bust-up | Squad ≥ 14, 50% | Fine both (−5 each, team +2); back one (+6 / −9); ignore (50%: team +1 or team −4) |
| Derby week | Next opponent is a rival | Feed the fire (attack +5% next match, team +2); treat it normally (defence +4% next match) |
| The board wants a word | Matchweek ≥ 12, more than 3 places below target, confidence < 50 | Ask for backing (50%: +£20M budget, confidence −3; else −6); ask for time (+4); blame the players (confidence +5, team morale −6) |
| An agent calls | 50%, Dynasty only | Take the meeting (+1 bonus spin at the summer draft); pass |
| Physio warning | 3+ players below 65 condition | Two days off (+8 condition all, team +2, sharpness unchanged in code); push through |
| Name your captain | Matchweek 1, or matchweek 20 on a coin flip | Pick one of the three leaders by mentality + age (+8 morale, team +1) |

### Finances

- Starting budget is `clamp(0.3 × host club's real squad value, 30, 250)` GBP millions.
- Wage cap is the larger of the club's natural bill, `clamp(5 × squad value, 900, 4500)` GBP thousands a week, and your drafted squad's wages with 15 per cent headroom, so the board always underwrites the squad you drafted. In Dynasty the cap is recomputed each summer; the budget carries over and the season's income, spending and wages-paid counters reset.
- Weekly cash flow (`weeklyCashFlow`): match-day and broadcast income is assumed to cover the wage cap; a tenth of what the wages leave under it (`HEADROOM_SHARE`, the rest runs the club) drips into the budget every matchweek, and a bill over the cap drains the whole overspend.
- League money at season end, net of running costs (`prizeMoney`): `20 × (1 − 2 × (position − 1) / (clubs − 1))` GBP millions, so +20 for the champions, 0 at mid-table and −20 for last place, plus 10 for the cup; the Continental Cup adds 2 (round of 16), 4, 7, 11 or 18 (winners). A season's loss takes the budget down to the board's summer floor at worst (`afterSeasonMoney`, `SUMMER_FLOOR` £10M: a club not in debt starts every summer with at least that, so a side that keeps finishing bottom can still sign a keeper); only wages over the cap run a club into debt, and a club in debt gets no floor. Nothing if sacked.
- Engine 6 (item 2): the whole headroom dripped before and a title paid £100M (last place £39M, the Continental Cup up to £45M), so a Dynasty that signed nobody grew about £125M to £150M a season whatever its finish (£94M to £756M in five seasons for a champion, to £623M for a side that started 13th). Measured over 36 five-season Dynasties (seeds `e6-dyn-0` to `-35`, England 2012, 2015, 2018 and 2021 hosts, no signings, the assistant managing; strong: an unrestricted draft on Calibrated, champions every season; middling: Scotland's internationals only on Calibrated, 10.8th in the first season, then 6.4th, 4.3rd, 4.3rd and 4.5th; weak: Northern Ireland's only on Realistic, 19th to 20th every season), the mean budget at each season's end: strong 94.0 → 127.1 → 159.2 → 190.2 → 222.5 → 255.5; middling 94.0 → 102.3 → 119.1 → 140.3 → 162.0 → 179.8; weak 94.0 → 82.5 → 71.0 → 60.3 → 51.9 → 44.4 (three of the twelve end at nothing or a little under: wages paid in a season can still overspend a budget at zero). On engine 5 the same seeds went to 756.0, 699.4 and 623.0, and its weak sides finished 5th by the fifth season. A weak side with no money also cannot refill its squad: by the fourth summer some Realistic squads were down to 14 or 15 men with one keeper, since the assistant has nothing to sign with.
- Squad cap 23. Releasing a player is free and returns nothing.
- The wage-to-transfer slider (Club tab, `src/game/logic/board.ts` `shiftWages`): each step right turns £10k a week of wage cap into £2M of budget, each step left the reverse; only within the wage room under the cap (or the budget), at most 25 steps either way, net steps kept in `finances.wageShift` and reset with the cap each summer. Cutting the cap costs the weekly surplus too (£10k a week less drips in), so the trade is a choice, not free money.
- Appearance and goal bonuses from contract talks are earned per match (`accrueBonuses`, `game.bonusDue`) and paid with the next weekly wages (`takeBonuses`): they count in `finances.wagesPaid` and the weekly flow like wages, and `finances.bonusesPaid` keeps the season's total. None due: the sums are exactly as before.

### See your squad: the dressing room, happiness, medical and the planner

Views over the existing state, added without a single new draw: seeded Seasons, Quick Drafts and Dailies play exactly as before (`src/game/__tests__/noMove.test.ts` pins hashes captured on `main` at 2bfa023 for three 38-matchweek Seasons, 30 Quick Drafts and 60 Dailies). Every rule is a pure function in `src/game/logic`; nothing here moves a result yet (the effects are Batch 3 of the FM-depth plan, with the `ENGINE_VERSION` bump).

| View | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Hierarchy (`dynamics.ts` `hierarchy`) | Influence = `(mentality − 60) × 0.6 + (min(age, 33) − 20, from 0) × 1.2 + (overall − 70) × 0.7 + 5 × seasons with the side (`careerStats`) + 0.2 × apps (to 38) + status (key 10, first team 6, rotation 2, backup 0, prospect −4)`. The captain is always first. Tiers from the top: 3 team leaders, 4 highly influential, 6 influential, the rest. Each row shows his two biggest reasons and his personality |
| Groups (`squadGroups`) | Every player in exactly one group, by the first that holds: two or more drafted from one club-season ("The 2017-18 Man City lot"), two or more from one club in other seasons ("The Man City old boys"), two or more of one nationality ("The Brazilians", or "The <nation> contingent"), then an age band (21 and under, 22 to 29, 30 and over). Largest first, ties by id. Each shows its average morale and its most influential member |
| Atmosphere (`atmosphereOf`) | `0.8 × morale weighted by tier (leaders 3, highly influential 2, influential 1.5, others 1) + 0.2 × clamp(50 + 20 × XI chemistry, 0, 100)`: Electric 80+, Good 65+, Steady 50+, Tense 35+, Toxic. Lines: the leaders' morale, the chemistry, anyone under 40 |
| Happiness (`happiness.ts`) | `SquadPlayer.moraleLog` (optional, user's squad only): every place that already moves morale records the change it applied, after the clamp, to one decimal: a result or cup tie (with the rating), the minutes against the status, the captain's lift, a promise, an event (its title), a talk, a status change, the Dynasty summer (which starts a new log). Changes that round to 0 are skipped; the newest `MORALE_LOG_CAP` (6) are kept, capped in `logMorale`; `normaliseGame` trims a longer one and drops one that is not a list. The player modal shows the log; the Dressing room lists the squad unhappiest first with the biggest cause in the direction of his net change |
| Medical (`medical.ts`) | Index = `(0.5 + proneness) × condition band (2 under 55, 1.45 under 70, 1.1 under 80, else 1) × tired legs (outfielders: fatigueInjuryFactor)`, the engine's own terms; per-match chance `2.1% × index`, training chance `0.6% × focus × (0.5 + proneness) × physio`. Low under 0.85, medium under 1.1, high from 1.1. Reasons: injury-prone (0.45+, very from 0.6), condition under 80, low stamina on tired legs, 240+ minutes over the last three recorded weeks (shown as a load warning: the engine feels minutes through condition), fitness training. Injured players are listed apart. The briefing warns about the riskiest high-band starter when his condition is under 80. Over 600 seeded matches high-band starters are hurt more than 1.5 times as often as low-band ones (about 3.6 times in the test) |
| Squad planner (`planner.ts`) | For each of the ten positions: everyone who can play there (natural or accomplished), rated there, best first; who is next in (the best available player outside the XI); a need on a formation position with fewer natural players than `max(2, places + 1)`, all naturals 30 or older, or all of them out of contract |
| Contracts overview (`contractsOverview`) | Years left, wage, status; expiring (the last year and not renewed, declined, or a loan) first; the wages leaving in the summer and the wages committed |
| Personality (`personality.ts`) | The first rule that holds: model professional (professionalism 0.70+, consistency 0.62+), temperamental (professionalism under 0.46, consistency under 0.5), mercurial (big match 0.68+, consistency under 0.5), big-game player (big match 0.66+), professional (professionalism 0.68+), steady (consistency 0.68+), fickle (consistency under 0.43), casual (professionalism under 0.46), driven (mentality 80+), balanced. Shown in the player modal and the hierarchy |
| League awards (`leagueAwards.ts`) | `computeAwards` over the user's squad and every league rival's squad states (all competitions), at the summary: player of the season, golden boot, playmaker, golden glove, young player and the team of the season, the user's players marked. Derived, never saved; `game.awards` (the squad's own, which `finishSeason` parses) is unchanged |
| Inbox actions (`inboxActions.ts`) | `NewsItem.playerId` (optional) names the player an injury, ban, promise or player event is about (team-wide events such as press conferences name nobody). An item gets at most two buttons that open existing screens: Offer a new contract (the pending renewal decision for him), Medical (injured), Pick the XI (banned), Rest him (a starter under 70 condition: the player modal and its bench button), Talk to him (events, or morale under 55: the player modal), Contracts; items without a player go to the Market, Cup, Club, Planner or Dressing room |

Monthly awards (manager and player of the month) are not built: they need a per-matchweek rating record the states do not keep.

### Recruitment: scouting, contract talks, agents, the board and the data hub

FM-depth batch 2. Every rule is a pure function in `src/game/logic/{scouting,contracts,board,dataHub}.ts`, every field is optional and absent until the manager uses it, and no default path draws differently: `noMove.test.ts` (three Seasons, 30 Quick Drafts, 60 Dailies) and `marketPin.test.ts` (200 engine windows and two game January windows with no focus, hashed on `main` at 3c577bc) hold, and it needed no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump.

| System | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Knowledge (`scouting.ts`) | Per league, 0 to 100: your own league starts at 70 (`OWN_LEAGUE_KNOWLEDGE`; you know it, not every era of it), any other at 30 + 20 × scout tier. An assignment adds 5 a week (`ASSIGNMENT_GAIN`, to 100); without one a league fades 2 a week (`KNOWLEDGE_FADE`) back to its starting level. A chief scout runs 1 + tier assignments, over the leagues the market draws from. Stored in `game.knowledge` only above the starting level |
| Masked ranges | A player outside the squad, not fully scouted and not in the league season you play in (you face those clubs every week) shows each attribute, and his overall, as a range of width `max(2, round((100 − knowledge) / 5))`, exact at 100. Where the true value sits inside is `floor(hash(playerId, attribute) × (width + 1))` below it (FNV-1a, `maskRange`), so the same player always shows the same range and nothing is drawn; the range always holds the true value. Hidden traits and the injury risk read Unknown. Market listings, approaches, the shortlist, scout reports and the scout report modal are masked; your own squad never |
| Full scout | One a market day while a window is open, otherwise one a matchweek (`scoutSlot`). He shows exactly from then on (`game.scouted`, last 80), with his hidden traits as pros (Consistent, Ironman, Big-game player, Model professional) and cons (Streaky, Injury prone, Maverick) and his personality |
| Recruitment focus | Position (natural), age band (21 or under, 24 or under, 25 to 29, 30 or over), highest asking price at the market's level (×1.05) and loan (loanable) or permanent; any part may be left open. When set, `openWindow` and every trickle (the daily new listings, the scouts' extra listings, the January and summer openings) list only players who fit (`MarketContext.focus`); approaches still reach anyone. With no focus the market is untouched. Over 200 seeded windows at least 80% of listings fit (all of them, in practice) |
| Scout reports | With a focus set the scouts file 2 reports a matchweek (and 2 at once when the focus is set), drawn by `openWindow` on `Rng.fromState(game.rngState).fork('scout-<year>-<mw>')`, a fork of a copy: the game's stream does not move. Never a player already ours, already reported or already listed. Graded on what the scouts can see (the masked midpoints, rated at the focus position) against the weakest starter in that position (or the best cover): A 3 or more better, B within 1, C within 5, D below. The 12 newest are kept, each posted to the Inbox |
| Contract talks (`contracts.ts`) | From the player modal, any time, any squad player not on loan. His ask at the offered status = `max(current wage × status ratio, market wage × WAGE_MULT[status])` with market wage `5 × value^0.85`; × 0.9 at 31 or over, × 1.05 at 23 or under; × 0.95 at morale 75 or more, × 1.15 under 45; × 1.15 greedy, × 0.9 loyal; + 10% a status step below his current one. Agent mood is a hash of the player: about 30% greedy, 45% fair, 25% loyal. Greedy locks the wage at 90% of the ask; loyal takes no release clause (off the table). An offer's worth in £k a week = wage + signing fee × 1000 / (52 × seasons) × (1.2 greedy, 1, 0.8 loyal) + appearance bonus × expected share × 0.8 + goal bonus × goals a week by position (striker 0.45 to keeper 0) × 0.7 + a release clause's worth (10% of the ask at 1.5 × value or less, 5% at 3 ×, 2% above; × 1.5 greedy) + 4% of the ask a season beyond two for a man of 30 or more. Accepted when nothing excluded is used, every lock is met, the length is 1 to 5 seasons (2 at 33 or more) and the worth reaches the ask: monotone, raising the wage never turns a yes into a no. A refusal costs 5 morale; the third closes talks for 4 matchweeks. Agreed: years, status and clauses replace the contract (a renewal or a declined flag is cleared), the wage goes into `customPlayers`, the signing fee comes out of the budget (+4 morale). The renewal event is unchanged |
| Release clauses | In a window, for each of your players with a clause, overall 68 or more, a clause at most 1.1 × his asking price: an AI club pays it with chance `min(0.45, 0.12 + 0.3 × (price / clause − 1))` a market day, drawn from a fork of a copy of the stream (`releaseClauses` in marketOps.ts). He is sold for the clause, no say. Without a clause nothing runs |
| Agents and finding a buyer | His agent says whether he would leave (unhappy, below his natural status, or listed). Find a buyer (`contract.shopped`) adds 0.4 to his desirability in the daily offer check and uses the listed rate (12% × desirability); a settled player resents it (−4 morale). The agent takes 5% of the fee when he is sold |
| Sell-on | A bid may offer the selling club 10% or 20% of his next sale. They count it as `value × share × 0.6` towards their price (`placeBid`'s sweetener); when you sell him on, the share goes back to them out of the fee (`contract.sellOn`) |
| Board requests (`board.ts`) | Transfer funds (+ wage cap / 150, at least £5M; condition: the wage bill within 95% of the cap), a backroom budget (+£60k a week on the cap; at least £10M in the bank) and, in Dynasty, youth facilities (+1 academy prospect a summer, at most 2; at least £20M in the bank). Granted at board confidence 60 or more with the condition met, no dice; refused otherwise for −2 confidence. One request per 8 matchweeks (`season.requests`) |
| Data hub (`dataHub.ts`) | Every full-detail match of yours (league and cups; not friendlies) adds each attempt's `shotXg` to the shooter and, for an assisted goal, the same xG to the assister as xA (`game.dataHub`, outside `seasonStats`, reset each summer). The Stats tab shows minutes, goals, xG, goals minus xG, xA and goals and assists per 90; every 5 matchweeks the analyst posts the biggest over- and under-performer with 6 or more attempts |

### The manager's office (Football Manager depth, round 8)

The Dugout's Office tab (`src/ui/screens/OfficeTab.tsx`) gathers the man-management that Football Manager is known for, over modules that sit beside contract talks, the one-to-ones and the market rather than inside them: `src/game/logic/office.ts` (the tick, the transfer list, what is waiting on the manager), `officeCore.ts`, `promises.ts`, `transferRequests.ts`, `conversations.ts`, `pressConference.ts` and `transferTalks.ts`. Everything is kept in `Game.office` (optional, absent until first used) by real player id, so a Dynasty summer, which gives every man a new player-season id, needs no remap; a man who leaves is dropped from it at the next tick. None of these modules is reached by the edge verifier's bundle.

**How the no-move rule is kept.** The store runs `officeTick` after every action (`mutate`) and at every simulated week's end (`withTick` around Sim 5, Sim to end and Sim to the next big match, and the sliced runner's `WEEK_END`); it is a hook the office's module registers when it loads (the Dugout's chunk, or an `import()` the first time a game with office state changes), and every part of the tick catches up idempotently. With no office state and player power off it returns before reading anything, so a game nobody manages through the office is byte for byte the game it was (`office.test.ts` plays Seasons both ways and compares the whole game). Every roll the office makes is `Rng.fromState(game.rngState).fork('office-<label>')` of a copy, never written back: a tick never moves `rngState`, so no match, market day or event draw moves. Fee and counter prices are pure hashes (`hashUnit`), conversations and the press have no dice at all.

**Squad status** (section 10, "Squad status and playing time") is the expectation every other system reads: the share of the minutes a key player (85%), first-team man (65%), rotation player (35%), backup (15%) or prospect (10%) expects. The Office's Squad status box shows each man's status, what he expects and what he has played over the last six matchweeks, with a List or Unlist button (`setListed`: listing a man who did not ask to go costs him 4; one who asked this season is glad, +5).

**Promises.** Made in a conversation or in answer to a transfer request, at most four running:

| Promise | Kept when | Due |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Football (a regular place 55%, more football 30%) | his minutes since the promise reach the share, 5 points of slack | six matchweeks on (the season's last at the latest) |
| A new contract | a new contract signed in talks after it was made (any length; r9: a shorter one counted as broken before), or one that runs longer, or a renewal; refused for a man already renewed this season or already on the longest deal he would sign (two seasons from 33, five below) | eight matchweeks on |
| A signing | a man who plays the position whose best natural man rates lowest in the shape, at that man's rating + 2 or better | the end of the next window to close (refused when no window is left this season) |

Made, he is lifted (football +5, contract +4, signing +3), 40% less for each promise broken to him before. Kept, +6 and one broken promise forgiven. Broken, −14, his group in the dressing room −2 (the whole squad −1 when he is one of the top two tiers of the hierarchy), the broken count goes up, and he hands in a transfer request. Promises of an earlier season are dropped at the summer. So is a transfer request an earlier season left waiting (r9): only this season's request stops him lodging a second.

**Players who want out.** A transfer request comes when a promise is broken, and, with **player power** on (`GameSettings.playerPower`, a Season or Dynasty option on Setup's More options and the Office's switch, off by default), on his own. Each week from matchweek 3 the office weighs every man (`requestPressure`, pure): 0.6 for minutes more than 30 points short of his status over four or more recorded weeks, 0.5 for morale under 35 (0.8 under 25), 0.6 for a bid of 80% of his value or more turned down this window, 0.4 for an ambitious (driven, big-game or a greedy agent) key or first-team man in the bottom third from matchweek 8, 0.3 for each promise broken to him; ambition multiplies it by 1.4, a model professional by 0.6, a loyal agent by 0.7. From 0.5 his weekly chance is a fifth of it, at most 40%, rolled on `office-wantout-<year>-<week>-<player>`; at most one new request a week (the lowest roll against its chance), none within eight matchweeks of his last one settling. Handing it in costs him 3.

The manager answers in the Office (or after three matchweeks at the desk, weeks inside Sim 5 or Sim to end not counted, the request counts as refused at half the cost, −5):

| Answer | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| Talk him round | the odds by his reason (morale 45%, results 35%, minutes 30%, interest 25%, a broken promise 15%) + 0.3 × (professionalism − 0.5) − 0.15 a broken promise, 5% to 95%; won, he withdraws it (+5), lost, −3; two lost talks and he hears no more |
| Promise football / a new contract | makes the promise, then the same roll with 85% (40% when the reason is not football or morale) or 70% (40%) as the base |
| List him | he goes on the transfer list (+5) and offers come as for any listed man; taken off it again, it counts as refused (−6) |
| Refuse | −10 for him and the dressing-room ripple of −2 |

The Office also lists the restless (pressure 0.3 and up) with their main reason, so a conversation or a promise can head a request off.

**Conversations** (`conversations.ts`) add four topics to the one-to-ones of `talks.ts`, sharing their one conversation a week per player. How he takes a hard word follows his personality (model professional and professional take it well, steady and driven fairly, big-game and balanced evenly, mercurial and fickle a little badly, casual and temperamental badly):

| Topic | Answers |
| --- | --- |
| His form | Encourage him (+2 to +5); Demand more (a thick skin −1 and +5 sharpness, a thin one −7, after a good run −8) |
| Playing time | Explain where he stands (+2 when he is getting his share or takes it well, −2 to −5 otherwise); Demand more (a thick skin +4 sharpness, otherwise −3 to −6); Make a promise (football) |
| Squad status | Explain (+3 when his status matches where he ranks, −2 to −6 when he sees himself higher); Raise his status (the Club tab's status change, its morale and wage); Make a promise (a contract) |
| His future | Sell the project (+4, +6 for the driven and big-game, when the side sits in the top half or before matchweek 4; −1 or −4 otherwise); Make a promise (a signing) |

**Press conferences** (`pressConference.ts`), optional and once a matchweek while there is a match ahead, beside the post-match conferences of the event deck. Up to three questions, chosen by a pure hash of the seed, year and week from those that apply: the next opponent, a good run (two wins in three) or a bad one (two defeats), a man who wants out, the board (confidence under 50), the best prospect, the title race (top three from matchweek 10). Each answer moves every squad man's morale, the subject's (a big-game player +2 more on praise, a temperamental one −2 more on a hard line) and board confidence by fixed amounts (`PRESS_ANSWERS`, at most ±3 each). Skipped, nothing changes. Before a big match (round 9: `bigMatchOf`, the match stops of `nextStop`: a derby, a title decider, a title or survival run-in, a run-in decider, a cup semi-final or final) the press asks about it first, in place of the opponent question: for a league match "We will win it" (squad +2), "We relish games like this" (squad +2, board +1) or "Three points like any other" (squad +1); for a tie "We relish it", "One step at a time" (squad +1) or "Nobody expects anything of us" (squad −1, board +1). "We will win it" is a boast the press holds the manager to (`Game.office.boast`, settled by the office's tick once that matchweek is played, `settleBoast`): a win lifts every man 2 and the board 3, a draw passes, a defeat costs every man 1 and the board 4, with an Inbox item either way. No draws.

**Transfer talks** (`transferTalks.ts`), beside the market's plain bid, which stays as it was. Buying is two stages, from the Negotiate button on any listing (or the Office's Transfer talks box):
1. *The fee.* Offer a fee, a sell-on share (10% or 20%, worth `sellOnValue` to them now) and up front or in two or three instalments (worth 94% or 90% of the fee to them; 50% or 40% paid now, the rest in equal parts as each later window opens; in a Season, whatever is owed is settled at the final matchweek). Their lowest price is 86% to 98% of the asking price by a hash of the listing and the window, 5% more with rivals in. At or over it they accept; at 75% or more they counter with the fee that meets it in the same structure, 3% more each round; under that they laugh it off (two rounds' worth). Three rounds and they end talks for the window. Walk away at any time.
2. *Personal terms.* He asks his value's market wage (or what he earns, if more) times the status multiplier, 10% more for the move, 10% less at 31 and over, 5% more at 23 and under, his agent's mood (greedy 15% more, loyal 10% less), and a signing-on fee of the agent's cut (8%, 5% or 3% of the fee; a greedy agent will not go under it). He signs for 1 to 5 seasons (3 at 30 and over, 2 at 33 and over) and refuses a status beneath his standing in the squad (`acceptsStatus`). An offer is worth its wage plus the signing-on fee above the agent's cut spread over the contract's weeks. Three turned-down offers and he stays where he is. Agreed, the deal goes through the market's own bid at the price now due, which an asking price of exactly that accepts with no draw; then his wage, years, sell-on and fee are written in and any instalments booked (`Game.office.instalments`).

Selling: every bid for your players can be countered. The club's ceiling is 8% to 30% over its bid by a hash of the bid: ask within it and they pay; up to 20% over and they come back once with a final offer at the ceiling (accept or reject it as any bid); further over and they walk.

**The Inbox** shows what waits in the office (requests, agreed fees waiting on personal terms, promises due within two matchweeks) above the news, each opening the Office; the tab carries the same count as a badge. Every item the office posts carries `NewsItem.office` (round 9): the Inbox's Office filter (shown once the game has office state) lists them with an In the Office button, and the "while you were away" digest of Sim 5 or Sim to end groups them under In the office (requests handed in, promises kept or broken, a boast settled; its transfer items stay under transfers).

**At a glance** (round 9, `src/game/logic/officeView.ts`, display only: pure reads that never write, not even `officeOf`, and draw nothing). Each answer to a request shows what it risks beside its button (`requestRisks`): the odds a talk or a promise changes his mind, his morale if it works (+5) or fails (−3), the promise it makes and its lift now, the +5 of listing him, the −10 of a refusal and the team-mates who would feel it (`rippleTargets`, the same reckoning as `dressingRoomRipple`), and how many matchweeks at the desk are left before it counts as refused. His view (`playerRead`): his morale, personality, how he takes a hard word, his agent's mood and whether he trusts your word (kept promises) or is wary (broken ones). Each promise has a clock (`promiseClock`): the matchweeks left (amber at two, red at one), and for football whether it is on course, behind (with the minutes a match he still needs) or out of reach. Each press answer shows its exact effect (`pressEffect`, the subject's personality included), the board's mood sits on the press box, and before a big match the press's line (display only, picked by a hash of the week). Conversations name what each tone risks. The player card's Happiness view says what a kept or broken promise was (`promiseLineKind`) and lists the promises to him with their clocks. `src/game/__tests__/officeView.test.ts` plays a Season and a Dynasty through its summer with all of it read every week and ends byte for byte the games never read.

### Inbox

A rolling feed of up to `NEWS_CAP` (120) items, newest first: results, injuries, suspensions, board, transfers, awards, events, cup, training, info. Every item goes through `postNews` (`src/game/logic/news.ts`), which trims the feed to the cap and counts the item in `game.newsTotal`, the number of items ever posted this season (an optional save field; an older save starts it from the feed's length). `game.newsSeen` (optional) is `newsTotal` when the Inbox tab was last opened; `beginSeason` sets `newsTotal` to the opening items and `newsSeen` to 0, so a new season's opening news is unread, and a save without `newsSeen` counts as everything read. The unread count is `newsTotal − newsSeen`, measured on the counter rather than the feed's length because the feed stops growing at the cap. The Inbox tab shows that count as a badge (`99+` above 99; it takes the place of the pending-event dot). Opening the tab writes `newsSeen` (only when something is unread or the field is missing, so a visit to a read inbox never rewrites the save), marks the unread rows, says `n new since your last visit`, and draws a divider under the last new item when older items follow it; a batch that arrives while the tab is open (a sim run from it) moves the marking to that batch.

### The Dugout at a glance (display only)

Pure readings in `src/game/logic/squadDisplay.ts`; nothing draws a number or changes the game, so no ENGINE_VERSION bump.

| Piece | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Form arrow | Mean of the last three `recentRatings` against `ratingSum / apps`: above by more than `FORM_BAND` (0.3) ▲ in `--good`, below by more ▼ in `--danger`, else ▶ in `--text-3`. None before three ratings and three appearances. |
| Sparkline | The last five ratings on a fixed 5 to 9 scale (40 × 14), dots ≥ 7.5 `--good`, under 6 `--danger`, else `--meter-mid`; hidden on a phone. |
| Squad views | Overview (apps, goals, assists, average), Form (last rating, average, MOTM), Fitness (condition, stamina, minutes over the last three matchweeks from `recentMinutes`), Contract (years or loan, wage, status, value); per viewer under `touchline-squad-view` (the old `touchline.squad.stamina` on opens Fitness). |
| Role fit | His effective rating in the role (condition and form included) against his overall: within 1 Suits, up to 4 below Fair, more Poor. Each role option shows `roleRating` there, rounded. The panel head shows the XI's mean familiarity. |
| Next match | The opposition report, then `matchUp`: our attack on their defence, midfield on midfield, our defence and keeper on their attack, overall; more than `EVEN_BAND` (1.5) either way is Edge or Behind. The team talk moved here from the Squad tab. |
| Season calendar | One cell a matchweek (`seasonStrip`): opponent, venue, W/D/L from the score, cup ties after it, upcoming cells tinted by the opponent's band today (top four, bottom four). Scores only, so a compacted save draws the same. |
| Position chart | `positionHistory` recomputes the table after every matchweek with a result; the Table tab draws ours with the two clubs placed nearest now and the board's target dashed. |
| Inbox filters | Matches (result, cup), Market (transfer), Board (board, info), Squad (the rest). A Needs you row is pinned while an event is pending or offers are open. |
| Window | "Window shuts in N days" (market days after today) on the Market head and the next-match box, red on the last day; on the last day a ticker of the newest five log lines; Done deals lists signings (the bid, or the loan fee) and accepted offers. |
| Hover card | `PlayerPeek` after 350 ms of a mouse over a name (depth chart, contracts, league stats): rating, positions, age, club-season, the sparkline, arrow and morale face in a squad, and `topAttributes` (the best three of the eight classic attributes; a keeper's goalkeeping first, never an outfielder's). Not on market rows, where scouting masks the numbers. |
| Pick grades | `pickGrades` (`src/game/logic/pickGrades.ts`), on a complete draft (blind mode included, `ratingShown(rules, 'grades', { complete })`): each XI pick's rating in his slot against the best there in the spun club-season's squad, both by `inSlotRating` (the pitch disc's `discRating`, overall less the out-of-position cost, so the recap's "84 at CB" is the 84 on his disc; before round 4 of 2026-10-26 it was `ratingAt`, the attributes weighted for the position, and the recap could print 84 beside an 88 on the pitch), every man resolved through the game's lookup as the pick is (under Prime ratings at his career best; before 2026-10-26 the rest of the squad was read at its season rating, so grades ran high), leaving out the men drafted on other spins. Since round 5 (2026-10-26) a pick is measured against the best play his spin offered, not the best man for his own slot: `left` is how many XI rating points the pick gave away, the best XI the open slots could still hold from this spin and the spins after it (each later spin filling one slot with its best man there, an exact search over the open slot sets, as the Hindsight XI reasons) against the best with your man in your slot; `best`, `bestName` and `bestSlotId` name the best play. Position First, Crazy, the salary cap and the teammate chain count only the slot filled (the gap to the best man the spin held there). `gradeFor(left)`: 0 an A, up to 2 a B, up to 4 a C, more a D (before round 5: the gap to the best man in his slot, within 1 an A, 3 a B, 6 a C, so the highest rating who fits graded A on 99% of picks). The Icons case, bench picks and re-spins are not graded. It reads the draft log only, pure and draw-free. |
| Draft recap | `draftRecap` (`src/game/logic/draftRecap.ts`, `components/DraftRecap.tsx`) fills the Draft's middle box once the draft is complete (where an empty "All picks made" stood), unless a manager pick is on offer there: the draft's grade, the mean of the pick grades (A 4 to D 1; 3.7 and up an A, 3.2 a B, 2.5 a C, below that a D, `draftGrade`; 3.5, 2.75 and 2 before round 5), a verdict (`recapVerdict`: "A perfect draft" only when every graded pick is an A), the grade tally, the best pick (`bestPick`), the one that got away (the pick that left the most on the table, 2 XI points or more, with the play it passed up), the clubs, nationalities and seasons drafted from and the graded XI's mean rating in its slots, and the `draft-recap` coach mark the first time. The next step's button (`finishDraftLabel`) is the left box's only (the recap's own copy went, round 3), so the main action is in one place. With no graded pick (a run-in's reinforcements, which go to the squad rather than a slot, or grades hidden) it reads "Squad ready" and lists the picks instead (`ungraded`: position, name, his overall unless ratings are hidden, season), so the box is never empty. It reads the draft log only, like `pickGrades`. The share text and the share card of a Quick Draft, Daily or Weekly carry the same grade (`draftGradeLine`: "Draft grade B", an accent line under the record on the card); the Hindsight XI's gap stays out of the share (the owner's decision). |
| Grade calibration (round 5) | Measured over 200 seeded auto-drafts per ratings mode (England, 4-3-3, `QUICK_RULES`; `autoDraft`, the highest rating who fits, spin by spin). Before: picks 99% A, drafts 100% A. After, Season ratings: picks A 1520, B 426, C 190, D 64 (69/19/9/3%), drafts A 57, B 120, C 23, D 0 (28/60/12/0%); Prime ratings: picks A 1493, B 426, C 195, D 86, drafts A 56, B 117, C 27. A pick at random from what fits grades a D. `pickGrades.test.ts` pins it statistically: the assistant's mean draft grade between 2.7 and 3.4 points, A for under half, every pick grade in use, a random draft a full grade lower or worse. |
| Hindsight XI | `hindsightXI` (`src/game/logic/hindsight.ts`, the recap's `hindsight`, `HindsightXI` in `DraftRecap.tsx`): the best XI the same spins could have given had you known them all. The XI spins (every history row with a pick in a slot, not the Icons case, the bench or re-spins) are matched to the slots you filled, each filled with the man from that squad who rates best there (`inSlotRating`, the disc's number, through the game's lookup), by an exact search over the 2^n slot sets (n ≤ 11) for the highest total; a tie keeps your own pick in your own slot. A man on two spun squads plays once: he is banned from one spin (never the one you drafted him from) and the search runs again. Your own slots with each spin's best man are always one of the XIs it weighs, so it is never below yours. Shows its mean rating, yours and the gap, how many of your picks it keeps where you put them (ticked) and each other man's spin; "You made the most of every spin" when the gap is 0. Null with grades hidden, under the salary cap (`rules.budget`) or the teammate chain (`rules.chain`), where the spins could not be rearranged freely. Pure, no draws. |
| Squad complete's report | The third box (manager, tactics) ends with `squadReport` (`src/game/logic/squadReport.ts`): the key man and the weak link by the number on his disc (`discRating`), the weak link only when 3 or more under the XI's mean; who is not natural in his slot, worst fit first; the mean age, starters 23 or under and 32 or over (`VETERAN_AGE`); pairs from the same club-season, clubs and nations. With ratings hidden no key man or weak link. The manager line adds `managerEdge` ("On your XI: +1.5% goals · −1.0% conceded", the same `managerBoost` the season applies, moving with the tactics) and `edgeHint` (a youth manager's young starters, or that the instructions suit him). Pure. |
| Manager spin's guide | Under the reels, "What a manager brings": all six styles with `MANAGER_STYLE_HELP`, each style's `managerEdge` (`src/game/logic/managerEdge.ts`) on the XI with the current instructions and its `edgeHint`; the landed styles lit once all three land, and each landed card shows its edge. Before a reel lands its card waits in place, dashed ("Reel 1"). |
| Case screen's guide | Beside the reel, "Your locker" with the count of each power-up (`caseGuide`, `src/game/logic/caseGuide.ts`, through `lockerCount`); under the odds, "What each one does", rarest first (`POWER_UP_INFO` lines, `CASE_ICON_LINE` for the Icon). |
| Pending decision | A dressing-room event waiting on the manager sits above the tabs as one strip on a desktop: the title, the story cut to the room left, the choices (their hints as tooltips) on the same line when they fit; More opens the whole story, the player's line and every hint. Play stays locked until it is decided. A phone shows the whole card. |
| Keys | Space plays the matchweek (not while a decision is pending), 1 to 9 and 0 open the first ten tabs, and each tab has a letter that opens it wherever it sits (`TAB_KEYS`: S Squad, N Next match, T Tactics, I Inbox, O Office, D Dressing room, P Planner, M Market, F Fixtures, L Table, C Cup, E Europe, H Stats, B Club; A and R stay the assistant's: pick the XI, rest the tired); every tab names its keys in a tooltip and `aria-keyshortcuts`, and an Info beside the tabs lists them on a desktop.
