- **Cup nights after the league** (`src/game/logic/quickCups.ts`, `src/ui/components/CupRuns.tsx`; Quick Draft only, never the Daily or a managed season). Once the league season is over and closed (not `quickOpen`), the summary offers two post-season cups, each with "Play" and "Skip it": **the Cup** (`buildAiClubs`, the league's own clubs) and, after a finish in the top `QUICK_CONTINENTAL_PLACES = 7`, **the Continental Cup** (`buildContinentalClubs`, the fifteen strongest clubs of the other leagues that season; the store loads every league pack first, and the card is offered while they are still unloaded; Skip it loads them the same way, `leaguesBeforeSkip` in `src/game/logic/cupSkip.ts`, since 2026-10-26: before that, with only the Premier League in memory, Skip only toasted "Too few clubs from the other leagues are loaded"). `playQuickCup(game, u, comp)` draws sixteen entrants with `drawFirstRound` (the user always among them), plays every tie of a round, the field's too, and draws the next from the winners (`drawNextRound`) through the round of 16, quarter-final and semi-final to a final at a neutral ground. **The Continental Cup from engine 7** plays the Season's format instead (`playQuickContinental`, `POST_SEASON_PLAN`): the sixteen drawn into four groups of four (the user heading the second pot), six matchdays home and away, the quarter-finals fixed by the groups, two-legged quarter- and semi-finals (aggregate, no away goals, extra time and penalties after the second leg) and a one-off neutral final; the field plays on to the final whatever the user does. It is kept as `QuickCupRun.groups` (`QuickGroup`: the four sides and every match as `[matchday, home, away, goals, goals]`) with `bracket` from the quarter-finals and `legs` on each two-legged tie (`QuickCupTie.legs` from the user's side, `QuickBracketTie.legs` from the tie's), `reached` `G` for out in the groups; the user's knockout scorers cover both legs and extra time. The cup night opens on the draw, fills the four group tables a matchday a step (`components/GroupTables.tsx`), then reveals the bracket tie by tie, and afterwards switches between Groups and Knockouts; the card under the summary says the group finish (`quickGroupLine`) above the ties, each with its legs. A run played before engine 7 (no `groups`) shows as it was. A level tie goes to extra time (`extraTime` in `src/engine/season.ts`: each side's goals a Poisson draw from its match xG times `EXTRA_TIME_RATE = 0.85 / 3`; the user's extra-time goals go to his XI, weighted to the front line, minutes 91 to 120) and then a shoot-out (`shootout`). The user's side is the XI and tactics the league ended with (`quickCupXI`; no substitutes when a bench played between games, and then a starter still injured after the last league game is covered from the bench by `fieldXI`, drawing nothing), the AI clubs carry the league's calibration. The user's run ends when he goes out; the rest of the field then plays on to the final, so the bracket has a winner. **Determinism:** each cup draws from `Rng.fromState(game.rngState).fork('quick-cup' | 'quick-continental')`, a fork of a copy of the state the league ended on, and never writes `Game.rngState`: the league result, the RNG state and the other cup are identical whether a cup is played, skipped, or played first or second (tested), so no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump. The ties played after the user goes out draw from the same fork after every draw his run took, so they move nothing he played: `cupBracket.test.ts` pins the runs, achievements and RNG state of sixteen seeded seasons to their values on main at 238b040, from before the bracket was kept. **Saved** as `QuickResult.cup` and `QuickResult.continentalCup` (`QuickCupRun`: the user's `QuickCupTie`s with round, opponent, home or neutral, the score over the whole tie, `et` goals, `pens`, scorers, won; and `reached`, R16 to F or W), `null` for a skipped cup, absent while on offer and on older saves (which are therefore offered the cups). `QuickCupRun.bracket` (optional) keeps every tie of every round in the order drawn (`QuickBracketTie`: the two sides, `USER_TEAM` for the user, the goals over the whole tie, `et`, `pens`, the winner), written at play time with no draw of its own; a run from before it has none and shows the user's path alone. **Shown** as a bracket (`quickCupBracket` in `src/game/logic/bracket.ts`, drawn by `components/Bracket.tsx` inside the cup night, `components/CupNight.tsx`): "Play the Cup" plays the whole cup in the store, then opens a modal on the draw; the ties land round by round (`revealSteps`: the user's tie first in its round, then the others top to bottom; a round's sides show when the reveal reaches it, so the route is never given away), the user's tie held "In play" for about a second and a half before its score lands and for a moment after (`CUP_REVEAL` in `src/ui/cupReveal.ts`: 340 ms a tie, 200 ms once the user is out, 1400 ms for his tie, an 850 ms hold after it, 650 ms more to open each round; slowed by about 40 % at the owner's request on 2026-10-27, so a sixteen-club cup the user wins takes about thirteen seconds; about nine seconds for a whole run), with "Skip to the end". A won final ends on the trophy lifted (`CupLift`: our own cartoon cup, `components/TrophyArt.tsx`, rising on a gold flare and a burst of `confettiPieces`), and the second cup of a league-winning season on the Treble (the three trophies under a crown, a bigger flare, two bursts); "See the bracket" goes back to it, Done closes. Reduced motion shows the whole bracket at once and the trophy without confetti. The card then lists the user's ties, newest at the top, the trophy line and "See the bracket". The summary banner holds each cup's "Cup winners" or "Continental champions" chip and share line (tracked per cup, so both can reveal at once; `cupLine`: "🏆 Cup winners", "🌍 Continental Cup: semi-finals"; a Cup run out in its first round reads "fourth round", the name its tie carries, and a Continental run "round of 16" (`cupReachLabel(reach, comp)`); the tier line gains "+ CUP" or "+ EUROPE") back until the run has been revealed. Winning earns the `cup`, `continental`, `double` and `treble` achievements (`cupUnlocks`) and the trophy cabinet counts the cup and the double or treble it completes (`addCupTrophies`, through `creditCupRun`, which also files the season on the honours board). **The Treble and the Double** (`honourOf` in `cabinet.ts`): the league with both cups is the Treble, the league with one cup a Double ("The Double" for the league and Cup, "League and Continental double" otherwise; the cabinet's `doubles` count stays league and Cup, as before). Once its cups are revealed the summary banner shows a gold Treble chip with a crown (`banner-treble`) or a Double chip (`banner-double`), the share text gains `honourLine` ("👑 The Treble: League, Cup and Continental Cup", "🏆 The Double: League and Cup") and the share card a gold line under the record ("The Treble · League, Cup and Continental Cup"). A Season or Dynasty treble shows the same, from the season's own cups. **Challenge links** carry how far each played cup went (`ChallengeClaim.cu`, `eu`; older builds ignore them): `replayChallenge` plays the same cups on the replayed season and reports a run that does not replay as not verified; the head-to-head shows both sides' cups (`claimCupsLine`). The old in-league Continental Cup run (`GameSettings.continental` on a quick game, top four, `QuickResult.europe`) is no longer played; a season saved with one keeps its panel and is not offered a second. A cup won without the league has a gold line on the share card too (`cupWinsLine`: "Cup winners", "Continental champions"), since 2026-10-27.
- **The 38-0 chase** (`src/game/logic/perfect.ts`, pure, display only). The goal of a Quick Draft is the flawless season, every league game won. Setup's bottom bar and the kick-off screen state it ("Win every game", the target `perfectTarget(games)`, 38-0 or 34-0 or 36-0 by the league). The pundits' prediction adds the chance to win every game (`PreSeasonOdds.perfect`) and to go unbeaten (`unbeaten`), counted by `sampleSeasons` from the draws it already makes, so the odds' stream is unchanged. The playback carries a chase badge from `chaseAt(results, upTo)` and `chaseLine`: "Chasing 38-0", then "Still perfect: 12-0" (with how many are left), and on the game that ends it a red flash and "Perfect run over at 12-0" (or "in game 1") over "Unbeaten: 15" while unbeaten, then "Unbeaten run ended at 15". The summary leads with the record against 38-0 (`recordLabel` "31-4-3", "against 38-0", `versusPerfectLine` "7 wins short", "Unbeaten, 8 draws short" or "The perfect season", and `runsLine`). The Profile's "The 38-0 chase" panel keeps `Profile.quickBests` (`withQuickBests` at `recordResult`: the best record by wins, then fewer defeats, then points; the longest perfect run from the first game; the perfect seasons count), optional and filled by `normaliseProfile` in `save.ts` from the store's `merge`, so a profile saved before it reads as none yet. **Feasibility, measured on 2026-10-25** (`src/game/__tests__/perfectChase.test.ts`, the real engine, no retune): an elite XI (the best-rated man for each slot of a 4-3-3 across every Premier League season in the pack, one per human: De Gea, Evra, Van Dijk, Mascherano, Walker, Rodri, De Bruyne, Gerrard, Hazard, Haaland, Salah) against the 2025-26 league on Calibrated went 38-0 in 26 of 2,000 seeds (1.3%; shards of 500 gave 1.0 to 1.8%), unbeaten in 605 (30.3%), won the league in all 2,000 and averaged 34.0 wins. The pundits' sampler gives the same XI 2.2% and 33.1%. The suite runs 200 seeds (`PERFECT_SEEDS`, `PERFECT_OFFSET` run more); the elite XI is drawn from the standard seasons only, never the opt-in classic ones. Measured again on the packs of #38 (2008-14 positions and traits fixed, classic seasons added): 26 of 2,000 (1.3%, shards 1.2 to 1.4%), unbeaten 604 (30.2%), mean wins 34.0; the pundits say 2.3% and 35.4%. Since 2026-10-27 the playback's moment chip on the game that ends the perfect run names that game (`Lost 0-1 to Brentford`, `Drew 2-2 with Everton`: `brokeMoment` in `src/ui/screenText.ts`) instead of repeating the banner's "Perfect run over at 4-0" (the screen-reader line keeps both), and the summary's runs line says "your best perfect start" when this season set the best instead of printing the run twice.
- **The live league table and the position chart** (`src/game/logic/liveTable.ts`, pure; `src/ui/components/LiveLeague.tsx`). `runQuickSeason` keeps every club's league results by matchweek on the result (`QuickResult.rounds`, `packRounds`: the club ids and, per matchweek, `[home, away, homeGoals, awayGoals]` by index; about 4 KB of JSON; the January pause keeps the first half as `QuickJanuary.firstHalfRounds`), from fixtures already played, so nothing draws. `weeklyTables` folds them into the table after every matchweek in `computeTable`'s order (any league size: a classic 22-club season of 1992-93 to 1994-95 stores 42 matchweeks of eleven fixtures, no byes, and the target reads 42-0); the last equals the summary's final table and each week's user place equals the game's recorded position (tested over several seeds). Older saves have no rounds and show the game list only. Beside the game list the playback shows the live table after the game shown (every club, the user's row highlighted, kept in view inside its box; rows keep one DOM order and are placed by a transform, so a change of place slides, FLIP-style; up and down arrows since the game before via `movement`; tints and lines for the title, the top 4, the top 6 and the bottom three via `zoneOf`; position, badge, name, P, GD, Pts) and the position chart (`positionSeries`: 1st at the top, zone bands behind, the user's line thick in `--primary`, drawing its last segment on as each game lands, the best three other clubs now (`rivalsOf`) as faint lines). Moments (`momentAt`): "Top of the league!", "Into the top four", "15-0 and counting" every fifth win of a perfect run, and the red "Perfect run over" when it ends; with the gap chip (`gapLine`: "Top by 3 pts", "2 pts off top" in the top four, else "1 pt off 7th", the place above). Reduced motion turns the slides, the drawing and the pops off.
- **One screen on a desktop** (`.page.fit`, `.fit-grid`, `.fit-cell` in `styles.css`, inside the app shell `.app.shell` from 1100 px wide and 600 px tall). Squad complete (pundits' prediction | the XI | manager and tactics) and the playback and January (game list | live table | position chart, January and the bench) fill the window under the top bar, or all of it with the bar folded away, in boxed panels side by side; each panel scrolls inside itself and the page never scrolls. The quick summary takes the redesign's layout (`.page.summary`): the result banner on the left (the record against 38-0, the tier and its blurb, the stats, the trophies and manager chips, the actions), and on the right one box with the cups, the season game by game (newest first, folded to eight with "Show all"), January, the bench, the challenge, the record books, the XI, the awards, the table and the share text. Checked at 1280x720, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440 with the top bar shown and hidden. On a phone the boxes stack, and the playback's three views become tabs (Games, Table, Chart, which reads January while the window is open) under the tally. The live table's rows jump instead of sliding when the playback reveals games faster than the 400 ms slide (4x).
- **Names.** A quick season's screens show only the user's team name (`QuickTeamLine`: the name and the league's flag, "Touchline XI"), never "as" the club whose place it takes, and in Quick Draft no league-season text either (the kick-off, the playback header, the summary banner, the final table's heading and the pundits' note); the Daily keeps its season chip, Season and Dynasty their labels; `summariseSetup` leaves "as <club>" to Season and Dynasty, the summary drops the real club's finish, and the share card's subtitle carries the record instead.

- **The Profile page and Explore (display only, no draw).** Profile: the Manager box holds the name, the counts and a Career overview (`careerOverview` in `src/game/logic/careerOverview.ts`): win rate, points a game, finished first, over the league seasons kept on Best seasons (the best 50), the title rate from the cabinet's titles against every league season credited, results by mode from `Cabinet.byMode`, and the clubs most often replaced (twice or more, three at most); the theme, sound, match sounds, reel speed and Show tips again sit in their own Settings box (`settings` in Boxes). Achievements list the unlocked first, newest first, with an All / Unlocked / Locked switch (`orderAchievements`); the honours board shows the latest five, Show all for the rest. Explore: the search covers every league (the ones not loaded yet load on the first search), ignores accents and case, and lists each real player once at his best season with his span, season count and clubs, a position filter beside it (`searchPlayers` in `src/game/logic/exploreSearch.ts`, a word that starts the name first, then the peak rating, at most 100); Highest rated filters by season and can list each player once at his best season in that league (`topPlayerSeasons`); opening a career loads the other leagues so it shows every club he played for.
- **The player card as a profile (round 8, display only).** `src/game/logic/playerProfile.ts` over the engine's own functions, no draw: attributes in five tones (`attrTone`: 85 and up elite, 75 good, 65 fair, 50 poor, under 50 weak) with each group's average (`groupAverage`); the career table (`careerRows`: every season with his club's finish and the change in rating) and its summary; Best seasons (`bestSeasons`, the top five); Roles (`roleSuitability`: every role at the positions he plays naturally or can cover, rated by the engine's `roleRating`, the best eight); Similar players (`similarPlayers`: other players' seasons at the same first position within `SIMILAR_RANGE` (6) of his overall, one season per player, the closest over the twelve numbered attributes (`SIMILAR_KEYS`) first, the match 100 less twice the root-mean-square gap, six shown, classic seasons only with the opt-in). Explore's indexes (`src/game/logic/exploreIndex.ts`: `playerIndex`, `playerEntry`, `byRating`) are built once per universe in WeakMaps and warmed when idle (`warmExplore`); a name narrows them through `nameMatchesOf` (round 10: a query that narrows one of the last eight filters its answer, otherwise one string search over every folded name; the same rows as a pass over the whole index, tested over a thousand queries), and the search draws its rows a slice at a time (`src/ui/progressiveRows.ts`: 12, then 24 a paint once the list has stood 250 ms); its Records view (`exploreRecords.ts`, `leagueTrivia`) derives the climbs, falls, title runs, the longest serving, the most titles, rating jumps and the youngest and oldest rated 80 or more from the data.
- **Explore's deeper views (display only, no draw; round 7).** The view switch holds Seasons, Clubs, League, Top rated, Search and Compare. *Clubs*: a league's clubs, most titles first (`leagueClubs`), then the club's every season newest first with its real finish, points and W-D-L where the data has a table (a dash where it has none), the XI average and its chance on the wheel (`clubHistory`, `clubSummary`); beside it the squad of the season picked, or its best XI ever (`bestXIEver`: a 4-3-3, each real player once at his best season there for the slot, greedy over every player-season and slot he can cover, best slot rating first, equals to the earlier season, then the id). *League*: champions by season with the points and the gap to the runner-up (`leagueChampions`; none named in a league without tables), the three top-rated player-seasons of each season (`topRatedBySeason`) and the record holders (`leagueRecordBook` over `records.ts`'s complete tables of the league's usual length, 38 games in England, 34 in Germany, every holder named: most points, wins and goals, fewest conceded and defeats, best goal difference, fewest points, most conceded), the most titles in the data and the highest rated ever. *On the wheel*: the first spin of an untouched Quick Draft on the league's wheel (`QUICK_RULES`, every season, the Classic toggle's wheel for a classic club-season), read from the engine's exact `spinOdds` and shown as "1 in N first spins" and the club-season's share of the spins that land on its season (`wheelChances`, cached per universe; `src/game/logic/exploreDb.ts`). *Search*: any mix of a name, a nation (a drawn flag beside the picker and the ten commonest nations as flag chips), a league, a position (his first, or any of his), an age range and a rating range, one row per player at his best matching season or one per season, sorted by best match, rating, age, value, season or name either way, the first 200 shown (`filterPlayers`, `formFilter` in `src/game/logic/exploreFilter.ts`). *Compare*: up to three players (the columns icon in Search, the Seasons squad and on a career card; kept per browser in `touchline-explore-compare`), each with a season picker, the player card's rating-by-season chart and position map, and a side-by-side table of overall, age, value and every attribute in the card's groups with the leader marked (`compareRows`). Every classic season shown carries the estimated chip.
- **The player card profile and Explore's Records (display only, no draw; round 8).** Attribute tones: 85 and up elite, 75 good, 65 fair, 50 poor, under 50 weak (`attrTone`), and each column's head carries the mean of its numbered attributes (`groupAverage`). The career table reads each season's club finish from the club-season (complete tables only) and the change against his best rating of the season before (`careerRows`). Best seasons: one per season, best first, five (`bestSeasons`). Roles: every role of every position he plays naturally or can cover, rated by the engine's `roleRating` (the role's attribute weights blended 55/45 with his overall, times his fit), best first (`roleSuitability`); the head's "Best role" is the first. Similar: player-seasons with the same first position and an overall within six (`SIMILAR_RANGE`), the root-mean-square gap over the twelve numbered attributes, one season per player, shown as a match of 100 less twice the gap (`similarPlayers`; `src/game/logic/playerProfile.ts`). Records, per league, from the loaded data and labelled as derived from it: places climbed or lost between two seasons in a row with final tables, runs of titles, distinct seasons at one club, distinct title seasons per player, the biggest rise in rating between two seasons in a row of the same kind (never an estimate against an EA rating), and the youngest and oldest player-seasons rated 80 or more, one per player, eight lines each (`leagueTrivia` in `src/game/logic/exploreRecords.ts`).
- **The Stats tab's tables (display only, no draw; round 7).** Overview keeps the three boxes; Players is every man with an appearance in the league and all of the user's squad (apps, minutes, goals, assists, G+A, per 90 from 90 minutes, average rating, MOTM, clean sheets for keepers), sorted by any column either way, missing figures last and, by average rating, men under three appearances after the rest (`playerStatRows`, `sortPlayerStats`); Team is our numbers ranked against every side (points, goals scored and conceded, goal difference, clean sheets, games without a goal, home and away points) and a sortable table of every side (`teamStatRows`, `sortTeamStats`, `teamRank` in `src/game/logic/statsTables.ts`). Only scores are read, so a compacted save gives the same tables; a run-in counts the games after the takeover.

## 13. Difficulty calibration

This exists in code. `GameSettings.difficulty` is `'calibrated'` (default) or `'realistic'`.

- `aiRatingOffset` (`src/engine/season.ts`) auto-picks every AI club's best eleven, averages their overall team strength and subtracts `CALIBRATED_LEAGUE_AVERAGE = 75`, floored at 0 and rounded to 0.1. Realistic returns 0.
- The offset is subtracted from every unit (and the overall) of every AI club in every match, including quick seasons and pre-season friendly opponents. The board target is computed against the offset strengths. The Club tab shows "rivals play N rating points below their real strength".
- Rationale: a wheel draft rarely assembles eleven 85s, while a modern top club fields exactly that, and the engine stretches ratings above 80. Observed in the test suite at the time of writing, with no offset (realistic): an auto-drafted squad averaging 81.8 overall finished 19th with 32 points in 2025-26, and an elite XI beats a mid-table XI at home 88.8 per cent of the time, with equal sides splitting 42.8 / 28.0 / 29.2 home-draw-away.

## 14. Dynasty

`advanceToNextSeason` (`src/game/logic/dynasty.ts`) runs when you continue from the summary:

1. **Ageing.** Each player gets a new player-season (`${playerId}@dyn${year}`) one year older. A potential is estimated as `overall + max(0, 27 − age) × 1.3 + N(0, 1.5)`, then the common attribute delta by new age is:

| New age | Delta |
| --- | --- |
| ≤ 23 | min(growth room, N(2.2, 1.2)) |
| 24 to 27 | min(growth room, N(0.8, 1.0)) |
| 28 to 30 | N(−0.2, 0.8) |
| 31 to 32 | N(−1.4, 1.0) |
| 33 to 34 | N(−2.6, 1.2) |
| 35+ | N(−4.0, 1.5) |

   Physical attributes lead: at 30+ pace takes an extra −1.6, physical −0.8, dribbling −0.6; at 22 and under pace +0.8, physical +0.4, dribbling +0.3. Passing gains +0.6 and mentality +1.0 at 30+ (+0.3 otherwise). Goalkeeping gains +0.5 until 33, then −0.5. The depth attributes: stamina takes an extra −1.2 and jumping −1.0 at 30+ (+0.6 and +0.5 at 22 and under); strength follows the delta until 29, holds from 30 to 32 (the delta is cancelled, only the noise moves it) and falls with an extra −0.8 from 33; aggression and work rate are character, so the delta is cancelled at every age and only the noise moves them. Measured over 400 seeded summers on a 70-rated set: ageing to 31 moves stamina −2.5, jumping −2.4, strength 0.0, aggression and work rate 0.0; to 33 strength −3.4; to 21 stamina +2.9, strength +2.3, jumping +2.8. Each attribute also gets N(0, 0.6) noise and is clamped 1 to 99. Overall is recomputed from the primary position weights; value is `0.25 × 1.21^(overall − 60)` times an age factor (1.3 at ≤ 21, 1.15 to 26, 1.0 to 29, 0.72 to 31, 0.45 to 33, else 0.25); wage is `5 × value^0.85`.

2. **Retirements.** From 34: 30 per cent; from 36: 60; from 37: 90. A man who retires is listed on `Game.retiredPlayers` (his real player id) and never comes back: the summer wheel marks him taken and no market window, summer or January, nor a scout report, nor the academy's youth intake offers any season of him (before this, a keeper who retired at 34 could be redrafted as his 28-year-old self).
   **A man who left** (sold, released, out of contract, a declined renewal, a loan gone back) is filed on `Game.departedPlayers` with his age and the year (`recordDeparture` in `src/game/logic/departures.ts`, from `releasePlayer` and the summer; Dynasty only). Each year his floor is that age plus the years since (`ageFloors`), and no season of him younger than the floor is offered again: the summer wheel (`DraftState.ageFloor`, checked wherever the draft checks `takenPlayers`), every market window (`MarketContext.ageFloor`), the academy, the scouts and the assistant's cover. His own age and older seasons stay on offer.
3. **Contracts** lose a year. At zero nothing renews itself any more (E6-8; before, he left on morale under 50 or a 25 per cent roll and otherwise renewed for two or three years without a word): a renewal he turned down (the in-season contract event's let him go) means he walks now; anyone else is out of contract, kept in the squad on `Contract.expired` (years 0) and on the renewal list through pre-season, until the opener settles him (section 10, "The Dynasty opener"). A contract renewed in the season (`renewed`) still runs on three seasons (one at 32+). Drafted players start on one to three years, signings on three. The squad status carries over, and the wage the new player-season computes from its value is multiplied by the status again.
4. State resets to condition 95, sharpness 60, morale at least 50, familiarity +10, no injury or cards.
5. **Bonus spins** `min(6, 2 + players lost)` (plus any an agent's meeting earned), never more than the room left under the squad cap of 23 (`SQUAD_CAP`; a full squad that lost four used to draft back to 25). A summer draft opens with squad size = remaining squad + bonus spins and one re-spin. The surviving squad is seeded into it, best eleven on the pitch and the rest on the bench, so every bonus spin adds depth wherever the wheel lands; existing players are marked taken so the uniqueness rule holds.
6. **League.** The league season advances by one real year (after 2026-27 it wraps to 2008-09, and the summer report says so: "Back to the start"). You keep the same host club if it was in that division, otherwise a random club from that season. Budget and achievements carry over, the wage cap is recomputed for the new squad, and a new board target is set.
7. A summer window (section 11) is open until kick-off. The offseason modal lists who retired, who left, who improved and who declined, the summer's stories (round 9: the farewells, the breakthroughs and the milestones; "The club's history" above) and how many bonus spins the draft holds. On the season summary the continue button reads "On to year N" ("Join <club>" once an offer is taken), a link under it scrolls to the job offers, and New game asks before it ends the Dynasty.

## 15. Determinism and saves

All randomness runs through one `Rng` (xoshiro128** seeded by a cyrb53 hash) whose state is stored in the save after every action, so a seed replays identically and a daily seed is identical for everyone. A match paused at half time or at the 70th minute stores a forked stream for the rest of the match, so the week's other fixtures never share its numbers. A daily result is filed under the day its seed was set, not the day it was finished. The game is persisted with Zustand `persist` under the `localStorage` key `touchline-save-v1` (the active game, parked games, the shortlist, the head-to-head game, the profile and whether you were in a game), compacted and compressed as described below. Quick drafts, dailies and weeklies share one save slot; Season, Dynasty, the run-in and the arena (every format) each have their own, so starting a quick draft parks a running dynasty on the home screen instead of replacing it. On load, `normaliseGame` (`src/game/logic/save.ts`) fills in every field added since a save was written, for the active game and the parked ones, and the same path covers an imported file; the players a game created (`customPlayers`: dynasty ageing, development, wage changes) that predate the depth attributes get stamina, strength, aggression, jumping and work rate derived from their eight core attributes, age and height with the same `withDepth` the engine uses for an older data pack, so a dynasty saved before format 3 plays on without a `NaN` in its condition or its tactics. The default league must load; a saved game whose other leagues fail to load is parked with a toast rather than rendered against a half-loaded universe, league loads are serialised so two callers never overwrite each other, and a failed fetch is not cached. There is no backend.

**Player names at load.** 54 names in the packs carry the European Soccer Database's stray ",NN" ("D. Cousin,20"). Until a rebuild fixes them (docs/DATA.md, section 6), the store passes every freshly loaded universe through `tidyUniverseNames` (`src/game/logic/playerNames.ts`), the one load-time exception to the read-only universe. Names are display text only, never an id, a sort, a hash or a draw: `playerNames.test.ts` plays seeded quick seasons and the matches against the affected sides on a raw and a tidied universe and finds them identical but for the names. The engine's loader and the edge verifier (which builds its own universe) are untouched.

### Save size: compaction, compression and a full storage

A browser gives a site about 5 MB of `localStorage` (5 million UTF-16 characters in Chrome), shared by the active game, every parked game, the head-to-head game, the shortlist and the profile. Measured before this change (in memory, `src/game/__tests__/save.test.ts`, and in the browser): one Season at full time was 4.40 million characters of JSON, 4.37 M of it the season (fixtures 3.98 M: the 342 AI-vs-AI results 3.34 M, of which events 1.06 M, player stats 1.33 M, condition loss 0.30 M and the team reports 0.50 M; the user's 38 results 0.64 M; the AI clubs' player states 0.22 M; the cup ties 0.15 M; news 10 K), and everything else (squad, customPlayers, history, awards, career records, the last match) under 50 K. A Season parked at matchweek 30 (3.47 M) plus a Dynasty was refused on its first season's Sim to end, so the save stayed at the pre-season and the season played after it was lost on reload.

Two things now keep it small, and neither changes a result:

- **Compaction** (`compactGame`, `src/game/logic/compact.ts`, run by `partialize` on the active and every parked game). After the week has moved on nothing reads more of a stored result than its score: the tables (Dugout, Summary, the opposition report, the digest), the fixtures list, the season records, the objectives, the briefing and the event context read `homeGoals` and `awayGoals`; the cup and Continental Cup lists add `penalties` and `winnerId`. The match screen shows `game.lastMatch` and `game.lastCupMatch`, which are kept whole, and the league statistics and awards come from the squad's and the AI clubs' player states. So a stored fixture or tie result keeps `homeTeamId`, `awayTeamId`, `homeGoals`, `awayGoals`, `xg` and (ties) `penalties`, with empty `events`, `homeStats` and `awayStats` (still a valid `MatchResult`), except the user's league matches of the current and the previous matchweek and the user's latest tie in each knockout, which stay complete. The match-day dashboard reveals the round's other scores goal by goal at the user's clock, live and in the replay, so the other fixtures of the previous, the current and the in-play matchweek, and the other ties of the round of the user's latest tie and of the tie in play, keep their goal events cut to minute, type, side and scorer (`text` empty, a few dozen bytes a goal): a reload in the middle of a match shows the same latest scores and live table (tested for league and cup). The game in memory keeps its detail until the page reloads; a season played on from a compacted save ends exactly as the one played on in memory (same table, statistics, awards, finances and generator state, tested through the next Dynasty summer). Unbounded lists are capped at the source: the inbox at 120 items (as before) and a transfer window's log at its newest 80 lines (`WINDOW_LOG_CAP`).
- **Compression** (`src/game/saveStorage.ts`). The persisted JSON is written as lz-string `compressToUTF16` (lz-string 1.5.0). On read, a value whose first character is `{` is the plain JSON every earlier build wrote and is parsed as it is; anything else is decompressed. A compressed JSON object never starts with `{`, so old saves (including ones with full match detail on every fixture) load through the same `merge` and `normaliseGame` and are compacted on their next write. Compressing takes about 0.1 s per 400 K characters, so writes are coalesced: a change schedules one write 250 ms later carrying the latest state (a steady stream of changes still writes every 250 ms), and a waiting write goes out at once when the page is hidden or closed.

After both, a Season at full time stores about 49 K characters (4.41 M before: 429 K once compacted, then about 9 to 1 compression); a two-season Dynasty at full time with a Season parked at matchweek 31 stores about 83 K (7.98 M before, 0.80 M compacted). In the browser the same Dynasty with a Season parked at matchweek 30 stored 80 K characters at the end of its first season and 97 K at the end of its second.

**A full storage.** When the browser refuses a write (`QuotaExceededError`), the storage catches it, the game carries on in memory and the stored save stays as it was; a toast says the storage is full and suggests exporting the save or discarding a parked game (shown on the first refused write and every twentieth after, until a write succeeds), and a "Not saving" pill in the top bar (floating in the corner while the bar is hidden; it opens Your record, where Export save is) stays for as long as writes fail (the transient `saveFailing`, `SaveBanner.tsx`, cleared by the next write that lands). A write that would store exactly what the page last read or wrote is skipped, so a toast, a screen change or an idle page writes nothing.

**Two tabs.** Every write stores the whole save, so a second tab used to put its older game back over the first tab's progress on its next change (even a screen change). Now a page that hears another page write the save (the browser's `storage` event, `saveChangedElsewhere` in `store.ts`) stops writing at once and shows a dialog, "Saved in another tab", whose one button reloads it into the newer save; a page back from the back-forward cache or out of a frozen background tab compares the stored save with the one it last read or wrote (`changedElsewhere` in `saveStorage.ts`) and does the same. The last page to save wins; the others never overwrite it. **Export and import** (the Profile page): export writes the game in memory as plain, readable JSON (`{ state, version: 1 }`, compacted), so it works even when the storage is full; import accepts plain JSON from any build (an old uncompacted export included, with or without a byte-order mark) and the compressed text of a stored save, compacts it, stores it compressed and reloads; a file that is not a save, or that does not fit, leaves the current save untouched. The import refuses, before anything is stored: a file cut short ("the file is damaged or incomplete"), a save written under another persist version (it would load as nothing: "made by a newer version"), a game, parked game, record or head-to-head that cannot be read (`normaliseGame` takes a game only with an id, a known phase, a mode, a seed and a generator state), and a file holding none of them; a file with games but no record keeps the browser's own record.

**Side streams after the draft.** The manager spin and the end-of-draft case draw from forks of a copy of `Game.rngState` taken at `toKickoff` (`fork('manager-spin')`, `fork('end-case')`) and never write it back, so neither moves the season, the cups or anything after; a locker power-up's streams are seeded from the seed as `newDraft` seeds the tray's. No draw of a game without the rules changed, so neither needed an `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (`cases.test.ts`, with `noMove.test.ts` and `dailyPin.test.ts` unchanged).

### Cross-league seeds, the data stamp and the engine version

`spin()` chooses a season uniformly, then a club-season from that season by weighted index over `u.clubSeasons`, so the order of that array is part of the seed's meaning. `mergeUniverses` (`src/engine/universe.ts`) therefore concatenates league packs in the order of the data index `leagues.json` (England, Spain, Italy, Germany, France, the Netherlands, Portugal), never in the order they happened to be loaded; a pack missing from the index goes last, in load order. Before this, opening Spain before Germany or the other way round gave the same European Nights seed different clubs on nine of eleven quick-draft spins (seed `K7Q2-MX9A`, same eleven seasons). England-only wheels, the Daily included, were never affected; cross-league seeds changed once, with this fix. `h2hSpin` draws from the same pool and inherits the order.

Every `LoadedUniverse` carries `generatedAt`, the `YYYY-MM-DD` stamp of the data packs read from `leagues.json` (`2026-10-25` today, the format 3 packs with the depth columns; a single pack built offline falls back to its own `players.json` stamp). `ENGINE_VERSION` (`src/engine/version.ts`, currently `'7'`: `'1'` was before the wheel twists added a draw to every third spin, `'2'` before the 70th-minute window split every match into three segments, `'3'` before the depth attributes changed every match's expected goals and card rolls, `'4'` before the playtest fixes of 2026-10-26: the fixtures' balanced venues, which replaced two shuffles of the rounds with one shuffle of the clubs (TL-05), the assistant's positional placement in the draft and the head-to-head (TL-04), no keeper-only man on outfield in an automatic change (TL-01) and development as a delta from the shown overall (INV-01); every league season, assistant draft and quick season moved), `'5'` before the engine 6 release of 2026-10-28: the goal calibration (AI morale, familiarity and form held, morale and form effects halved, familiarity flattened, base xG 1.32 to 1.28, the soft ceiling on a side's expected goals, league scoring levels for Spain, Italy and Germany; section 8, "Calibration"), discipline (keepers booked, yellows up, reds down) and no booking after an injury, the reserve keeper, no human on both sides of a match (the user's men out of their real clubs), the assistant's keeper limits, the Dynasty economy, the youth intake's squad cap, no zero minutes while injured, the run-in's reconciled carried table and the Edge Function's room check; every match, league season, assistant draft, quick season, cup, arena run and Run-in moved), `'6'` before engine 7: the Continental Cup's group stage and two-legged knockouts in Season, Dynasty and the Quick Draft's post-season run (an engine 6 cup in a save plays on in its old format), the Gauntlet's era ladders taking the nearest bands' new clubs before repeating one, the Run-in's Bundesliga relegation play-off, a cup tie the assistant plays settling on its week's stream instead of a stale copy (the nested RNG in `afterLeagueMatch`), and the Dynasty's contracts no longer renewing themselves (the summer's renewal list and the opener's `settleRenewals`, the 25 per cent walk-out and the two-to-three-year rolls gone), a man who left never offered younger (`DraftState.ageFloor`, `MarketContext.ageFloor`) and the opener's thin-squad cover (section 14); Seasons and Dynasties with a cup tie, every Continental Cup, every Dynasty summer, era Gauntlets and German run-ins that finish 16th moved, while the match engine, the quick league season, the Daily, the Weekly, the quick wheels, head-to-heads and every pinned single match held) names the engine's random-number consumption and is bumped whenever a change alters how many draws a spin, a pick, a match or a season takes or the order it takes them in. Anything that replays a seed on another device stores both stamps beside the result so a mismatch is reported as unverified rather than failing silently. The quick season's January window and Continental Cup run (section 12) draw from forks of a copy of the season's stream, so neither changed a replayed outcome and neither bumped the version. Nor did the quick-season highlights: they replay the season from its kept start state and draw the reel's extra moments from a fork of a copy. Nor did the Quick Draft modes (section 5): they exist only under the new `modes` rule, Crazy's formation and surprises draw from streams seeded apart from the game's, and Speedrun's auto-pick is an ordinary spin and pick. Nor did pinning the Daily back to 2 re-spins: the budget moves no draw unless a re-spin is taken. Nor did the Quick Draft bench: it only exists under the new `benchPicks` rule, its spins after the XI draw from their own fork, and a season without anyone on the bench plays exactly as before. Nor did the power-ups: they exist only under the new `powerUps` rule, Re-roll season draws from its own seeded stream, Peek spins copies, and Swap, the armband and the lock draw nothing; a draft that uses none spins exactly as without the rule (tested). Nor did Gamble mode and the Player Upgrader: the Icons roll and the upgrade draw from streams seeded apart from the game's and exist only under the new `gamble` flag and `upgrader` rule; a draft without them draws exactly as before (the spin weight moved into `spinWeight` unchanged), and with Gamble on every spin before the Icons spin lands where the same draft without Gamble would (tested). The share text (`shareText`, section 12) takes an optional `extraLines` list, printed after the awards and before the seed line, so a mode can add one line without a new field each time.

### Pins and the link versions

- **Pins** (`src/engine/__tests__/pins.test.ts`, `src/game/__tests__/pins.test.ts`) freeze literal outcomes of fixed seeds, so a change that moves an existing draw fails even when every statistical test passes: the quick wheel's eleven history rows (`clubId@season`, with the twist) for seed `K7Q2-MX9A`, 4-3-3, `autoDraft`, on an England wheel and on an England, Spain and Italy wheel; the first eight log lines of a head-to-head draft (seed `pin-h2h`, the store's rules, `h2hSpin` then `h2hAutoPick` until complete, 33 lines in all); and the `flow-test` season played by the assistant (`playMatchweek(g, u, true)` 38 times): the top three `teamId:points` (`user:99`, `manchester-city:85`, `newcastle-united:69` on engine `'6'`), the cup winner (`leeds-united`) and the final `rngState`. They were computed on `ENGINE_VERSION` `'4'` with the `2026-10-25` packs and re-baselined on `'5'` and `'6'` (the literals are in the files; on `'6'` the quick wheels and the head-to-head log held, only the season moved); on `'7'` every one of them held, and only the version literal moved. A new mode draws from its own place in the stream and gates every new draft rule on its flag, so it leaves them green unchanged; they are re-baselined only in the commit that bumps `ENGINE_VERSION` or rebuilds the data, with the literals recomputed there.
- **Link versions** (`src/engine/challenge.ts`). `ChallengePayload` is `ChallengePayloadV1 | ChallengePayloadV2 | ArenaChallengePayload` (`QuickChallengePayload` is the first two). Version 2 is the version 1 shape plus `kind` (`'quick'`, the default when missing, or `'arena'`); an arena link (`kind: 'arena'`) carries its claim in `ac` instead of `c`, plus `arena`, `t`, `lf` and `auto` (section 17). `decodeChallenge` reads version 1 as before, except that its rules diff may use only `V1_RULE_KEYS` (every rule the live site knew before the casual modes merged: `squadSize`, `respins`, `fromSeason`, `toSeason`, `onlyClub`, `onlyNation`, `minFit`, `hardMode`, `uniqueClubSeasons`, `twists`, and from the 38-0 setup, Quick Draft modes, power-ups, classic seasons and the Super League `difficulty`, `showRatings`, `draftMode`, `ratings`, `benchPicks`, `modes`, `upgrader`, `powerUps`, `managers`, `classic`, `superLeague`), and a version 2 quick link may also carry main's `x`, `g` and `w` blocks; it accepts version 2, refuses a version 2 `kind` or top-level key it does not know with "This challenge link comes from a newer version of TouchLine" (version 1 still ignores extra keys; a `kind` that is not a string is not a link), refuses an arena link that `isArenaLink` does not accept as not a link (an arena link's own top-level keys are `v`, `kind`, `arena`, `data`, `engine`, `name`, `s`, `p`, `t`, `lf`, `auto`, `ac` and `k`; any other is newer), and refuses anything above version 2 as newer. Until arena links were built, every arena link read "This challenge needs the arena, which this build does not have yet"; no build that said so was released. `challengeFromGame` writes the version from `payloadVersionFor(rulesDiff, kind)`: 1 for a quick link whose rule keys are all v1 keys, 2 otherwise, so a link using a rule added later (a salary cap, a teammate chain) reaches an older build as "newer version" rather than "not a link". The salary cap (`budget`) is the first such rule: a capped quick link is version 2 with `kind: 'quick'`; every cap-less quick link is still version 1. When main merged into the casual modes (2026-10-25) its eleven rules joined `V1_RULE_KEYS` in that merge, because the live site writes them as version 1 (every main link carries `difficulty` and `managers`), so every link it made still opens and verifies here.
- **The arena's streams.** An arena game carries three streams of its seed, `${seed}#field`, `#ties` and `#draw`, made by `createGame` beside `rngState` (`Game.arena.streams`). They draw the field, the Gauntlet's ladder, the Cup's draws and its AI-against-AI ties, so two players on one seed meet the same opponents whatever they drafted; the user's own matches and shoot-outs draw from `rngState`. No existing draw moved, so the streams needed no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump and the pins held.
- **Labels and streaks.** Every place a screen names the mode reads `src/game/logic/modes.ts` (`MODE_TITLE`, `modeLabel(game, 'short' | 'heading' | 'share')`, `shareCardFooter`, `yearLabel`, `finishDraftLabel`, `isQuickLike`), each an exhaustive table, so a new `Mode` fails to compile there once. The Daily streak goes through `advanceStreak` and `streakDay` (`src/game/logic/streaks.ts`): the result files under the seed's own day, the streak grows only when the last counted day is the one before, and a second result on a counted day changes nothing. The Daily Arena's and the Weekly's books go through `fileStreakResult` on the same rules, with their own seeds: `arenaDailySeed`, `arenaDailyDateOf` and `previousArenaDailySeed` (`arena-YYYY-MM-DD`), and `isoWeekOf`, `weeklySeed`, `weeklyDateOf` (the week's Monday) and `previousWeeklySeed` (`weekly-YYYY-Www`, the ISO week: 2026 has 53, so 2026-12-31 and 2027-01-01 are `2026-W53` and 2027-01-04 is `2027-W01`), all in `src/engine/rng.ts`.

### The online layer: accounts, cloud saves and the ranked boards

The online layer (`src/online`, `docs/ONLINE.md`) is optional: a build without `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` and `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` never loads it. Its ranked boards never store a posted score. A finished Daily (or weekly challenge) posts its ghost-draft payload (`challengeFromGame`, the same format as a `#c=` link) with the team name and short name it was played with; the server rebuilds the game from the day's pinned settings (`puzzleOf`: the game settings without the player's names), replays the draft with `strictReplayDraft` and the season with `runQuickSeason`, and files the figures the replay produced. `strictReplayDraft` drives the engine's own `spin`, `pick` and `autoDraft` the way the store does but, unlike `replayDraft`, treats a null row as a re-spin only while re-spins are left; with none left a null row can only be the spin Auto-complete discarded, after which every remaining row must be exactly what the assistant drafts. A draft has at most squad size + bench places + re-spins + 1 rows (14 for the Daily, which has no bench); with a Quick Draft bench a last null row while the XI is full is the spin showing when the bench was closed, and a draft that ends with bench places to spare was closed (both replay as `closeBench` does, for the 38-0 chase). Tested over 200 Daily dates with eight kinds of tampering, and against `replayChallenge` for parity.

The **weekly challenge** is one Quick Draft per ISO week (Monday 00:00 UTC to the next Monday), seed `weekly-YYYY-Www`, settings from `weeklyGameSettings` (`src/online/weekly.ts`): an England wheel, a complete English season at `h mod seasons`, that season's club ids sorted at `floor(h / 64) mod clubs` and `DAILY_FORMATIONS` at `floor(h / 4096) mod 5`, where `h = hashString('weekly-' + key)`; Quick rules with twists and two re-spins, calibrated, the team name the player's. 2026-W43 is a 4-4-2 diamond as Southampton in 2018-19 (pinned by a test, like the Daily). Posts are accepted until 00:00 UTC on the Tuesday after the week; the first post per account per week stands. The **all-time Daily board** sums each account's verified Daily points and records its best streak (consecutive puzzle dates) and its current one. Every board can be filtered by the optional country on the player's online profile, which is copied onto each row when it is posted. The Daily itself stays at two re-spins (the owner's decision of 2026-10-25, pinned in `setup.test.ts` and `dailyPin.test.ts`).

In the browser (an online build only): signing in is optional (an email code, six digits; Google only where the project turns it on) and unlocks cloud saves for every mode, posting to the boards and short challenge links. A finished Daily or weekly challenge is filed in a per-device outbox the moment it ends and posted once the player is signed in; the Summary shows where it ranked, or why it did not. The World leaderboard screen has seven boards (`TABS` in `src/ui/online/WorldBoard.tsx`: today's Daily, this week's challenge, all-time Daily totals by points or best streak, the 38-0 chase, and the three casual boards below), a country filter and the player's own row. Cloud sync never deletes a game because it is missing, never swaps the game on screen without a "Load it", resolves the same game on two devices by progress (the other copy is kept as the cloud's previous one) and asks when two different games claim a slot. Details in `docs/ONLINE.md` section 5b.

**Cloud slots** (`SLOT_IDS` in `src/online/core/slots.ts`): `quick` (the Quick Draft, the Daily and both Weeklies), `season`, `dynasty`, `h2h`, and since engine 7 `arena` (every arena format and the Daily Arena, `Game.arena` kept whole) and `runin`. A project without the migration `cloud_slots_arena_runin` refuses the last two (`LATER_SLOTS`), and the sync engine keeps them on the device for the session. Two copies of one game are ordered by `progressKey`: the year, then a rank in which `preseason`, `week`, `match` and `january` are alike, then the matchweek (never the screen, since a match report sits between two hubs); an arena run by its step first.

**The casual boards** (`src/online/casual.ts` on the device, `src/online/server/casualVerify.ts` on the server, op `casual.submit`; `docs/ONLINE.md` section 5g). Three World boards, each one fixed puzzle for everyone in its period, pinned at the period's first ranked post and replayed by the server, one post per account and period (the first stands):
- `arena`, the Daily Arena: rounds won, then goal difference, fewer conceded, goals scored; only a run the assistant played (`auto`: no live match, no changed training or penalty order, no in-play change) ranks, since the matches are not in the payload.
- `themed`, the Weekly Challenge (mode `weekly`): points, then fewer defeats, goal difference, goals scored; the season replays with `playSeason`, and anything outside the week's puzzle (the Upgrader, power-ups, the case's Icon, the January gamble) is refused.
- `runin`, the Run-in of the week (seed `runin-YYYY-Www`, `src/game/logic/runinWeekly.ts`): the four picks replay strictly, then the assistant plays every matchweek as Sim to end does; a run-in managed by hand plays another season, so the device checks it first (`runInReplayProblem`) and never posts it.
Payloads and picks are public once a board's posting has closed; a summary shows `CasualStatus` (the place, "Sign in to post", or why not).

**Error reports** (`src/online/errors.ts`, started by `boot.ts`; `src/online/core/errorReport.ts`; `docs/ONLINE.md` section 5f). An online build reports uncaught errors, unhandled rejections, the screen boundary's catches and chunks that did not load to its own project, never a third party: the message and our own stack frames scrubbed (`scrubText`: emails, tokens, keys, every URL query and hash), the build, the engine, the screen and phase, the viewport, a browser family, a random device id made for reports alone; never game data, an account, an email or a token. The same error goes at most once a day; 10 reports an hour and 30 a day per device (`DEVICE_LIMITS`), a queue of 20 while offline. Posted as a guest with the publishable key (`errors.report`), filed by `svc_error_report` into `private.error_reports`, pruned after 30 days. Profile > Settings > Error reports turns it off and empties the queue.

The **38-0 chase** is Quick Draft's world board (`src/online/chase.ts`, `docs/ONLINE.md` section 5d). Because a Quick Draft seed is the player's own, only a season played on a wheel the server issued counts: "Start a chase run" (World > 38-0 chase) draws an attempt, seed `chase-<10-character code>`, at most five per account per UTC day, open 48 hours, posted once from the Summary ("Submit to the 38-0 chase"). The attempt fixes the standard Quick Draft (`CHASE_RULES`: `QUICK_RULES` at normal difficulty with the plain manager pick, the all-time wheel of the league played in, twists on), calibrated opposition, the January window, the league, shape, player ratings (Season or Prime) and bench (0 to 5) the player chose, a complete real season at `hashString('chase-' + code) mod seasons` and the mid-table side Quick Draft replaces by default. The plain manager pick counts (it draws nothing and replays); the manager spin, modes, Upgrader, power-ups, Super League, end case and Icon, classic seasons, cap and chain do not. The board keeps each account's best run, this week or all time, ordered by points dropped against the league's maximum (so 34-game and 38-game leagues compare), then defeats, goal difference and goals scored; a flawless season is flagged and the closest-ever record heads the board.

The server side is one Edge Function per engine version (`touchline-e{ENGINE_VERSION}`), running the game's own code from a bundle of `src/online/server/entry.ts`. A puzzle is pinned once, at the first ranked request of its day or week, from the data stamp and engine the live site's `build.json` names; a run is ranked on the pin's data or on a stamp the live site served after the pin (a mid-day rebuild), and refused on anything else. The first post per account per board stands (a retry of the same draft is `duplicate`, another draft `already_submitted`).

The **Privacy and fair play** screen (`privacy`, `src/ui/screens/Privacy.tsx`, linked from Home, How it works and the sign-in panel; rendered in every build) states what stays on the device, what an account stores and where (Supabase, Sydney), the retention figures from `src/online/core/retention.ts` (one previous copy per save slot, 16 save files per account, rate-limit counters 2 days, short links 365 days, an 8-character account prefix in logs, each pinned to the SQL or code by `retention.test.ts`), the two deletions, the leaderboard rules (server replay, first post stands, ties to the first post, fixed seeds can be practised by everyone alike, one account per person, abusive names renamed) and the data credits.

### Offline

The site is hosted as a Render static site (build `npm ci && npm run build`, publish `dist`, auto-deployed from the repository); the GitHub Pages workflow was retired when the repository went private, and none came back when it went public again on 2026-10-27. `public/sw.js` is registered on production builds relative to the deploy path. The shell (`index.html`, the manifest) is served network-first and cached as a fallback; hashed assets, icons and league packs are cached on first use and served cache-first, and so are the Google Fonts stylesheet and font files (the one typeface, Sora), so the type still loads offline after one visit. A new build changes the asset names, so it is picked up on the next online visit; stale assets stay in the single cache until its version string is bumped.

## 16. Roadmap

Not in the code today:

- Classic seasons for the other leagues (and in online head-to-head rooms).
- Online multiplayer beyond two-manager head-to-head rooms (section 18): no multiplayer leagues, no rooms for the arena or the Quick Draft. The online layer has sign-in, cloud saves, the verified world leaderboard, short links and the head-to-head rooms.
- 38-0 modes not reproduced: Multiplayer leagues. (Last One Standing is built, managed, as the Gauntlet, Tournaments as the Cup (section 17) and Teammate Chain as a wheel rule (section 5).)
- Extra time in the arena's knockouts: a level tie goes straight to penalties at 90.
- FM systems not reproduced: separate in-possession and out-of-possession shapes, individual training, regens (the Dynasty youth intake offers real young player-seasons only), mentoring, and finances beyond budget, wages, staff, prize money and the board's requests. Scouting with attribute ranges (`scouting.ts`), the youth intake (`youth.ts`) and press conferences (event cards in `src/engine/events.ts`) are built (section 10).

## 17. The arena

One-sitting knockouts against real club-seasons. The arena lives beside `SeasonState`, never inside it: a game of mode `arena` has `settings.arena` (`{ format: 'cup' | 'gauntlet', size?, field?, decade?, daily? }`) and `Game.arena` (`ArenaState`, `src/game/types.ts`; logic in `src/game/logic/arena.ts`, engine in `src/engine/knockout.ts`). This build ships three formats: the **Cup Final** (`format: 'cup'`, `size: 2`), **the Gauntlet** (`format: 'gauntlet'`) and **the Cup** of 8, 16 or 32 clubs (`format: 'cup'`, `size: 8 | 16 | 32`; engine in `src/engine/cup.ts`), each below.

**The arena's Cup keeps its one-off ties (engine 7's choice).** The Continental Cup went to groups and two legs in engine 7; the arena's Cup did not. Its runs are short sessions of one-off knockout nights by design (a 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32-club bracket, the user's draft against a field of real club-seasons), the Gauntlet and the final are single matches too, and the Daily Arena's mapping is frozen (invariant 4): a group stage would add six matches to every run, change what a Daily Arena is, and split the arena's formats from one another for no gain in what the mode is for.

- **Container.** `ArenaState` holds the three streams (section 15), the clubs about to play or still alive keyed `clubId@season` (`arenaClub`: the `buildAiClubs` recipe, familiarity 70, sharpness 85, the club's style; the name reads `Real Madrid 2017-18`), `next` (the key of the next opponent), `step` (the user's next match, 0-based), `log` (one `ArenaStep` per user match: label, opponent, venue, score, the shoot-out with every kick, man of the match, training focus, live or assistant, real strength and offset), `pending` (a live match level at 90 waiting on its penalties), `yellows` (bookings across the run), `over`, `won` and `handManaged`. An opponent keeps its real squad even when you drafted one of its players, as in Season mode.
- **Settings.** `finalGameSettings`: `{ ...QUICK_RULES, squadSize: 16 }` with the wheel leagues, era, hard mode and twists chosen; the league, season and host are colours and labels only (the wheel's first league, its latest complete season and that season's default host unless another club is chosen); no job security, no Continental Cup.
- **Flow.** `finishDraft` → `beginArena`: the drafted sixteen join the squad (`newSquadPlayer`, no contracts), the assistant picks the side, the field's stream draws the opponent, phase `week` (the arena hub); the draft stays on the game. `playArenaStep(live)`: a final is importance 1.4, neutral, labelled `Cup final`, with the training focus's boosts; live it pauses at half time and at 70 like a league match (`game.live`, kind `arena`), otherwise the assistant picks the side and plays it. `advanceArenaMatch` is `advanceLiveMatch`'s pattern (only the substitutions the engine made are written back). `resolveArenaStep` applies the result to both squads, writes the log row and, when level, goes to penalties: live, the manager sets the order and keeper (`pending.shootout`); otherwise at once with the default takers and the man in goal. `closeArenaMatch` returns to the hub or, with the run over, `finishArena` (achievements, phase `summary`).
- **Cards.** A red card is a one-match ban and so is every second booking of the run (`arena.yellows`); a banned man serves it by sitting out the next match. The league's five-yellow and two-to-three-match rules do not apply.
- **Recovery** between matches (`arenaRecovery(game, u, factor)`, for the formats with more than one): the week's recovery scaled by `factor`, the training focus's recovery, sharpness and familiarity on top, one match off every injury.
- **The field** (`finalOpponent`, from `streams.field`; the pool is every club-season with a squad in scope, sorted by key, so load order never changes a seed):
  - **Giants** (the default for a final): the strongest 5% of the pool by real team strength (`teamStrengthOf`: the auto-picked XI's `computeTeamStrength().overall`, fresh legs, no offset, memoised per universe and never saved). Ranking a whole pool costs about 0.5 ms a club-season (1.3 s for the 2,526 of a seven-league field), so while a run drafts `useArenaWarmup` works the same memoised values out in idle slices (`arenaWarmup.ts`: `arenaWarmPool`, `warmStrengths`) and the first `beginArena` reads them; the first "On to the draw" went from a 470 to 580 ms long task to 56 to 98 ms for a Giants cup. Warmed or cold, every run plays the same (`arenaWarmup.test.ts`).
  - **Champions**: the data's champions only, so leagues without tables never appear (an empty pool falls back to the giants).
  - **The wheel**: a season uniformly, then a club by the wheel's weighting, `1 + 0.06 × (21 − position)`, or 1.6 without a table.

  The champions and giants draw from every league (an arena game with those fields loads every pack, `leaguesFor`); the wheel from the wheel's leagues. The era applies to all three. The player's own landed club-seasons are never excluded.
- **Calibration.** `knockoutOffset(strengths, field, difficulty)`: 0 on Realistic, otherwise `max(0, round1(mean − target))`, taken off every unit of the opponent in the match.

  | Field | Target |
  | --- | --- |
  | wheel | 72 |
  | decade | 74 |
  | europe | 75 |
  | champions | 76 |
  | giants | 78 |

  Measured (England packs of 2026-10-25, engine `'4'`, an auto-drafted 4-3-3 sixteen, assistant management, 1,000 seeds `review-0` to `review-999`, so each share is good to about ±3 points; the England giants are 19 club-seasons from 85.2 to 87.5, all seven leagues' 127 from 83.8 to 87.7): against the giants the final is won 49.2% of the time (Realistic 14.3%), against a champion 60.4%, against the wheel 74.1%; 21% of the giants finals are level at 90 (12% on Realistic, 22% against a champion, 23% against the wheel). An earlier 200-seed run read 51.5%, 9.5%, 56.0% and 74.5%, within its own noise. A test keeps the calibrated giants between 35% and 85% and Realistic below it.
- **The shoot-out** (`penaltyShootout`, one draw per kick, a toss from `rngState` deciding who kicks first): each kick scores with `p = clamp(0.74 + (shooting − 70) × 0.004 + (mentality − 70) × 0.004 × pressure − (keeper goalkeeping − 75) × 0.003, 0.50, 0.92)`, `pressure` 0.5 for kicks 1 to 3, 1 for the fourth, 1.5 for the fifth and 2 in sudden death, so composure belongs on kicks four and five and the order is a real decision. Five each in turn, stopping as soon as one side cannot be caught, then sudden-death pairs; each side's takers cycle through its order (up to eleven) before anyone goes again. `defaultTakers`: the named penalty taker first, then by `(shooting + mentality) / 2`, the goalkeeper last. The manager's order may be any of the men on the pitch at 90 (the kick-off XI less substitutes taken off, red cards and injuries); the keeper is one of them, or a bench keeper while a change is left, which replaces the man in goal (he then cannot kick) and is logged. Over 4,000 shoot-outs against a plain side, three nervy men (mentality 50) then two composed ones (92) win 48.7%, the reverse order 46.4%.
- **On the screen** (r9): the kicks reveal on the report as a scoreboard (`ShootoutRows`, one row a side in kick order behind the side's code, dashed places up to five while it reveals, sudden death past them); the report's verdict reads "Penalties" and its Next button a plain Continue until the last kick has shown (`src/ui/shootoutReveal.ts`, per visit), so the scoreboard never gives the result away. The arena summary prints the last tie's shoot-out the same way. On a phone, Take the penalties stands on the dock (`--dock-h`, `--dock-safe`).
- **Tiers:** a final is `WINNERS` or `RUNNERS-UP`. Achievements: `final-whistle` (win a one-off final) and `spot-kick-kings` (win a shoot-out in the arena).
- **Results.** `recordResult` files an `ArenaRunEntry` on `profile.arenaRuns` (at most 50, by format, then score, then goal difference) and credits the arena shelf (`creditArena`: arena cups by size, a giant killed for every win on Realistic over a side with real strength 85 or more, results per mode); it returns before the leaderboard and the daily streak, and never counts a season or a league title.
- **Managing by hand.** Every manager decision while a run is under way sets `arena.handManaged`: tactics, a role, the formation (a shape change after the first match costs the −12 familiarity a mid-season change costs), a lineup change, the team talk, the captain, man-marking, a changed training focus (the Gauntlet and the Cup offer it on the hub), a live match, and a penalty order or keeper other than the default. Only assistant-managed runs verify as challenge links (below). Choosing the Gauntlet's lifeline and drafting a bonus spin by hand are not management: the link carries both and the replay repeats them.
- **Screens.** Home's casual row has the Cup tile (`arena-cup-card`) and the Gauntlet tile (`arena-gauntlet-card`). `ArenaSetup` (its own screen, so `Setup.tsx` is untouched, laid out as main's Setup: numbered steps in two boxes, 1 formation (`FormationPicker` beside the pitch), 2 name, 3 the format, size and field with More options folded, 4 Spin; one screen on a desktop): team name, the format, the opponent field (Giants, the default; Champions; The wheel), the formation, and under More options (folded, remembered per browser in `touchline-arena-advanced`) the wheel's league or European Nights, the era, difficulty, seed, hard mode, twists and the colours worn; the line above Start (`summariseArena`) states every choice, the Start bar is fixed on a phone, Start reads "Draft for the final" and asks before abandoning an unfinished run. The giants and champions fields load every league first. The Draft's finish button reads "On to the final". `Arena` (the hub, a `.page.fit` shell screen: the head, then three boxes side by side that scroll inside themselves: the opponent with the ladder or the draw and the opposition report, the team sheet or Tactics, the pitch with the talk, training, captain and Play): the format and round, the opponent's badge and name with its calibrated and real strength ("Plays at 78.0 (real 83.9)"), the opposition report (no table or form), the team sheet (squad list, pitch, Auto-pick), Tactics (the Dugout's tab), the team talk and captain, then "Manage this one" and "Assistant plays it". The match screen (main's match-day dashboard; an arena live match plays on it through `advanceArenaMatch` with `hold`, `completeArenaMatch` and `changeArenaMatch`, and opens on the replay dashboard after the assistant plays it) shows `ShootoutPanel` as the whole screen while penalties wait (a shell `.page.fit`): while penalties wait, the men on the pitch in the default order with up and down buttons, a keeper select and "Take the penalties", with the continue button disabled; then the kicks reveal one at a time (at once under `prefers-reduced-motion`). The report that follows opens at its top (on a phone Take the penalties sits under the eleven takers), keeps one sideways-scrolling line of kicks with Show them all in its head, and its scoreboard reads WON ON PENS or LOST ON PENS, never DRAW (`knockoutOutcome`, `src/ui/arenaText.ts`); a taker's shooting and composure go under his name on a phone. The Cup's bracket and Best runs draw our own badge (the team's code), never the club whose colours we wear, and a lost final's man of the match on the summary carries his club. Its continue reads "Run summary →" when the run is over. `ArenaSummary` (main's `.page.summary`: the result box on the left, the rest in `summary-body` beside it): the banner, the run with the shoot-out's kicks, the man of the match and scorers, the share text (`arenaShareText`: `TouchLine · Cup Final`, the side, its shape and wheel, the banner, `F 🟨 1-1 Manchester City 2017-18 (4-3 pens)` with 🟩 a win in 90, 🟨 level at 90 and 🟥 a defeat, the man of the match, the seed), the share card (`banner` and `lines`), "Play again" (a new seed) and "New game". The Profile page adds an Arena cups count to the cabinet row and a Best runs table.

### The Gauntlet

38-0's Last One Standing, managed: draft fourteen, then climb a ladder of fifteen real club-seasons, one match a rung, until the first defeat. The score is the rungs won.

- **Settings** (`gauntletGameSettings`): a final's, with `{ ...QUICK_RULES, squadSize: 14 }` and `arena: { format: 'gauntlet' }` (no field: the ladder comes from the wheel's own leagues and era). The wheel scope, era, hard mode and twists are chosen as for a final.
- **The ladder** (`buildLadder`, `src/engine/gauntlet.ts`), built once in `beginArena` from `streams.field` and stored as keys (`ArenaState.ladder`: 15 `mains`, then 15 `alternates`). The pool is every club-season with a squad in the wheel's leagues and era, in key order, ranked by `teamStrengthOf` (ties by key) and cut into ten percentile bands (`gauntletBands`). The mains are drawn from the top down, rung 15 to 1, one draw each, so the strongest clubs are kept for the top: rungs 11 to 15 draw uniformly from the strongest 5% less every club already on the ladder, widened five points at a time (top 10%, top 15%...) until a new club is left (`topSlice`; England's strongest 5% is 19 club-seasons of only four clubs, so rung 11 comes from the top 10%); rung r ≤ 10 draws uniformly from band r less every club already on the ladder; when an era's smaller pool has no new club left in the band, from the new clubs of the nearest bands (one below and one above, then two, and so on: `nearestFresh`, engine 7), and only when the whole pool has none, from any unused club-season of the band. A club (by club id) therefore appears once among the mains whenever the pool holds fifteen clubs: engine 6 fell back to the band at once, so every ladder of the 2014-15 to 2019-20 and 2020-21 onwards eras had a club twice (14 different clubs); engine 7 gives 15 in all three eras over 200 seeds each, with about one rung in ten drawn from a neighbouring band (`gauntlet.test.ts`). Measured on 40 assistant climbs per era on Calibrated, engine 6 then engine 7: 2020-21 onwards 5.78 then 5.55 rungs (30.0% then 25.0% reaching ten), 2014-15 to 2019-20 4.90 then 4.90 (17.5% then 15.0%), inside the Gauntlet's guards. Then one alternate per rung, rung 1 to 15, from the same band (the strongest 5% for rungs 11 to 15) less every main and earlier alternate. The player's draft never enters it, so two players on one seed climb the same ladder. Only the rung up next holds an `AiClub` in `arena.clubs`: it is put there when the previous rung is survived and deleted when it is beaten.
- **England** (the 2026-10-25 packs, 380 club-seasons): band medians 74.4 / 76.0 / 77.0 / 77.7 / 78.3 / 79.4 / 80.6 / 81.9 / 83.2 / 85.2, the strongest 5% 86.1 (Liverpool, Manchester City, Arsenal, Chelsea). Over 200 ladders no club repeats and every main sits in its band. The era pools are smaller (2020-21 onwards: 140; 2014-15 to 2019-20: 120) and every one of their ladders repeats a club through the fallback.
- **Every rung**: the venue alternates, away at rung 1 and at home at rung 2 (`gauntletHome`, the owner's choice over the plan's neutral ground), importance `min(1.4, 1 + 0.03 × (r − 1))`, the label `Rung 7` with the billing `Gauntlet night` from rung 5 or `Giant killing` against a real strength of 85+, and penalties when level at 90 (no extra time). Calibrated: the opponent plays at most `GAUNTLET_CEILING(r) = 66 + 1.5 × (r − 1)` (66 at rung 1, 72 at 5, 79.5 at 10, 87 at 15), `offset = max(0, round1(real − ceiling))`; Realistic: no ceiling.
- **Between rungs** (in `settleStep`, the moment a rung is survived): `arenaRecovery(game, u, 0.8)` (80% of a week's recovery plus the training focus), one rung off every injury, and the next rung's club put in place. A red card and every second booking of the run are one-rung bans, served by sitting the next rung out. One training focus per rung.
- **Bonus spins.** Leaving the match screen after surviving rungs 2, 4, 6 and 8, while the squad is under 18, reopens the run's own draft for one more pick (`closeArenaMatch`: the draft's rules are copied with `squadSize` one higher, because `createGame` hands `settings.rules` to the draft by reference, so `settings.rules.squadSize` stays 14). The Draft is headed "Bonus spin: N rungs climbed" (r9), its finish button reads "Back to the ladder", the match screen's continue "Bonus spin →". `finishDraft` → `beginArena` adds the new man to the bench at `newSquadPlayer` state and keeps the side the manager set. The pick draws from `rngState`, never the ladder, and the draft history stays the complete record of every spin.
- **The lifeline** (`changeTheDraw`): once a run, on the hub before a rung, the rung's main is swapped for its alternate (`arena.lifelineUsed = r`); a second use is refused. It is recorded rather than counted as managing by hand, so a replay can apply it.
- **Sim to the end** (`simArenaToEnd`): the assistant plays every rung left, penalties included, and drafts every bonus spin with `autoDraft` The hub asks first ("Let the assistant play every rung left…?", the Cup's "every tie left") and the button reads Simulating… until it is done.
- **End, tiers and achievements.** The run ends at the first defeat (a lost shoot-out included) or after fifteen rungs. Tiers: `N RUNGS` (`RUNGS` with the count, `1 RUNG` in the singular) and `GAUNTLET COMPLETE` (`arenaTierLabel`). Achievements: `gauntlet-5` (Five Rungs), `gauntlet-10` (Last One Standing), `gauntlet-15` (Gauntlet Complete), and `giant-killer` (beat a side with a real team strength of 85+ on Realistic, in any arena format).
- **Cabinet** (`creditArena`): `gauntlet.runs`, `gauntlet.best` (the most rungs, which the Ironman badge reads) and `gauntlet.complete`, plus `giantKills`, on the arena's own shelf; never a cup or a league title. The Profile page's cabinet row shows Best Gauntlet beside Arena cups, and Best runs lists the run with its rungs.
- **Calibration, measured** (England packs of 2026-10-25, engine `'4'`, an auto-drafted 4-3-3 fourteen, assistant management, bonus spins drafted by the assistant, the real shoot-out). With the venue alternating, the plan's ceiling of `64 + 1.5 × (r − 1)` (probed on neutral ground) was far too easy: 7.10 rungs on average over 300 seeds with home first. Starting away keeps Realistic under its guard of 2 (1.79 over 1,000 seeds, against 2.05 over 300 with home first), so rung 1 is away. Over the 1,000 seeds `probe-0` to `probe-999`:

  | Ceiling start (step 1.5, away first) | Mean rungs | p10 / p25 / p50 / p75 / p90 | Reach 5 | Reach 10 | Complete |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 66 (chosen) | 5.15 | 0 / 1 / 4 / 8 / 12 | 47.9% | 20.8% | 2.2% |
  | 66.5 | 4.63 | 0 / 1 / 4 / 8 / 11 | 42.6% | 15.9% | 1.5% |
  | Realistic | 1.79 | 0 / 0 / 1 / 3 / 5 | 11.1% | 1.3% | 0.1% |

  66 and 66.5 sit 0.25 and 0.27 either side of the plan's 4.9 (each mean is good to about ±0.27 at two standard errors), and 66 keeps a whole number on the setup screen. Per-rung success at 66, rungs 1 to 10: 82 / 91 / 85 / 91 / 83 / 91 / 80 / 87 / 78 / 87% (the away rungs are the odd ones), rungs 11 to 15: 66 / 79 / 49 / 74 / 56%; 14% of rungs are level at 90. Over 300 seeds, starting away: 65 gives 5.99, 67 gives 4.39. The balance guard (`src/game/__tests__/gauntlet.test.ts`, 300 assistant runs `gauntlet-guard-0` to `-299`) keeps the calibrated mean in [3.5, 6.5] (it reads 5.04) with 4% to 25% reaching ten (17.3%), and Realistic below 2; if it fails, tune the ceiling's start first, then its step.
- **Screens.** Home's Arena row has The Gauntlet card (`5 minutes · how far can you climb?`), which opens `ArenaSetup` on the Gauntlet (`openArenaSetup`); the setup's format switch shows no opponent field for it and Start reads "Draft for the Gauntlet". The hub adds the ladder strip (`GauntletLadder`: rungs climbed as club codes in green, the defeat in red, the rung up next with its badge), the band ("Rung 8 · stronger than 70% of the wheel": `gauntletBandText` in `src/ui/arenaText.ts` rewords the engine's `rungBandLabel` "top 30% of the wheel" for rungs 2 to 10; rung 1 reads "the weakest 10%", 11 to 15 "the strongest 5%"), the venue and billing, the calibrated rating, the lifeline (Change the draw, with a confirm), and Sim to the end beside Manage this one and Assistant plays it; the squad list carries condition and sharpness bars and ban chips. The summary shows the ladder, every rung with its badge (the defeat in red), the run's scorers with their man-of-the-match awards, the share text and the card (banner `7 RUNGS`, the strip and the lines below as its lines). Share text (`arenaShareText` with `strip` and `lines`):

  ```
  TouchLine · The Gauntlet
  Touchline XI · 4-3-3 · Premier League wheel
  🪜 7 RUNGS
  🟩🟩🟩🟨🟩🟩🟩🟥
  Fell to Real Madrid 2017-18 at rung 8, 1-2
  ⚽ Haaland 6 · ⭐ De Bruyne
  Seed K7Q2-MX9A
  ```
